# Knowlu C3 — sync — Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Status: WRITTEN 2026-09-14; reviewed and amended (fix rounds 1 and 2) 2026-09-15; not started.** Execute on a branch `c3-sync` in a worktree of this repository (`knowlu`), merged **after** C2. The review is `docs/reports/2026-09-14-c3-sync-plan-review.md`, and its *Fix round 1 — resolutions* section is the line-by-line record of what changed. **Five** things moved in design rather than in wording, and a reader who knows the first draft should read them first: the index hashes are **keyed** (`HMAC` under an HKDF-derived `K_index`, not bare SHA-256); a restore takes a **seed allowlist** instead of demanding an empty vault; `/sync-pull` reads **behind a ten-second lag** so two devices cannot strand a row between them; the switch's off-then-on path **re-uploads under the new key** with a `reset` the server honours; and the account records **which key generation its copy is sealed under**, so a device left behind by another machine's re-key is told and offered the fix instead of failing quietly for ever. Written against `docs/specs/2026-09-09-knowlu-cloud-design.md` (**SIGNED** 2026-09-09) — §1 D1, D4, D7, D9; §3.1; §4.1; §4.4; §5.1; **§5.5 (the section this plan implements)**; §5.6; §6; §7.3; §8; §9; §10; §11 R4; §11a; §12's C3 row; §13 — and against Quinn's calls of 2026-09-14 recorded in `HANDOFF.md` §4: **restore from a backup becomes a link on the picker, built in C3**, and **export gets a settings row in C3**.

**Goal:** The journal stops being a thing that exists only on one laptop. Every note write this device makes is uploaded — encrypted on the device, with a key our servers never hold — to `POST /sync-push`; everything another device of the same account wrote comes back down `GET /sync-pull` and is applied through `write`, so a second device is journalled exactly like the first and a genuine two-sided conflict surfaces as an amend card instead of a silent merge. A student whose laptop is gone signs in on a new one, pastes their recovery code on the picker, and gets their vault back. Git leaves the product entirely: `engine/src/history.rs` is deleted, the app stops spawning `git`, and the local snapshot mirror stays exactly as it is.

**Architecture:** One encrypted blob store, two endpoints, one engine module. On the **device**, `engine/src/sync.rs` seals each new journal record and each changed note with **AES-256-GCM under a 32-byte key that lives only in Windows Credential Manager** (`knowlu/<profile_id>/sync-key`, written by the app, read by the engine, never sent anywhere), posts them to `/sync-push`, pulls what it has not seen from `/sync-pull`, appends foreign journal records to the local ledger **verbatim** — they carry their own `ts`, `device` and `seq`, and rewriting those would destroy the attribution judge-once reads — and settles the note-level effect through the existing pure `reconcile::resolve`, writing the winners through `write` and filing an amend card for every field both sides moved. On the **server**, two thin Deno handlers over two Postgres tables that hold ciphertext, an opaque per-device token, a content hash and nothing else: no note text, no field name, no title, no path. The cursor is a per-account identity column; a retried push is idempotent on the content hash. `knowlu-engine sync` is one more slot step with the same discipline as `judge` — **it always exits 0** — and it runs **first**, so the day ranks on what came down. The key's presence in Credential Manager *is* the on-switch, which is why there is no settings field to keep in step with it.

**Tech Stack:** Supabase (Postgres 15 + Edge Functions on Deno) for the two endpoints, reusing C1's `_shared/{auth,db,entitlement,http}.ts` and C2's `CloudClient` — **no second HTTP client anywhere**; Rust 1.98 `stable-x86_64-pc-windows-gnu` with `ring` 0.17 (AES-256-GCM, SHA-256, `SystemRandom` — already in this workspace's `Cargo.lock` through `rustls`, so this adds a direct edge and no new crate) and `base64` 0.22 (likewise already resolved); `deno test` for every pure module and one request/response contract test per function; `std::net::TcpListener` on `127.0.0.1:0` for every Rust test that exercises a client.

**Spec:** `docs/specs/2026-09-09-knowlu-cloud-design.md`. Read **§5.5** before Task 1, then §3.1, §4.1, §4.4, §5.1, §6, §9 and §11 R4. The parent design for the vault, the journal and `write` is `docs/specs/2026-08-11-personal-ops-system-design.md` (§4 the vault schema; the journal section). The two sibling plans are `docs/plans/2026-09-09-c1-accounts-plan.md` and `docs/plans/2026-09-09-c2-judge-plan.md`; their review reports carry the rulings this plan inherits (`docs/reports/2026-09-09-c1-accounts-plan-review.md`, `docs/reports/2026-09-09-c2-judge-plan-review.md`).

## Global Constraints

Every task's requirements implicitly include this section.

- **Add no single-user assumptions.** Nothing in `cloud/`, `engine/`, `app/` or a fixture names a person, a vault, a machine, an account or a credential. Anything that would need hand-editing for a second user is a bug. (`CLAUDE.md`, rule 1.)
- **Never regenerate a frozen reference.** The eight Python-written references in `engine/tests/fixtures/` are read-only oracles. The three Rust-generated surface references (`surface-today-{s1,s1-migrated,full}.json`) may be regenerated only in a commit whose diff shows the change and whose message says why (console spec §4.6) — **no task here regenerates one**, and `git status --porcelain --untracked-files=all engine/tests/fixtures/` is **empty at every task boundary**: no task in this plan adds, edits or removes a file under `engine/tests/fixtures/`. The one new committed vector this plan produces (Task 3's `sync_vectors.json`) lives under `cloud/`, read from Rust by relative path, for the same reason C2 reads the two parsed references the other way round: one file, two readers, no chance of two copies drifting apart.
- `engine/tests/oracle.rs` and `engine/tests/surface_oracle.rs` must pass **unchanged** at every task boundary. Sync writes nothing on a vault with no sync key, so the three fixture vaults' `today.md` cannot move.
- **Every note write goes through `write` — journal record first, single-line frontmatter surgery second.** `src/yamlemit.rs` is the crate's one YAML emitter. **No note is ever parsed and re-dumped.** There is exactly one recorded exception in this plan, argued in the fidelity ledger and again at Task 9: **restore materialises bytes that were already journalled on the device that made them**, so it writes note files with `pystr::write_text` and journal lines with `JsonlLedger::append`, and it runs only into a vault whose `tasks/` holds no `.md` at all.
- **`journal::VIAS`, `journal::OPS`, run records, ledgers and note frontmatter are contracts with existing vaults: byte-identical, never renamed.** This plan adds no `via`, no `op`, no frontmatter key and no run-record field. The one new file it puts in a vault is `state/sync-cursor.json`, which is generated, device-local and never synced.
- **All JSON this crate writes goes through `ledger::dumps_value`** (Python `json.dumps` separators), never `serde_json::to_string`. The canonical bytes a record is hashed over are `dumps_value`'s, which is what makes a hash computed on two machines the same hash.
- **The vault stays plain text on the student's machine, and the cloud copy is the durable one — never the authority for a running device.** A pull that fails, a 402, a 500, an unreadable key: every one is a named line and exit 0, and the day still ranks from the folder on disk. Nothing in this plan ever deletes a local note because the cloud does not have it.
- **`rank` never calls a model, and no path under `cli.rs` may reach `/judge-*`** (Knowlu spec decision 11, `CLAUDE.md`). `sync` is transport and carries no judgment; `rank_cannot_reach_a_judgment_endpoint` (C2) must still pass.
- **`knowlu-engine sync` always exits 0.** No `config/cloud.yaml`, no sync key, no entitlement, no network, a 402, a 429, a 5xx, a timeout — all normal outcomes reported on stdout as named lines. A non-zero exit sets `RunSummary.engine_ok = false` in the app's scheduler, which paints the tray amber and puts the slot into retry backoff twice a day forever.
- **No test reaches the network.** A `TcpListener` (Rust) or `Deno.serve` (TypeScript) bound to `127.0.0.1:0` inside one test, answering that same test's own request, is not egress: no DNS, no route off the machine, no listener on a routable interface. Every such test binds `127.0.0.1`, never `0.0.0.0`, and joins its listener thread (or `await`s its server's `shutdown()`) before returning.
- **Two `deno test` command lines, and which is which.** A run over **C3's own files alone** is `deno test --allow-read --config cloud/supabase/deno.json <path>` — nothing C3 writes needs more. A run over **the whole tree** is C2's merged command and must be copied exactly, because C3 merges after C2 and a narrower flag set fails on C2's tests rather than on C3's:

  ```
  deno test --allow-read --allow-net=127.0.0.1 --allow-env=ANTHROPIC_WEBHOOK_SIGNING_KEY,ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN,ANTHROPIC_LOG,ANTHROPIC_CUSTOM_HEADERS --config cloud/supabase/deno.json cloud/supabase/
  ```

- **A third-party import in an edge function is an `npm:` or `jsr:` specifier on the import line**, never a bare name resolved through the root `deno.json` import map: the `--use-api` bundler does not read that file. C3 imports nothing third-party, so this costs it nothing and is stated so the next person adding one does not learn it at deploy time.
- **No engine command is ever run against the worktree root or a real vault** (ruling R-3a-12). No `--vault .`, no path inside the worktree, and never `C:\Users\danie\GitHub\quinn-ops`. Tests copy a fixture vault into a temp directory.
- **Tests that touch the real Credential Manager are serialised.** Windows races parallel `CredWriteW`/`CredReadW` calls (spurious `ERROR_NOT_FOUND`). `app/tests/account.rs` holds a file-scoped `CREDMAN_LOCK`; **any new test file in this plan that writes, reads or deletes a real credential carries its own file-scoped lock**, a generated test id, and a `Drop` guard that deletes what it wrote (`CLAUDE.md`).
- **TDD, per task:** the failing test first, run it and see it fail with the message the step names, the minimal implementation, run it and see it pass, commit.
- `cargo build --workspace` and `cargo test --workspace` from the root at **0 warnings**. The one accepted line is the app's `.rsrc merge failure: multiple non-default manifests` linker message. `cargo test --release` will not link — test in the dev profile.
- **Line endings: LF everywhere** (`.gitattributes`: `* text=auto eol=lf`; `*.ps1` CRLF; `engine/tests/fixtures/** -text`). New `.ts`, `.sql`, `.rs`, `.html`, `.css`, `.js` and `.md` files are LF. `git diff --stat` never shows a whole-file flip. Vault files the engine writes go through `pystr::write_text`, which is what every other vault file already gets.
- **No secret in the repo, a log, a fixture, a test name, a migration, a commit message or this plan.** The sync key is generated on the device and never leaves it; no server-side secret is added by this stream. The one key-shaped literal this plan commits is a **test vector** and is named `SYNC_TEST_KEY_NOT_A_SECRET` so no reader has to wonder.
- **Migrations are applied to the STAGING project only** (§11 R6). Nothing in this plan touches the production project; Task 14 records what production still needs.
- **Migrations are stamped `cloud/supabase/migrations/20260912……_<name>.sql`.** C1 owns 2026-09-10, C2 owns 2026-09-11, C3 owns **2026-09-12**. The three streams never collide, and C3 carries its own `migrations_sync_test.ts` filtered to its own day rather than editing C1's `migrations_test.ts` (R-X-8's rule, applied from C3's side).
- **Commits:** specific `git add` (never `git add -A`), the message through a file (`git commit -F <file>`), and both trailers. The two literals below are **the writing session's**; the executing session substitutes its own model name and session URL, exactly as `HANDOFF.md` §5 says (ruling R-C2-6 — this is not a defect to fix, it is a template to fill):

```
Co-Authored-By: Claude Fable 5.1 <noreply@anthropic.com>
Claude-Session: https://claude.ai/code/session_018EXZqBCHaJBKtYtkNfjj1Z
```

- Never bare `git stash` / `git stash pop` (ruling R-3a-29): the stash stack is shared with the main checkout and every worktree.
- **File ownership.** C1 and C2 are merged before this stream starts, so their exclusive-ownership rows retire with them. **C3 owns:** `cloud/supabase/functions/{sync-push,sync-pull}/**`, `cloud/supabase/functions/_shared/sync_*.ts`, `cloud/supabase/migrations/20260912*.sql`, `cloud/supabase/migrations_sync_test.ts`, `engine/src/sync.rs` (new) and the deletion of `engine/src/history.rs`, `engine/tests/**`, `app/src/sync.rs` (new), `app/tests/**`, `app/static/**`, `site/privacy.html`, and this plan file. **C0 still owns** `.github/**`, `scripts/ci/**`, `scripts/release.ps1`, `scripts/sign.ps1`, `engine/tests/workflows.rs` and the three `app/tauri.conf.json` updater keys — C3 touches none of them except through hand-off **H9**. Everything else named under *Controller hand-offs* is applied by the controller **on the branch, at the task that needs it** (see that section's preamble). `site/index.html` is C1's and is hand-off **H12**; `engine/tests/site.rs` is inside C3's own `engine/tests/**`. **A task that silently edits a hand-off file is a plan defect** — stop and report it instead of editing.

## Quinn-owned preconditions (asked one at a time, when a task reaches them, with the context)

| # | Needed by | What, and what breaks without it |
|---|---|---|
| **P1** | Task 2 | **Where the sync key comes from.** §11 R4 already ruled *yes* to client-side encryption of note bodies with an account-derived key (2026-09-09, at its default), so **whether** to encrypt is settled. **How the key is derived is not**, and it decides one thing only: what happens to the cloud copy when a student forgets their password or loses their laptop. Three answers, and their costs: **(a) a device-generated 32-byte key plus a printed recovery code** — the key is made on the device the first time sync is switched on, kept in Credential Manager, never sent; the student is shown a 52-character recovery code once and must tick *I have saved this* before the switch turns on; restoring on a new machine asks for that code. Cost: a student who loses both the machine and the code loses the cloud copy — the local `Backups\` mirror is then the only copy, which is why it stays. **(b) derived from the account password** (Argon2/PBKDF2 at each sign-in). Cost: Supabase Auth offers a password reset by email, and a reset would silently destroy every stored note body with nothing anywhere saying so; we would have to disable password reset or accept that failure. **(c) a key the server holds.** Cost: it is not client-side encryption in any sense a reader would accept, and the published privacy policy's sentence would have to say *we hold your notes, encrypted*, which is a materially different promise. **Recommendation: (a).** It is the only one of the three where the sentence "our servers cannot read your notes" is true without qualification, the recovery code has a natural home (P2's screen), and it needs no new server-side secret. **Without a ruling:** Task 2 is written behind `sync::load_key`, so every later task still builds and tests against (a); reversing to (b) or (c) changes that one function and its two callers, and nothing else in this plan. |
| **P2** | Task 10 | **The switch, its screen, and the privacy policy's own promise.** `site/privacy.html` line 54, published, says: *"**No note bodies.** The text of your tasks and notes stays in the folder on your machine. If that ever changes it will be a separate switch, off by default, with its own screen explaining what and why — and no such switch exists today."* C3 is that change, and the policy has already pre-committed its design. So this plan ships sync **off by default, behind one switch, with its own screen** — and **four** pieces of published copy must be re-read and re-published before it ships: (1) `site/privacy.html`'s §"What we do not collect" bullet, (2) the same sentence at `site/privacy.html:13`, (3) **the same sentence again at `site/index.html:16`** — hand-off H12, and the copy the first review found missing — and (4) its pinned twin `PRIVACY` in `app/static/console.js:1371`. **Three tests hold those four in step and all four move together**: `app/tests/static_assets.rs::the_wizards_privacy_sentence_is_the_sites_privacy_sentence` (reads the sentence out of `site/privacy.html` and looks for exactly it in `console.js`), `engine/tests/site.rs::the_site_is_plain_html_and_carries_the_privacy_sentence_on_both_pages` (whose `PRIVACY` const at `engine/tests/site.rs:15` is the sentence, spelled out), and Task 13's own new case. Two of the screen's sentences — *"We store it; we cannot read it"* and *"a key we never receive and cannot reconstruct"* — are **only true once Task 2's index hashes are keyed** (the `K_index` HMAC), which is why that is a design change in this plan and not a refinement. Task 10 writes the screen's words and Task 13 writes the policy's; **Quinn reads both, and they go to the lawyer with the C1 packet's P5 list** (`docs/reports/2026-09-10-c1-quinn-packets.md`). **Without it:** every task still runs and the switch still works, but the branch must not merge — shipping a feature the live policy says does not exist is the one thing in this stream that is not fixable afterwards. |
| **P3** | Task 1 | **The per-account storage ceiling and how long a cloud copy is kept.** Two numbers, and they are product decisions, not engineering ones. **Recommended: 200 MB of ciphertext per account, and journal records kept for 400 days** (note text is kept as long as the note exists — it is current state, not history). A typical vault after a year is a few megabytes; 200 MB is ~50× a heavy user and is a runaway guard, not a quota anyone should meet. 400 days is thirteen months, so a student who returns after a summer still finds last year's history. **The retention number is not only about storage, and that is the half that needs Quinn:** `journal::human_set` (`engine/src/journal.rs:271`) is what **judge-once** reads, and it reads *records*. A student who restores onto a new machine after a long absence gets every note but only the last thirteen months of history — so an agent may re-set a field they decided fourteen months ago, which is exactly the failure the invariant exists to prevent, arriving quietly. Two ways to answer it: **keep 400 days for everything** (simple; the loss is bounded to attribution older than a year and only on a *restored* machine — the original machine's own journal is never pruned), or **prune only agent-authored and `supersede` records and keep every `op: set` with a human actor for ever** (one `where` clause on `sync_prune`, and judge-once survives a restore intact at a storage cost that is a rounding error, because human sets are the rare records). **Recommendation: the second.** It costs almost nothing and removes a silent correctness loss from the one path the feature exists for. **Without a ruling:** Task 1 ships 200 MiB and 400 days **with the second shape**, and changing either number or dropping the `where` clause is one migration row. |
| **P4** | Task 12 | **The one-line go to delete `engine/src/history.rs`.** It is 707 lines with a full test suite, it is the last of plan 2's sync work, and §4.4 says plainly that the app stops doing git. This is not a reversal — it is the removal the spec ordered — but it is irreversible in the sense that re-adding it would be a rewrite, so it is said out loud once. **Without it:** Tasks 1–11 and 13 all land; Task 12 stops, `history.rs` stays compiled and unreferenced, and the exit gate records the gap. |
| **P5** | Task 10 | **Off, then on again: what happens to the copy already in the account?** Turning the switch off deletes this machine's key; turning it on again cannot recover it, so every row already in `sync_records`/`sync_notes` becomes permanently unopenable while still counting against the ceiling. Two answers. **(a) Turning it back on starts a fresh copy and discards the old one:** the device notices the key's fingerprint has changed, sends `reset: true` with its next push, the server deletes every row for the account, and the device re-uploads the whole vault. Cost: one full re-upload — a few megabytes — and the old copy is gone, which it effectively was already, because nobody holds its key. **(b) Turning it off keeps the key on the machine and only stops the copying**, so turning it on again resumes the same copy. Cost: "off" no longer means the secret has left this machine, which is a weaker promise than the screen can otherwise make, and a student who turned it off *because* they are handing the laptop on is not served. **Recommendation: (a)**, with the screen saying it in one sentence. **The plan is built to (a)**: Task 4 gains a `reset` flag, Task 6 stores the key's fingerprint in the cursor and re-uploads from zero when it changes, Task 10's screen carries the sentence. Reversing to (b) removes the `reset` flag and makes `turn_off` keep the credential; it touches no other task.
| | | **And the question has a second half, which (a) forces and which the plan answers rather than asking:** what happens to the account's **other** devices. A re-key replaces the whole copy, so every other machine is holding a key the copy is no longer sealed with — its own vault is fine and its own key is fine, but it can no longer read or add to the shared copy. Ignoring that would mean a second laptop pulling rows it cannot decrypt every slot for ever and pushing rows nobody can open. The plan therefore records the generation on the account (`public.sync_generation`), refuses a push or pull under a stale one with a 409, and turns that on the device into a named non-failing skip plus one Good-to-know item pointing at *Settings → Cloud copy → use a code from another machine*. **It is not a separate decision** — (b) removes the re-key and so removes this too — but it is the cost of (a) and it is stated here so (a) is chosen with it. |
| **P6** | Task 11 | **Which restore did the 2026-09-14 call mean?** `HANDOFF.md` §4's row — *"Restore from a backup: a link on the picker, built in C3"* — is written about the **local `Backups` mirror** and the caller-less `onboarding::restore_vault`; §5.5's *"'Restore from cloud' is a wizard entry point"* is about the **cloud copy**. They answer different questions: the mirror is for *this machine is fine and I lost a file yesterday*, the cloud copy is for *this machine is gone*. **Recommendation: both links, on the same picker** — `restore_vault` / `restore_vault_in` already exist, already copy the mirror out to a new folder rather than adopting it in place, and are about fifteen lines of `console.js` plus a dialog away from having a caller; and a student who has a mirror but no recovery code is otherwise told nothing. **The plan is built to both**: Task 11's main route is the cloud link and its step 5 wires the local one beside it. If Quinn wants only the cloud link, delete that step and add a *What is NOT in this plan* line saying `restore_vault` is still caller-less. |
| **P7** | Task 5 | **`/sync-pull` returns this device's own rows and the device filters them by content hash.** For a single-device account — every account at launch — that is one wasted page per slot immediately after a push. **(a) Accept it** (recommended): the hash filter has to exist anyway, because it is also what makes a re-install on the same machine idempotent, and a wasted page is a few hundred kilobytes twice a day. **(b) Add `device=neq.<token>` on the server** and keep the hash filter for the re-install case: two lines in `sync_db.ts`, at the cost of the server learning which device is asking, which it has no other reason to know. **Recommendation: (a)**; Task 14 records (b) as the first lever if the pull ever costs anything. **The plan is built to (a).** |

Nothing in this stream needs a new Supabase project secret, a new Stripe object, a new Google scope or a new DNS record.

## Fidelity ledger

One row per spec decision or sentence C3 carries or narrows, with the narrowing on the record and its reason.

| # | Decision / sentence | Source | Carried by |
|---|---|---|---|
| §5.5 Up | "The client uploads new journal records (and the note text they produced) to `/sync/push`; the server stores them per account in Storage (encrypted at rest…)" | cloud design §5.5 | Tasks 1, 4, 6. **Narrowed, on the record: Postgres, not Supabase Storage.** A vault's whole corpus is markdown and JSON — 500 notes is about a megabyte — and every row this endpoint writes is already an opaque base64 blob under a 64 KiB cap. Postgres gives it row-level security, one access path, a cursor that is just an identity column, and a `DELETE /account` purge that is one more table name in a list C1 already maintains. Storage would add a bucket, a second authorisation surface, a second retention mechanism and object names that are themselves metadata. The §3 diagram's "Storage: journal snapshots (encrypted)" is a sketch of where the bytes live, and this is where they live. **The endpoints are `/sync-push` and `/sync-pull`, hyphenated**, not the spec's slashed `/sync/push` and `/sync/pull`: Supabase routes `/functions/v1/<function-name>` and a slash in a function name is a different function, which is the same rename C2's review ruled for `/judge-task` (its Critical 1). The rename is recorded here and asserted by exit-gate item 1. |
| §5.5 Up | "encrypted at rest; **client-side encryption with an account-derived key is recommended for note bodies — confirm**" | cloud design §5.5, ruled §11 R4 "yes (default)" | Tasks 2, 4, 5. **Carried, and widened: everything is client-side encrypted, not only note bodies.** A journal `set` record carries `old` and `new` for a field that may be `title` or `decision_note`, so encrypting note bodies and leaving records in clear would leak note text by the other door. What travels in clear is exactly what the server needs to order, deduplicate and bill: an opaque 16-hex device token, a 64-hex content hash, a 12-byte IV, the ciphertext's length, and a receive timestamp. **And every one of those three opaque values is KEYED, not a bare digest** — `note_ref`, `record_hash` and `device_token` are `HMAC-SHA256(K_index, …)` where `K_index = HKDF-Expand(sync key, "knowlu/index")`. A plain `SHA-256` of a guessable string *is* the string to anyone holding the table: a vault path is `tasks/<slugified title>.md` over a known alphabet, and a journal record's shape is public (`{actor, device, field, id, new, old, op, path, seq, ts, via}` in `dumps_value`'s sorted form) with `op` one of six, `via` one of five, `field` a small closed set and `ts` bounded by `received_at` — a few million offline guesses recovers most of a task list. The HMAC costs nothing (same column types, same 64-hex shape, same `sync_records_once` idempotence, same cursor) and it is what makes the switch's own sentence — *"We store it; we cannot read it"* — true rather than nearly true. **The remaining "confirm" is P1** — where the key comes from — because §11 R4's phrase *account-derived* does not by itself say what happens at a password reset. |
| §5.5 Down | "the service's own writes (judgment fields, Gmail-derived notes, event verdicts, rule proposals) are queued as journal-shaped records the client pulls at `/sync/pull` and applies through `write`… with `actor: agent:knowlu.<kind>` and judge-once intact" | cloud design §5.5 | **Narrowed, on the record, and this is the largest narrowing in the plan.** All four kinds are **already delivered by pull, inside a slot step, by C2** — `/judge-task` per item inside `judge`, `/ingest-coursework` inside `coursework`, `/judge-event` inside `rank`'s roster pass, and the Gmail and promoted-rule queues inside `judge`. Every one of those replies is written through `write` under `agent:knowlu.<kind>` with judge-once intact, which is exactly the property this sentence asks for. **C3 does not re-route them**, for two reasons. The first is that doing so would re-open every C2 test for no behaviour change. The second is structural and decides it: after this plan the down channel is **encrypted with a key the server does not have**, so a server-originated row could not be sealed and would have to travel in clear — which would make the encryption promise conditional and impossible to explain on P2's screen. **What C3's `/sync-pull` carries is therefore exactly one thing: records another device of the same account pushed.** C2's own replies are journalled on the device that asked for them and then pushed like any other record, so a second device receives them through the same encrypted channel as everything else — which is the property the sentence was written for. Recorded again in *What is NOT in this plan*. |
| §5.5 Second device | "replaying the journal onto an empty vault reconstructs it — the migration script already proved the shape" | cloud design §5.5 | Task 9. **Carried, with one recorded exception to `CLAUDE.md`'s "every note write goes through `write`".** A restore writes note files with `pystr::write_text` and journal lines with `JsonlLedger::append`, because every one of those records **already exists** and carries the `ts`, the `device` and the `seq` of the machine that made it. Putting them through `write` would fabricate a second `create` record, stamped with *this* device's name and *today's* `ts`, for a note created three months ago on another laptop — and `journal::human_set`, which is what judge-once reads, would then answer with the restore instead of with the student's own decision. The invariant exists to make attribution complete; re-journalling a restore is the one way to break it while appearing to obey it. **Bounded, and the bound is stated exactly as the code enforces it:** `restore` refuses a `dest` that holds **any note in any of `ids::NOTE_FOLDERS`** (`tasks, approvals, archive, courses, issues, info`) **other than the ones its caller explicitly tolerates** — and the only caller passes the scaffold's own seeds, which are the three or more notes `scaffold::seed_writes` has just written into a vault seconds old (`archive/_migrated.md`, `tasks/get-to-know-knowlu.md` and one `courses/<slug>.md` per seeded course). A restored note at a seed's path overwrites it; a seed with no twin in the account stays. Without that allowlist the only caller could never succeed, which is the defect the first review found. |
| §5.5 Second device | "replaying the journal onto an empty vault reconstructs it" — and therefore **every device of an account must be able to open the account's copy** | cloud design §5.5; VISION ("conflicts surface as proposals, never silent merges") | **Tasks 1, 4, 5, 8 and 10 — the key generation.** A copy is only a copy to a device that can open it, and a device can lose that without knowing: another machine turns the switch off and on again, re-uploads the whole vault under a fresh key, and every other device is left holding a key the copy is no longer sealed with. Left alone it would pull rows it cannot decrypt for ever and push rows nobody can ever open. So the account carries the **fingerprint of the generation its copy is sealed under** (`public.sync_generation`; a fingerprint, not a key, and the same non-secret eight hex characters Credential Manager already stores as that credential's `UserName`), both endpoints compare it, and a device whose key is not the account's gets a **409 that stores and reads nothing**. On the device that is a **named, non-failing skip** — `sync (skipped: this machine's key is not the account's)`, exit 0, no retry backoff, because retrying fixes nothing — **plus one `info/` item** in the Good-to-know deck saying what happened and what to do, opened once by its `close_key` and closed by the first sync that succeeds. VISION's rule is that nothing is silent: this is neither a silent push nor a silent pull nor a silent failure. The action it points at is `set_sync_key`, which **adopts** the account's generation (writes the key and resets the cursor) rather than **claiming** a new one, because turning the switch off and on would start the copy again from the wrong machine. |
| §5.5 Second device | "Conflicts (two devices editing one field offline) surface as amend cards, never silent merges (VISION)" | cloud design §5.5; VISION | Task 7. Carried through the existing pure `reconcile::resolve`: a field whose resolution produced a `supersede` record is a field both sides moved, and that is the definition of a conflict this plan uses. **Narrowed where it must be:** `approvals::validate_amendment` only ever applies a card whose target is under `AMENDABLE_FOLDERS` (`tasks`, `courses`) and whose field is one of `AMENDABLE_FIELDS` (nine), with non-null scalars on both sides. A conflict outside that set cannot become a card that would ever apply, so it takes reconcile's own rule (later `ts` wins), keeps its `supersede` record for the loser, and emits **one named warning line** that says which note and which field. Nothing is ever silently dropped; the losing value is in the journal either way. |
| §5.5 Backups | "the local snapshot tick stays; the cloud copy is the durable one. 'Restore from cloud' is a wizard entry point" | cloud design §5.5 | Tasks 9 and 11, and the backup mirror is untouched. **`engine/src/backup.rs` is not modified by any task in this plan**, which is the whole of "the local snapshot tick stays". The restore entry point is the picker's, per Quinn 2026-09-14 (`HANDOFF.md` §4): a link beside *Use an existing vault…*, which asks for the recovery code and then runs the ordinary wizard with `restore: true`. |
| `HANDOFF.md` §4 | "**Restore from a backup: a link on the picker, built in C3**" (Quinn, 2026-09-14) | `HANDOFF.md` §4's row, and the caller-less `onboarding::restore_vault` it names | **Two different restores, and the row is about the other one — on the record, and P6 is Quinn's to settle.** §5.5's restore is the **cloud copy**; `HANDOFF.md`'s row is the **local `Backups` mirror** (`onboarding::restore_vault` / `restore_vault_in`, registered in the wizard's handler list and with no caller in `console.js`). The plan quotes the row in its own opening line and builds §5.5's, so without P6 it would be recording the other one as done. **Built to the recommendation — both links** — in Task 11 step 5, which is the smaller half: `restore_vault_in` already copies the mirror out to a new folder rather than adopting it in place, so the work is a dialog and a call. |
| §5.5 Backups + §9 | "the cloud copy is the durable one" versus judge-once's need for the whole journal | cloud design §5.5; `CLAUDE.md`; `journal::human_set` | **P3's second half, on the record.** `sync_prune` deletes journal records past the retention window, and `journal::human_set` — which is what judge-once reads — reads *records*. On a **restored** machine a pruned record is a decision the student made and the device can no longer see. The plan is built to P3's recommendation (prune agent-authored and `supersede` records only; keep every human `op: set` for ever), so the loss is bounded to noise; the number and the shape are both Quinn's. |
| §12 C3 | "`history.rs`'s git removed" | cloud design §12's C3 row; §4.4 | Task 12. `engine/src/history.rs` is deleted whole; `state::run_sync`, `state::refresh_history`, `state::refresh_head`, `ConsoleState::{history, head_sha, auto_sync}` and the page's repo/remote/ahead/behind copy go with it. **`runs::git_sha` stays**: it fills the `sha` field of a run record, that field is a contract with existing vaults, and on a vault that is not a repository — which is every vault the app creates (§4.1) — it already answers `None`. Its only app-side caller (`state::refresh_head`) goes, so no `git` process is spawned by the app any more. |
| §4.1 | "The vault is **not** a git repository and the app never assumes git exists" | cloud design §4.1 | Task 12, and the static test `no_git_process_is_spawned_for_a_vault` that keeps it true. |
| C2 ledger | "the vault copy of `ics_url` … **who removes it and when:** C3, together with the journal sync that gives the device somewhere else to get the URL from" | C2 plan, fidelity ledger row "C1 §3"; ruling R-X-16 | Task 13. **Carried, and the stated reason is corrected on the record.** Journal sync gives the device nowhere else to get the URL from — `/ingest-ics` does, and it has since C2. The real reason to remove the vault copy now is that it is a **second writer of a capability credential**: `config/ingest.yaml` holds, in plain text the student can read, edit and paste into a support ticket, a URL that reads their whole timetable (§9, Alabama SPII), and it silently diverges from the account's copy the moment they re-generate the secret address. Removing it makes the account the single writer. The cost — an offline `ingest` — is paid in the same task: `ingest` learns to exit **0** with `ingest: the calendar service could not be reached` on a vault that has an account, keeping its existing exit 1 for a vault that has neither an account nor a URL. |
| §11a | "the personal calendar … C1 delivers it by its secret iCal address … written to the vault's `calendars:`" | cloud design §11a | Task 13, the same removal and the same argument: `- name: personal` / `ics_url:` in `config/ingest.yaml` becomes `- name: personal` / `ics_url: 'cloud:personal'`, which C2's hand-off H4 already routes to `/ingest-calendar?name=personal`. The vault stops holding the address; the account keeps it. |
| §6 (b) | Corrections are derived from the journal on the device and sent to `POST /telemetry`; **no note body** | cloud design §6 | **Unchanged, and deliberately separate.** `app/src/telemetry.rs` keeps its own watermark, its own endpoint and its own rules; sync does not feed it and it does not feed sync. The two read the same ledger for different purposes, and merging them would put a pseudonymous analytics row and an end-to-end-encrypted backup under one consent. Named here so the separation is a decision. |
| §9 | Alabama § 8-38: session tokens and OAuth tokens are SPII, encrypted at rest, 45-day notice | cloud design §9 | Tasks 1 and 4. The sync tables hold no token and no plaintext; the only new device-side secret is the sync key, which lives in Credential Manager beside the session and is never logged, never in a `Debug`, never in an error string. `sync::SyncKey` has a hand-written `Debug` for the reason `wincred::Secret` and `account::Session` have theirs. |
| §9 | "Export / access / delete implemented for everyone (`DELETE /account`, `GET /account/export`)" | cloud design §9 | Tasks 1 and 10. The two new tables join C1's purge list (hand-off **H5**), and the settings *Export my data* row Quinn asked for on 2026-09-14 calls C1's existing `GET /account/export` and writes the file the student picks — replacing "ask at support@". |
| §10 | VISION: "**Sync replays the journal** to the account's own storage in our cloud. Accounts over device pairing. Conflicts surface as proposals, never silent merges." | cloud design §10 | The whole plan; Task 7 is the last clause. |
| §10 | VISION: "The source of truth is plain text on the user's machine. The cloud judges, syncs and bills; it never owns the vault." | cloud design §10 | Global Constraints, and the exit gate's item 3: a device with no network, no key or no entitlement ranks the day from the folder on disk and says why in one line. |
| `CLAUDE.md` | Approvals are capped at 15 new proposals a day; overflow is snoozed, never deleted | `CLAUDE.md`; `approvals::defer_over_budget` | Task 7 files conflict cards through the ordinary `propose_amendment` path, so the existing cap applies unchanged and a hundred-conflict first pull spreads over days rather than burying the deck. |
| `CLAUDE.md` | `journal::VIAS` does not grow | `CLAUDE.md` | The slot's sync step runs `--via local-runner`; the console's *Sync now* runs `via: "dashboard"`. Both are already in `VIAS`. |
| §8 | `dependency_boundary.rs` pins the engine's dependency budget; TLS is rustls/ring, never OpenSSL | cloud design §8 | Task 2 adds one test, `the_crypto_dependencies_are_the_two_already_in_the_graph`, naming `ring` and `base64` and the reason each is not a new crate to audit. |
| §5.6 | Judgment logs never enter the vault | cloud design §5.6; `CLAUDE.md` | Unchanged: `sync` writes no judgment log, and `state/sync-cursor.json` is a cursor, not a log — it holds two integers, one timestamp and a map of note path → content hash, all of which are already in the vault in plainer form. |
| §13 | "the console's visual redesign (parked)" | cloud design §13 | Task 12 rewrites exactly one line of the page — the sync line — because the facts behind it no longer exist. Nothing else in the console's appearance is touched. |

---

## File structure

### What this plan does *not* move, and why — read this before Task 1

Three things a reader will look for and not find, each with its argument in the fidelity ledger:

1. **Server-originated writes do not come down `/sync-pull`.** C2 already delivers all four kinds by pull inside its own slot steps, and a server-originated row could not be encrypted with a key the server does not have. `/sync-pull` carries other devices' records and nothing else.
2. **Supabase Storage is not used.** Two Postgres tables of ciphertext, under RLS, purged by the account function C1 already owns.
3. **`engine/src/backup.rs` is not touched.** The local mirror and its thirty snapshots are the answer to "I lost the file five minutes ago"; the cloud copy is the answer to "I lost the laptop". They are different products and this plan keeps both.

### New — the service

- `cloud/supabase/migrations/20260912000100_sync.sql` — `sync_records`, `sync_notes`, the `sync_notes_rev` sequence and its trigger, RLS on both, the per-account ceiling function, the retention function and its `pg_cron` schedule.
- `cloud/supabase/migrations_sync_test.ts` — C3's own migration guards, filtered to `20260912…` so C1's and C2's suites cannot go red on a C3 commit and vice versa.
- `cloud/supabase/functions/_shared/sync_rows.ts` — the row validators: the shapes of a pushed record and a pushed note, the hash and IV character classes, the caps. Pure; no I/O.
- `cloud/supabase/functions/_shared/sync_db.ts` — the four PostgREST calls these two endpoints make, over C1's `Rest`. The only database access C3 adds.
- `cloud/supabase/functions/_shared/sync_vectors.json` — two AES-256-GCM test vectors, one produced by Deno and one by Rust, so each language proves it can open the other's envelope. Committed, never regenerated by a test.
- `cloud/supabase/functions/_shared/sync_envelope_test.ts` — Deno's half of that proof.
- `cloud/supabase/functions/sync-push/{handler.ts,handler_test.ts,index.ts}` — `POST /sync-push`.
- `cloud/supabase/functions/sync-pull/{handler.ts,handler_test.ts,index.ts}` — `GET /sync-pull`.

Every `_shared/` file this plan creates is prefixed `sync_`, because `_shared/entitlement.ts`, `crypto.ts`, `db.ts`, `http.ts`, `auth.ts`, `scrub.ts` and `stripe.ts` are C1's and `_shared/judge_*.ts` are C2's.

### New — the device (engine)

- `engine/src/sync.rs` — one module, seven responsibilities, each a named section with its own tests: the key (`SyncKey`, `key_target`, `load_key`), the envelope (`seal`, `open`, `record_hash`, `note_ref`, `device_token`), the cursor (`Cursor`, `load_cursor`, `save_cursor`), the push builder (`build_push`), the pull reader (`pull`), the applier (`apply`), the restorer (`restore`), and the command body (`run_lines`). It is the only new engine module and it reaches the network only through C2's `cloudmodel::CloudClient`.
- `engine/tests/sync_contract.rs` — the loopback contract tests: every request shape the device sends, every reply it tolerates, and the data-minimisation assertions (no path, no title, no field name, no token on the wire).
- `engine/tests/sync_replay.rs` — the round trip: a fixture vault's notes and journal are pushed into a loopback server, pulled back into a temp directory, and compared **byte for byte**.

### New — the app

- `app/src/sync.rs` — the device half the app owns: generating the key, showing the recovery code, turning the switch on and off, staging a pasted recovery code before a vault exists, and the status the page renders. Its own Tauri commands live here (`CLAUDE.md`: commands live beside the module they serve).

### Modified — the device

- `engine/src/lib.rs` — one `pub mod sync;` added, one `pub mod history;` removed (hand-off **H1**).
- `engine/src/main.rs` — the `Sync` subcommand (hand-off **H2**).
- `engine/src/ingest.rs` — Task 13: the vault's `ics_url` stops being required, and a cloud vault whose service call failed exits 0 with a named line (hand-off **H3**).
- `engine/src/cli.rs` — Task 13: `calfeed`'s `- name: personal` entry resolves through `cloud:personal` (hand-off **H4**, an extension of C2's own H4).
- `engine/src/runs.rs` — untouched; `git_sha` stays (fidelity ledger).
- `engine/tests/dependency_boundary.rs` — one new test naming the two crypto dependencies.
- `engine/tests/no_console.rs` — its `with_spawns >= 3` floor becomes `>= 2` when `history.rs` goes, with the reason in the assertion message.

### Modified — the app

- `app/src/commands.rs` — `sync_inner` rewritten to call the engine's sync instead of git; `backup_now_inner` unchanged; `build_state_value`'s three git-derived topline keys removed (hand-off **H6**).
- `app/src/state.rs` — `run_sync`, `refresh_history`, `refresh_head` and four `ConsoleState` fields removed; one `SyncStatus` field added (hand-off **H6**).
- `app/src/scheduler.rs` — `sync` becomes the slot's first step; the two `sync_step` calls and the `has_remote` gate go; `ics_state` gains a cloud arm (hand-off **H7**).
- `app/src/onboarding.rs` — `WizardPlan.restore`, the pending sync key's move at Finish, and the picker's restore route (hand-off **H8**).
- `app/src/main.rs` — the two `generate_handler!` lists (hand-off **H8**).
- `app/src/scaffold.rs` — Task 13: `ingest_yaml` stops writing the two URLs (hand-off **H8**).
- `app/src/lms_link.rs` — Task 13: `finish` stops writing the vault copy (hand-off **H8**).
- `app/static/index.html`, `app/static/console.js`, `app/static/console.css` — the picker's restore link, the sync switch and its screen, the export row, the rewritten sync line. **C3's own files.**
- `site/privacy.html` — Task 13's copy change, under P2. **C3's own file.**
- `cloud/supabase/config.toml`, `cloud/supabase/deno.json` — two `[functions.<name>]` entries (hand-off **H10**).
- `.github/workflows/ci.yml` — the `cloud` job's path scope (hand-off **H9**, C0's file).
- `scripts/wizard-check.py`, `scripts/settings-check.py` — the restore route and the two new settings rows (hand-off **H8**).

### Deleted

- `engine/src/history.rs` (707 lines, with its own test module) — Task 12, under P4.

---

## Interfaces with C1 and C2

**Nine contracts, checked against the checkout on 2026-09-14.** Use these spellings verbatim; changing one is a conversation, not an edit.

**1. `config/cloud.yaml`** — written into the vault by C1's `scaffold::create_vault` at onboarding, absent on a vault that has never signed in. Exactly four keys, in this order:

```yaml
api_base: 'https://<ref>.supabase.co/functions/v1'
anon_key: '<the project anon key — public>'
session_credential_target: 'knowlu/<profile_id>/session'
account_id: '<uuid>'
```

C3 **adds no fifth key**. The sync key's Credential Manager target is derived from `session_credential_target` by `sync::key_target`, which is a documented derivation and not a new field: C1's own `account::auth_base` derives the auth base from `api_base` for exactly this reason ("adding a fifth would be a conversation"). The file is read through `pystr::read_text` + `serde_yaml_ng`, never by comparing bytes — it is a vault file and gets whatever line endings every other vault file gets.

**2. The session credential is a JSON object, not a bare JWT.** Windows Credential Manager, target `knowlu/<profile_id>/session`. `UserName` is the `account_id`; the blob is `{"access_token","refresh_token","expires_at","email"}`, `expires_at` in Unix seconds. **Refresh is C1's job** (`account::valid_access_token_at` refreshes at fewer than 120 seconds remaining and rewrites the entry). C3, like C2, **only reads it**, through C2's `cloudmodel::resolve`, and tolerates an expired one by reporting `no session` and waiting for the app's next slot.

**3. The sync key credential — C3's own, and the only credential this stream adds.** Windows Credential Manager, target **`knowlu/<profile_id>/sync-key`**.

| | |
|---|---|
| `UserName` | the key's **fingerprint**: the first 8 lowercase hex characters of `SHA-256(key bytes)`. Not a secret, and it is what the restore screen shows so a student can tell two keys apart. |
| blob | the 32-byte key, **standard base64 with padding**, 44 characters. Nothing else — not JSON, because a key is not a record and a JSON wrapper would only invite a second field. |

**The app writes it; the engine only reads it.** `app/src/credentials.rs::write` is the one writer of a Credential Manager entry in this product, and that does not change. **Its presence is the on-switch** (P2's switch, spec §5.5): a vault whose `config/cloud.yaml` resolves but whose `knowlu/<profile_id>/sync-key` is empty reports `sync (skipped: not turned on)` and exits 0. There is deliberately **no `Settings` field**: `state::Settings` has no `#[serde(default)]` on any field and `Settings::load` falls back to defaults on a parse failure, so adding one would silently reset a student's backup folder and autostart choice on their first launch after the update — the argument `account::cache_path` and `onboarding::offer_marker` both already make.

**4. `cloudmodel::CloudClient` — C2's, reused, and C3 adds no second HTTP client.** Task 4 of the C2 plan produces:

```rust
pub struct CloudConfig { pub api_base: String, pub anon_key: String, pub session_credential_target: String, pub account_id: String }
pub fn load(vault: &Path) -> Option<CloudConfig>;
pub enum Unavailable { NoConfig, NoSession(String) }          // .label() -> &'static str
pub fn resolve(vault: &Path) -> Result<CloudClient, Unavailable>;
pub enum CloudError { Transport(String), Status { code: u16, detail: String }, Body(String) }
                                                               // .label() -> &'static str, .fatal() -> bool
impl CloudClient {
    pub fn new(cfg: &CloudConfig, token: &str) -> CloudClient;
    pub fn account_id(&self) -> &str;
    pub fn post(&self, path: &str, body: &Value) -> Result<Value, CloudError>;
    pub fn get(&self, path: &str) -> Result<Value, CloudError>;
}
pub const CALL_TIMEOUT: Duration = Duration::from_secs(120);
```

`post` serialises through `ledger::dumps_value`, sends `Authorization: Bearer <the session's access token>` and `apikey: <anon key>`, and scrubs the bearer out of every error string it produces. C3 calls `post("/sync-push", …)` and `get("/sync-pull?…")` and nothing else. `CloudError::label()` maps 401 to `no session`, 402 to `no entitlement`, 429 to `rate limited`, a transport failure to `no network` — the same words the `sync` step prints, so a student reads the same sentence whichever cloud step hit it first.

**5. `cloud/supabase/functions/_shared/entitlement.ts`** — C1's, imported by both C3 functions and called first:

```ts
export async function requireActiveEntitlement(req: Request): Promise<{ account_id: string }>;
```

It **throws a `Response`** — 401 on a missing or invalid bearer, 402 when the account has no `active` or `trialing` entitlement — and so does `_shared/http.ts`'s `fail(status, message)`, which every validator in this stream uses.

**Nothing catches it inside a `handle`, and that is C1's shape, not an omission** (review finding B2). C1's own `telemetry/handler.ts` and `account/handler.ts` let a thrown `Response` propagate out of `handle` to `asResponse(e)` in `index.ts`, which returns it verbatim; C2's per-function `try { … } catch (e) { if (e instanceof Response) return e; throw e; }` is its own convention for handlers that also catch model errors, and C3 has none. **The consequence is a rule for every handler test in this plan**: a case that expects a refusal must await the rejection, exactly as C1's suite does at `cloud/supabase/functions/telemetry/handler_test.ts:79` —

```ts
const res = await handle(req, deps()).catch((e) => e as Response);
```

— and every such case in Tasks 4 and 5 is written that way. The production path is unchanged: `index.ts` wraps `handle` in `try { … } catch (e) { return asResponse(e); }`.

A 402 on `/sync-push` is a named skip on the device (`sync (skipped: no entitlement)`), exit 0, and nothing is lost — the records are still in the journal and go up at the next slot after the subscription resumes.

**6. `cloud/supabase/functions/_shared/db.ts`** — C1's PostgREST client, used verbatim:

```ts
export interface Rest { url: string; serviceKey: string; fetch: typeof fetch }
export async function restSelect<T>(rest: Rest, table: string, query: string): Promise<T[]>;
export async function restSelectAll<T>(rest: Rest, table: string, query: string, pageSize?: number): Promise<T[]>;
export async function restUpsert(rest: Rest, table: string, rows: unknown[], onConflict?: string): Promise<void>;
export async function restPatch(rest: Rest, table: string, query: string, patch: unknown): Promise<void>;
export async function restDelete(rest: Rest, table: string, query: string): Promise<void>;
export async function authGetUser(rest: Rest, token: string): Promise<AuthedUser | null>;
export function restFromEnv(): Rest;
```

Two things this constrains, and both are handled in Task 3: **PostgREST caps a select at `config.toml`'s `max_rows = 1000`**, so `/sync-pull` pages with an explicit `limit` and never relies on the default; and **the service role bypasses RLS**, so `account_id=eq.<id>` in every query string *is* the access control. C3 adds its own scan for that in Task 14, the way C2 does.

**And one thing these helpers cannot express, which C3 needs exactly twice.** `restUpsert` hard-codes `Prefer: resolution=merge-duplicates,return=minimal` and `restPatch` hard-codes `Prefer: return=minimal`; the key generation's compare-and-set needs `resolution=ignore-duplicates` on an insert and `return=representation` on a filtered patch, and neither helper takes a `Prefer`. **`_shared/db.ts` is C1's file and C3 does not widen it** — a `Prefer` parameter on two helpers three streams call is a signature change for one caller's benefit. So `_shared/sync_db.ts` makes those two calls with `rest.fetch` directly, rebuilding the same four headers (`apikey`, `authorization`, `content-type`, `prefer`) because `db.ts`'s `headers()` is private, and Task 14's account-scoping scan is extended to read them so they cannot slip past it. Two raw fetches, both inside `claimGeneration`, and the scan asserts that count.

**7. `cloud/supabase/functions/_shared/http.ts`** — C1's, used verbatim: `json(status, body)`, `fail(status, message)` (which throws-as-`Response` by convention at the call site), `methodNotAllowed(allowed)`, `subPath(url, fnName)`, `readJson<T>(req, limit?)` (throws 413 over the cap, 400 for non-JSON), `asResponse(e)` (a thrown `Response` goes back verbatim; anything else becomes a bare 500). Every error body in this codebase is `{"error": "<one sentence>"}`.

**8. `public.accounts` and `DELETE /account`** — C1's. The two sync tables reference `public.accounts (id) on delete cascade`, and C1's purge list in `cloud/supabase/functions/account/index.ts` gains two table names (hand-off **H5**). The cascade alone is not enough: the purge runs `restDelete` per table **before** the `accounts` row goes, and a table missing from that list is a table whose rows would only disappear if the cascade fired — which it does, but the list is what the privacy policy's deletion paragraph is written from, so it stays complete.

**9. `app/src/scheduler.rs`'s slot, as C1 and C2 left it.** After C2's merge the slot is

```rust
pub enum JudgePlan { Cloud { log_dir: PathBuf }, Local(JudgeArgs), Skip(&'static str) }
pub fn slot_argv(vault: &Path, exe: &Path, judge: &JudgePlan) -> Vec<(PathBuf, Vec<String>)>;
```

producing `coursework → [ingest] → [judge] → rank`, each the sibling `knowlu-engine.exe`. C3 prepends **one** step — `sync` — and changes nothing else about the function's shape (hand-off **H7**). `run_slot_inner` names a step by `args[0]`, so one `sync` step is one row in the Runs view; the two-step `sync pull … sync push` shape was considered and rejected for exactly that reason, and because the pull is the half that has to precede `rank`.

---

## Controller hand-offs

Every change below is outside C3's file ownership. **No task in this plan edits these files.**

**How and when they are applied — the controller's rule, learned from C2's execution and stated once here (ruling R-C3-8, resolving review findings I8 and I9).** Every hand-off in this section lands **on the branch, at the task that first needs it, as that task's own separate commit**, applied **verbatim from the hand-off text** by the controller and reviewed together with the task. It is not a merge-time activity and there is no mid-branch round trip to wait for: the implementer finishes the task, names the hand-off in its report, and the controller's commit follows immediately. Nothing in this plan is ever red for more than that one commit, and **no task in this plan may be reported green while a hand-off it names is unapplied**.

Each entry below therefore carries **"applied at Task N"**. The order is the task order, which is:

| Task | Hand-off applied with it |
|---|---|
| 2 | **H1a** — `engine/src/lib.rs`'s `pub mod sync;` (**compile-blocking**: nothing under `engine/src/sync.rs` is compiled, and no test in Tasks 2 or 6–9 links, until it lands) |
| 1 | **H5** — `account/index.ts`'s purge list (Task 1's last test is red until it lands) |
| 4 | **H10** — `config.toml`'s two `[functions.…]` entries (nothing deploys without them) |
| 8 | **H2** — `engine/src/main.rs`'s `Sync` subcommand; **H7a** — `scheduler.rs`'s slot step |
| 10 | **H8a** — `app/src/lib.rs`'s `pub mod sync;` and `app/src/main.rs`'s two handler lists; **H6a** — `ConsoleState.sync` |
| 11 | **H8b** — `WizardPlan.restore`, `create_vault_in`'s restore block, `scripts/wizard-check.py` |
| 12 | **H1b** — `lib.rs`'s `history` removal; **H6b** — `state.rs`/`commands.rs`'s git removal; **H7b** — `scheduler.rs`'s two sync steps and the housekeeping loop; **H8c** — `app/src/main.rs`'s startup thread |
| 13 | **H3** — `ingest.rs`; **H7c** — `ics_state`'s `Cloud` arm; **H8d** — `scaffold.rs` and `lms_link.rs`; **H12** — `site/index.html` |
| 14 | **H11** — the docs; **H4** and **H9** — verified, not edited |

`H1` and `H6`–`H8` are split into lettered parts because their halves are needed at different tasks; each part is a separate commit and each is written out in full below.

### H1a — `engine/src/lib.rs`, the addition (applied at Task 2; **compile-blocking**)

Add, after the `judge` / `runtime` / `judgelog` / `enrich` block and before `backup`:

```rust
// Knowlu C3 — journal sync (cloud design §5.5). Every record this device writes goes up sealed with
// a key that lives only in this machine's Credential Manager; every record another device of the
// same account wrote comes down and is applied through `write`. Transport, never judgment: `rank`
// does not reach it, and `sync` always exits 0.
pub mod sync;
```

### H1b — `engine/src/lib.rs`, the removal (applied at Task 12)

**Delete** these four lines:

```rust
// Git as a transport for the console's sync loop (Knowlu plan 1, Task 5). The journal is the
// real history; this module only drives `git` — status, commit by name, push/rebase, the lock.
pub mod childproc;
pub mod history;
```

replacing them with:

```rust
// `childproc` outlives `history`: `runs::git_sha` and `runtime.rs` both spawn children, and a GUI
// application's child process must never flash a console window.
pub mod childproc;
```

### H2 — `engine/src/main.rs`, the `Sync` subcommand (applied at Task 8)

One import change and one subcommand. In the `use` block, `knowlu_engine::{cli, coursework, enrich, ingest, journal, runs}` becomes `knowlu_engine::{cli, coursework, enrich, ingest, journal, runs, sync}`. Then, **between** the `Judge { … }` and `Runs { … }` variants:

```rust
    /// Send this device's new journal records and note text to the account's encrypted copy, and
    /// apply what another device of the same account wrote.
    ///
    /// Always exits 0: no account, no sync key, no entitlement and no network are all normal
    /// outcomes (cloud design §5.5), and a non-zero exit here would put the app's scheduler into
    /// retry backoff and paint the tray amber for a student who has simply not turned sync on.
    Sync {
        #[arg(long, default_value = ".")]
        vault: PathBuf,
        /// pull | push | both. The slot runs `both`; the console's Sync now runs `both`; the two
        /// halves are separable for a smoke test and for a restore that must not push.
        #[arg(long, default_value = "both", value_parser = ["pull", "push", "both"])]
        direction: String,
        /// Without these the run's writes journal as `via: cli, run_id: null` — indistinguishable
        /// from someone typing the command by hand.
        #[arg(long, default_value = "cli", value_parser = journal::VIAS)]
        via: String,
        #[arg(long = "run-id")]
        run_id: Option<String>,
    },
```

and, in `main`'s `match`, **between** the `Command::Judge` and `Command::Runs` arms:

```rust
        Command::Sync { vault, direction, via, run_id } => {
            // Always SUCCESS: `sync::run_lines` only ever returns 0, and this arm says so out loud
            // rather than mapping a code that cannot occur.
            let (_, lines) = sync::run_lines(
                &vault,
                sync::Direction::parse(&direction).unwrap_or(sync::Direction::Both),
                &via,
                run_id.as_deref(),
            );
            for line in lines {
                println!("{line}");
            }
            ExitCode::SUCCESS
        }
```

`ingest::run_lines` currently refuses a blank `ics_url` at `engine/src/ingest.rs:741-742`:

```rust
    if url.is_empty() { return (1, vec!["ingest: no ics_url configured".to_string()]); }
```

becomes — and C2's own hand-off H3 has already put the service call in front of it, so this is an amendment to H3's merged shape, not a replacement of it:

```rust
    let url = config.get("ics_url").and_then(|v| v.as_str()).unwrap_or("").trim().to_string();
    if url.is_empty() {
        // C3, Task 13: the capability URL lives in the account, not in the vault. A vault with an
        // account and no URL is the normal shape now, and an unreachable service is an OFFLINE
        // cloud step — exit 0 with a named line, exactly as `judge` and `sync` do, because a
        // non-zero exit here paints the tray amber twice a day forever for a student on a train.
        // A vault with NEITHER an account NOR a URL keeps the old exit 1: that is a misconfigured
        // vault, it is what `scheduler::ics_state` still refuses to run, and nothing has changed
        // about it.
        return match crate::cloudmodel::load(vault) {
            Some(_) => (0, vec!["ingest: the calendar service could not be reached; nothing imported".to_string()]),
            None => (1, vec!["ingest: no ics_url configured".to_string()]),
        };
    }
```

**`app/src/state.rs`.** Remove the `use` of `HistoryStatus`, the three fields `history`, `head_sha`, `auto_sync`, their initialisers, and the three functions `refresh_history`, `refresh_head`, `run_sync`. In `quit_flush`, the git branch

becomes

```rust
            // A quit flush pushes what is already on disk; it never pulls. A pull applies writes and
```

**`app/src/commands.rs`.** Remove the three git-derived topline keys in `build_state_value`:

```rust
    v["topline"]["vault_head"] = json!(head);
```


and the sync line's source:

```rust

```

becomes


and `auto_sync` goes:

```rust

```

is deleted — it existed to record "a merge conflict turned auto-sync off", and there are no merge conflicts any more: a conflict is an amend card and the next sync runs normally.

Finally `sync_inner` (`app/src/commands.rs:264-269`) becomes:

```rust
/// Runs the engine's sync OUTSIDE `cs.lock` — a pull can take seconds and must not block a
/// concurrent `state` poll — then takes the lock only to rebuild `state`. `ok` is
/// `last_error.is_none()`, not whether anything actually moved.
pub fn sync_inner(cs: &ConsoleState, view: &str) -> Result<Value, String> {
    let out = crate::sync::run_here(cs, knowlu_engine::sync::Direction::Both);
    let _g = cs.lock.lock().map_err(|_| "console lock poisoned".to_string())?;
    let state = build_state_value(cs, view)?;
    Ok(json!({ "ok": out.ok, "error": out.last_error, "state": state }))
}
```

The three doc comments in `commands.rs` that name `history::sync` as the reason `vault_io` exists (lines 142-143, 261, 276-277) are re-worded to name the engine's sync instead; the lock and its ordering are unchanged, and so is `backup_now_inner`.

### H7a — `app/src/scheduler.rs`, the slot's first step (applied at Task 8)

**One step, first.** In `slot_argv`:

```rust
pub fn slot_argv(vault: &Path, exe: &Path, judge: &JudgePlan) -> Vec<(PathBuf, Vec<String>)> {
    let v = vault.to_string_lossy().to_string();
    let mut steps = vec![(exe.to_path_buf(), vec!["coursework".into(), "--vault".into(), v.clone(), "--via".into(), "local-runner".into()])];
```

becomes

```rust
pub fn slot_argv(vault: &Path, exe: &Path, judge: &JudgePlan) -> Vec<(PathBuf, Vec<String>)> {
    let v = vault.to_string_lossy().to_string();
    // **`sync` first** (C3, cloud design §5.5). The pull is the half that has to precede `rank`: a
    // field another device set this morning must be in the note before the day is ordered, or every
    // second device ranks a slot behind forever. The push then carries everything written since the
    // last sync — the console's own edits and the previous slot's machine writes. One step, not two,
    // because `run_slot_inner` names a step by `args[0]` and two rows both reading `sync` would say
    // less than one row does. It always exits 0.
    let mut steps = vec![(exe.to_path_buf(), vec!["sync".into(), "--vault".into(), v.clone(), "--via".into(), "local-runner".into()])];
    steps.push((exe.to_path_buf(), vec!["coursework".into(), "--vault".into(), v.clone(), "--via".into(), "local-runner".into()]));
```

### H8b — `app/src/onboarding.rs` and `scripts/wizard-check.py` (applied at Task 11)

**`app/src/onboarding.rs`.** `WizardPlan` gains one field, after `personal_calendar`:

```rust
    /// The picker's *Restore from Knowlu's copy* route (Quinn, 2026-09-14). When true, Finish pulls
    /// the account's whole encrypted copy into the vault it has just created, before the profile is
    /// registered. `#[serde(default)]` so an older page that omits it is not a refusal.
    #[serde(default)]
    pub restore: bool,
```

and `create_vault_in`, immediately after the `move_session` block and before `finish_or_roll_back` — whose returned envelope gains one key so the page can say what happened:

```rust
    // **Placed after `scaffold::create_vault` and after `move_session`, and that is deliberate.**
    // The vault has to exist (its `config/cloud.yaml` is what `restore_into` reads) and the session
    // has to be on this profile (the pull needs a bearer). `scaffold` has therefore already seeded
    // `archive/_migrated.md`, `tasks/get-to-know-knowlu.md` and one `courses/<slug>.md` per course —
    // which is exactly why `restore_into` computes its allowlist from what is on disk right now
    // rather than demanding an empty folder.
    let mut restored = crate::sync::Restored::default();
    if plan.restore {
        // The recovery code was staged before the wizard started (`sync::stage_restore_key`), under
        // the same pending target the session used and for the same reason: the profile id is
        // derived from a vault path that did not exist yet. Move it, then pull.
        if let Err(e) = crate::sync::move_pending_key(&profile_id) {
            let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dest);
            return json!({ "ok": false, "error": format!("the recovery code could not be attached to this vault ({e}) — the new vault was removed, so nothing is half-made"), "profile": Value::Null });
        }
        match crate::sync::restore_into(&dest) {
            // **An empty copy keeps the vault.** A student who signs in on a second machine before
            // the first has ever pushed has made a perfectly good vault; rolling it back would throw
            // away a nine-panel wizard run to tell them, accurately and uselessly, that there was
            // nothing to restore. The finish panel says so instead (`restored.empty`).
            Ok(r) => restored = r,
            // A wrong recovery code, or a copy that will not open, IS a failure of this route: the
            // student asked for their vault back and did not get it, and a half-restored folder is
            // worse than none.
            Err(e) => {
                let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dest);
                return json!({ "ok": false, "error": e, "profile": Value::Null });
            }
        }
    }
```

The sentence Task 13 rewrites lives in **four** places, not three: `site/privacy.html:13`, `app/static/console.js:1371`, `engine/tests/site.rs:15`'s `const PRIVACY`, and **`site/index.html:16`** — and `engine/tests/site.rs:26-27` asserts it in both site pages:
