### Task 4: `POST /sync-push`

The first endpoint. It verifies the entitlement, refuses anything that is not an opaque bounded row, checks the account is under its ceiling, and upserts. It never decrypts, never logs a row, and never tells the caller anything about another device.

**Files:**
- Create: `cloud/supabase/functions/_shared/sync_db.ts`, `cloud/supabase/functions/sync-push/{handler.ts,handler_test.ts,index.ts}`
- **Hand-off this task needs:** **H10** (`config.toml`'s two `[functions.…]` entries), applied by the controller beside this task

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: C1's `requireActiveEntitlement`, `_shared/db.ts`, `_shared/http.ts`; Task 3's `sync_rows.ts`; Task 1's tables.
- Produces:
  - `sync_db.ts`: `READ_LAG_SECONDS`, `saveRecords(rest, rows)`, `saveNotes(rest, rows)`, `bytesUsed(rest, accountId)`, `resetCopy(rest, accountId)`, `generation(rest, accountId)`, **`claimGeneration(rest, accountId, fingerprint, expected) -> Promise<string>`** (a compare-and-set that answers who holds the generation afterwards), `readRecords(rest, accountId, after, limit, now)`, `readNotes(rest, accountId, after, limit, now)`
  - `POST /sync-push` → `200 {"records": n, "notes": m, "bytes_used": b, "bytes_ceiling": c}`; the body carries a required `key_fingerprint` and an optional `reset: true` that empties the account's copy and claims a new generation (P5); 400 on a malformed row or fingerprint; 401/402 from the gate — **thrown**, so a handler test awaits the rejection; **409** `{"error", "key_fingerprint"}` when this machine's key is not the account's, storing nothing; **413** when the batch would carry the account past its ceiling.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test** — `cloud/supabase/functions/sync-push/handler_test.ts`:

```ts
import { assert, assertEquals } from "@std/assert";
import { handle } from "./handler.ts";

const DEVICE = "0123456789abcdef";
const HASH = (n: number) => n.toString(16).padStart(64, "0");
const IV = "AAAAAAAAAAAAAAAA";
const FP = "0a1b2c3d";
const OTHER_FP = "9f8e7d6c";
const OK = () => Promise.resolve({ account_id: "acct-1" });

function deps(over: Partial<Parameters<typeof handle>[1]> = {}) {
  return {
    requireEntitled: OK,
    bytesUsed: () => Promise.resolve(0),
    ceiling: () => Promise.resolve(1_000_000),
    saveRecords: () => Promise.resolve(),
    saveNotes: () => Promise.resolve(),
    resetCopy: () => Promise.resolve(),
    generation: () => Promise.resolve(FP as string | null),
    // The default account is uncontested: whoever claims, wins.
    claimGeneration: (_id: string, fp: string) => Promise.resolve(fp),
    ...over,
  };
}

/** Every request in this file carries the account's own fingerprint unless it says otherwise. */
function push(body: Record<string, unknown>): Request {
  return new Request("http://127.0.0.1/sync-push", {
    method: "POST",
    headers: { authorization: "Bearer t", "content-type": "application/json" },
    body: JSON.stringify({ key_fingerprint: FP, ...body }),
  });
}

/** **Every refusal in this codebase is a THROWN `Response`** — `_shared/http.ts`'s `fail` throws and
 * `requireActiveEntitlement` rejects, and `index.ts`'s `asResponse` is what turns either into the
 * reply. A handler test therefore awaits the rejection, exactly as C1's
 * `telemetry/handler_test.ts:79` does; a bare `await handle(…)` would reject the test instead of
 * failing an assertion. */
function refusal(req: Request, d: Parameters<typeof handle>[1]): Promise<Response> {
  return handle(req, d).catch((e) => e as Response);
}

Deno.test("a batch of records and notes is stored and counted", async () => {
  let records: unknown[] = [];
  let notes: unknown[] = [];
  const res = await handle(
    push({
      device: DEVICE,
      records: [{ hash: HASH(1), iv: IV, ciphertext: "Zm9v" }, { hash: HASH(2), iv: IV, ciphertext: "YmFy" }],
      notes: [{ ref: HASH(3), iv: IV, ciphertext: "YmF6" }, { ref: HASH(4), deleted: true }],
    }),
    deps({ saveRecords: (r) => { records = r; return Promise.resolve(); }, saveNotes: (n) => { notes = n; return Promise.resolve(); } }),
  );
  assertEquals(res.status, 200);
  assertEquals(await res.json(), { records: 2, notes: 2, bytes_used: 0, bytes_ceiling: 1_000_000 });
  assertEquals(records.length, 2);
  assertEquals(notes.length, 2);
  assertEquals((records[0] as Record<string, unknown>).account_id, "acct-1");
  assertEquals((notes[1] as Record<string, unknown>).deleted, true);
});

Deno.test("a duplicate row inside one batch becomes one write", async () => {
  // `sync_records_once` catches it across retries; this catches it within a batch, before the
  // upsert, the same way C1's `/telemetry` dedupes on its own unique key.
  let records: unknown[] = [];
  const res = await handle(
    push({ device: DEVICE, records: [{ hash: HASH(1), iv: IV, ciphertext: "Zm9v" }, { hash: HASH(1), iv: IV, ciphertext: "Zm9v" }] }),
    deps({ saveRecords: (r) => { records = r; return Promise.resolve(); } }),
  );
  assertEquals(res.status, 200);
  assertEquals((await res.json()).records, 1);
  assertEquals(records.length, 1);
});

Deno.test("a row with a plaintext field beside the ciphertext is a 400 that names the field", async () => {
  const res = await refusal(
    push({ device: DEVICE, records: [{ hash: HASH(1), iv: IV, ciphertext: "Zm9v", title: "CS 100 HW 1" }] }),
    deps(),
  );
  assertEquals(res.status, 400);
  const body = await res.json();
  assert(body.error.includes("title"), body.error);
});

Deno.test("a device token that is not a device token is a 400", async () => {
  assertEquals((await refusal(push({ device: "nope", records: [] }), deps())).status, 400);
  assertEquals((await refusal(push({ records: [] }), deps())).status, 400);
});

Deno.test("a push with no usable key fingerprint is a 400", async () => {
  const bare = new Request("http://127.0.0.1/sync-push", {
    method: "POST",
    headers: { authorization: "Bearer t", "content-type": "application/json" },
    body: JSON.stringify({ device: DEVICE, records: [] }),
  });
  assertEquals((await refusal(bare, deps())).status, 400);
  assertEquals((await refusal(push({ device: DEVICE, key_fingerprint: "NOPE", records: [] }), deps())).status, 400);
});

Deno.test("a device whose key is not the account's is a 409 that names the account's, and stores nothing", async () => {
  // **The account's OTHER devices.** One machine turned the switch off and on again and re-uploaded
  // the whole vault under a fresh key; this one's key is unchanged and perfectly good, but the copy
  // is no longer sealed with it. Storing its rows would fill the copy with bytes nobody can open.
  // The reply carries the account's fingerprint so the device can tell the student which key it
  // needs — a fingerprint is not a key and not reversible.
  let stored = 0;
  let emptied = 0;
  const res = await handle(
    push({ device: DEVICE, key_fingerprint: OTHER_FP, records: [{ hash: HASH(1), iv: IV, ciphertext: "Zm9v" }] }),
    deps({
      generation: () => Promise.resolve(FP),
      saveRecords: () => { stored += 1; return Promise.resolve(); },
      resetCopy: () => { emptied += 1; return Promise.resolve(); },
    }),
  );
  assertEquals(res.status, 409);
  assertEquals((await res.json()).key_fingerprint, FP);
  assertEquals([stored, emptied], [0, 0], "a stale device neither writes nor empties");
});

Deno.test("the first push of an account's life claims the generation", async () => {
  let claimed = "";
  const res = await handle(
    push({ device: DEVICE, records: [{ hash: HASH(1), iv: IV, ciphertext: "Zm9v" }] }),
    deps({ generation: () => Promise.resolve(null), claimGeneration: (_id, fp) => { claimed = fp; return Promise.resolve(fp); } }),
  );
  assertEquals(res.status, 200);
  assertEquals(claimed, FP);
});

Deno.test("a reset claims the new generation as well as emptying the old copy", async () => {
  const order: string[] = [];
  const res = await handle(
    push({ device: DEVICE, key_fingerprint: OTHER_FP, reset: true, records: [] }),
    deps({
      generation: () => Promise.resolve(FP),
      resetCopy: () => { order.push("reset"); return Promise.resolve(); },
      claimGeneration: (_id, fp) => { order.push("claim"); return Promise.resolve(fp); },
    }),
  );
  assertEquals(res.status, 200);
  assertEquals(order, ["claim", "reset"], "own it, THEN empty it — a reset by a device that lost the claim destroys somebody else's copy");
});

Deno.test("every push claims, including one whose key already matches", async () => {
  // **Not an optimisation to skip.** The claim is a compare-and-set against the value just read, so
  // an ordinary push is how a device learns that a re-key landed underneath it between its read and
  // its write. Skipping it for a matching key is exactly the hole this fix closes.
  let expected: string | null | undefined;
  await handle(push({ device: DEVICE, records: [] }), deps({
    claimGeneration: (_id, fp, exp) => { expected = exp; return Promise.resolve(fp); },
  }));
  assertEquals(expected, FP, "and it carries the value it read, which is what makes it a compare-and-set");
});

Deno.test("an empty batch is a 200 and writes nothing", async () => {
  let called = 0;
  const res = await handle(push({ device: DEVICE, records: [], notes: [] }), deps({
    saveRecords: () => { called += 1; return Promise.resolve(); },
    saveNotes: () => { called += 1; return Promise.resolve(); },
  }));
  assertEquals(res.status, 200);
  assertEquals(await res.json(), { records: 0, notes: 0, bytes_used: 0, bytes_ceiling: 1_000_000 });
  assertEquals(called, 0);
});

Deno.test("a batch that would take the account past its ceiling is a 413 that says the numbers", async () => {
  const res = await handle(
    push({ device: DEVICE, records: [{ hash: HASH(1), iv: IV, ciphertext: "A".repeat(100) }] }),
    deps({ bytesUsed: () => Promise.resolve(999_990), ceiling: () => Promise.resolve(1_000_000) }),
  );
  assertEquals(res.status, 413);
  const body = await res.json();
  assert(body.error.includes("1000000"), body.error);
  // Nothing is stored — a partial write past the ceiling would leave the account stuck, able to
  // push neither more nor less.
  assertEquals(body.bytes_used, 999_990);
});

Deno.test("over five hundred rows of either kind is a 400, not a slow 200", async () => {
  const many = Array.from({ length: 501 }, (_, i) => ({ hash: HASH(i + 1), iv: IV, ciphertext: "Zm9v" }));
  assertEquals((await refusal(push({ device: DEVICE, records: many }), deps())).status, 400);
});

Deno.test("a reset empties the account's copy before it stores anything, and only then", async () => {
  // P5: the student turned the switch off and on again, so this device has a new key and every row
  // in the account is unopenable by anyone. The device says so once, with its first push under the
  // new key; the order is delete-then-store, because the reverse would drop the batch it just took.
  const order: string[] = [];
  // **The fake models the delete**, because that is what the assertion below is about: an account
  // sitting at 999,999 of its 1,000,000 bytes, emptied by the reset, and a re-upload that therefore
  // fits. A `bytesUsed` that ignored `resetCopy` would make the last line true by accident.
  let emptied = false;
  const res = await handle(
    push({ device: DEVICE, key_fingerprint: OTHER_FP, reset: true, records: [{ hash: HASH(1), iv: IV, ciphertext: "Zm9v" }] }),
    deps({
      generation: () => Promise.resolve(FP),
      resetCopy: () => { order.push("reset"); emptied = true; return Promise.resolve(); },
      saveRecords: () => { order.push("store"); return Promise.resolve(); },
      bytesUsed: () => Promise.resolve(emptied ? 0 : 999_999),
    }),
  );
  assertEquals(res.status, 200);
  assertEquals(order, ["reset", "store"]);
  // And the ceiling is measured AFTER the reset — the old copy's bytes are gone, so a full
  // re-upload is not refused by the rows it is replacing. Without the ordering this is a 413.
  assertEquals((await res.json()).bytes_used, 4);
});

Deno.test("on a first-push race exactly one device wins and the other stores nothing", async () => {
  // Two machines of one account, two keys, neither having pushed before: both read `generation` as
  // `null` and both believe they may write. The database settles it — `claimGeneration` is an INSERT
  // with `resolution=ignore-duplicates` followed by a re-read — and the loser is told with the same
  // 409 a stale device gets, having stored nothing. Without this, the copy would hold two
  // generations, neither device would know, and a later restore would open some rows and refuse the
  // rest: a silently partial vault.
  let stored = 0;
  const loser = await handle(
    push({ device: DEVICE, key_fingerprint: OTHER_FP, records: [{ hash: HASH(1), iv: IV, ciphertext: "Zm9v" }] }),
    deps({
      generation: () => Promise.resolve(null),
      // The other device got there first; the re-read inside `claimGeneration` says so.
      claimGeneration: () => Promise.resolve(FP),
      saveRecords: () => { stored += 1; return Promise.resolve(); },
    }),
  );
  assertEquals(loser.status, 409);
  assertEquals((await loser.json()).key_fingerprint, FP);
  assertEquals(stored, 0, "the loser of a first-push race writes nothing");

  // …and the winner of the same race is an ordinary 200.
  const winner = await handle(
    push({ device: DEVICE, records: [{ hash: HASH(1), iv: IV, ciphertext: "Zm9v" }] }),
    deps({ generation: () => Promise.resolve(null), claimGeneration: (_id, fp) => Promise.resolve(fp) }),
  );
  assertEquals(winner.status, 200);
});

Deno.test("a re-key landing under a normal push refuses the pusher instead of orphaning its rows", async () => {
  // The other race: this device's key IS the account's when it reads, and another machine re-keys in
  // the instant before it writes. The compare-and-set is filtered on the value this request read, so
  // the PATCH matches nothing, `claimGeneration` answers with the new owner, and the push is refused
  // with nothing stored. Before the fix its rows would have gone into a copy sealed under a key it
  // does not have — unreadable by anyone, and counting against the ceiling until the next reset.
  let stored = 0;
  let emptied = 0;
  const res = await handle(
    push({ device: DEVICE, records: [{ hash: HASH(1), iv: IV, ciphertext: "Zm9v" }], notes: [{ ref: HASH(2), iv: IV, ciphertext: "YmFy" }] }),
    deps({
      generation: () => Promise.resolve(FP),
      claimGeneration: () => Promise.resolve(OTHER_FP),
      saveRecords: () => { stored += 1; return Promise.resolve(); },
      saveNotes: () => { stored += 1; return Promise.resolve(); },
      resetCopy: () => { emptied += 1; return Promise.resolve(); },
    }),
  );
  assertEquals(res.status, 409);
  const body = await res.json();
  assertEquals(body.key_fingerprint, OTHER_FP);
  assert(body.error.includes(OTHER_FP), body.error);
  assertEquals([stored, emptied], [0, 0], "nothing stored, and nothing emptied by a device that lost the claim");
});

Deno.test("without the flag nothing is ever emptied", async () => {
  let reset = 0;
  await handle(push({ device: DEVICE, records: [] }), deps({ resetCopy: () => { reset += 1; return Promise.resolve(); } }));
  await handle(push({ device: DEVICE, reset: false, records: [] }), deps({ resetCopy: () => { reset += 1; return Promise.resolve(); } }));
  assertEquals(reset, 0);
  assertEquals((await refusal(push({ device: DEVICE, reset: "yes", records: [] }), deps())).status, 400);
});

Deno.test("anything but POST is a 405", async () => {
  const res = await handle(new Request("http://127.0.0.1/sync-push", { method: "GET" }), deps());
  assertEquals(res.status, 405);
  assertEquals(res.headers.get("allow"), "POST");
});

Deno.test("the entitlement gate answers before anything is read", async () => {
  let read = false;
  const res = await refusal(
    push({ device: DEVICE, records: [{ hash: HASH(1), iv: IV, ciphertext: "Zm9v" }] }),
    deps({
      requireEntitled: () => Promise.reject(new Response(JSON.stringify({ error: "this account has no active subscription" }), { status: 402 })),
      bytesUsed: () => { read = true; return Promise.resolve(0); },
    }),
  );
  assertEquals(res.status, 402);
  assertEquals(read, false);
});
```

Run: `deno test --allow-read --config cloud/supabase/deno.json cloud/supabase/functions/sync-push/`
Expected: FAIL — `Module not found "./handler.ts"`.

- [ ] **Step 3: Write `cloud/supabase/functions/sync-push/handler.ts`.**

```ts
/**
 * `POST /sync-push` — the device's new journal records and changed notes, sealed. Spec §5.5.
 *
 * This function **cannot read what it stores** and is written so that it could not start to. It
 * takes opaque rows, checks their shapes, bounds the batch and the account, and upserts. Two things
 * it deliberately does not do: it does not tell the caller anything about another device (a push
 * reply carries counts and the account's own usage, and no cursor — a device advances its pull
 * cursor only past rows it has actually seen), and it does not log a row, not even on an error,
 * because a ciphertext in a log is a ciphertext in a place nobody is watching.
 */
import { fail, json, methodNotAllowed, readJson } from "../_shared/http.ts";
import { checkNote, checkRecord, isDeviceToken, isFingerprint, MAX_ROWS } from "../_shared/sync_rows.ts";

export interface Deps {
  /** C1's `requireActiveEntitlement`, injected so this handler stays testable. */
  requireEntitled: (req: Request) => Promise<{ account_id: string }>;
  bytesUsed: (accountId: string) => Promise<number>;
  ceiling: () => Promise<number>;
  saveRecords: (rows: unknown[]) => Promise<void>;
  saveNotes: (rows: unknown[]) => Promise<void>;
  /** Delete every row this account has. Reached by one path only — see `reset` in the handler. */
  resetCopy: (accountId: string) => Promise<void>;
  /** Which key generation this account's copy is sealed under, or null if nothing is stored yet. */
  generation: (accountId: string) => Promise<string | null>;
  /** Compare-and-set against `expected` (the value `generation` just returned). Answers the
   * fingerprint the account ends up under — ours if we won the claim, somebody else's if we did
   * not. Never throws for a lost race; losing is an answer, not an error. */
  claimGeneration: (accountId: string, fingerprint: string, expected: string | null) => Promise<string>;
}

/** The last row wins, keyed on the database's own unique column — a batch retried after a partial
 * failure carries the same rows twice, and this is where that becomes one write. */
function dedupeBy<T>(rows: T[], key: (row: T) => string): T[] {
  const byKey = new Map<string, T>();
  for (const row of rows) byKey.set(key(row), row);
  return [...byKey.values()];
}

export async function handle(req: Request, deps: Deps): Promise<Response> {
  if (req.method !== "POST") return methodNotAllowed(["POST"]);
  const { account_id } = await deps.requireEntitled(req);
  const body = await readJson<
    { device?: unknown; key_fingerprint?: unknown; records?: unknown; notes?: unknown; reset?: unknown }
  >(req);
  if (!isDeviceToken(body.device)) throw fail(400, "device must be a sixteen-character lower-case hex token");
  const device = body.device as string;
  if (!isFingerprint(body.key_fingerprint)) {
    throw fail(400, "key_fingerprint must be eight lower-case hex characters");
  }
  const fingerprint = body.key_fingerprint as string;
  if (body.records !== undefined && !Array.isArray(body.records)) throw fail(400, "records must be an array");
  if (body.notes !== undefined && !Array.isArray(body.notes)) throw fail(400, "notes must be an array");
  const records = (body.records ?? []) as unknown[];
  const notes = (body.notes ?? []) as unknown[];
  if (records.length > MAX_ROWS || notes.length > MAX_ROWS) {
    throw fail(400, `at most ${MAX_ROWS} rows of each kind per batch`);
  }
  if (body.reset !== undefined && typeof body.reset !== "boolean") throw fail(400, "reset must be a boolean");

  const recordRows = dedupeBy(records.map((r) => checkRecord(r, device, account_id)), (r) => r.record_hash as string);
  const noteRows = dedupeBy(notes.map((n) => checkNote(n, device, account_id)), (n) => n.note_ref as string);

  // **The key generation, and it decides everything below** (precondition P5, and the account's
  // OTHER devices). Four cases, in five lines:
  //
  //   * nothing stored yet         -> this push claims the generation;
  //   * the account's own key      -> normal;
  //   * a different key, `reset`   -> a deliberate re-key: empty the copy, claim the generation;
  //   * a different key, no reset  -> **409, and nothing is stored**.
  //
  // The fourth case is the account's second device after the first one re-keyed. Its key is fine and
  // its vault is fine; what it has lost is the ability to read or add to the shared copy, and the
  // only honest thing this endpoint can do is say so in a way the device can turn into a sentence.
  // Storing its rows would fill the copy with bytes nobody can open; letting it pull would hand it
  // rows it cannot decrypt. `409` — the conflict status — carries the account's current fingerprint
  // so the device can show the student which key it needs.
  const current = await deps.generation(account_id);
  const refuse = (owner: string) =>
    json(409, {
      // The fingerprint is IN the sentence as well as in its own field: `CloudError` keeps only the
      // `error` string, so a sentence that names it is what lets the desktop show the student which
      // key it needs without a second round trip or a parser.
      error: `this machine's key is not the one this account's copy is sealed under (the account's key is ${owner})`,
      key_fingerprint: owner,
    });
  if (current !== null && current !== fingerprint && body.reset !== true) return refuse(current);

  // **Claim before emptying and before storing, and let the DATABASE settle the race.** The read
  // above cannot: two devices with two keys can both see `null` on an account's first push, or one
  // can re-key in the instant between another's read and its write, and a read-then-upsert would let
  // both store — a copy under two generations, neither device told, and a restore that opens some
  // rows and refuses the rest. `claimGeneration` is a compare-and-set against the value just read and
  // answers who actually holds the generation; anything but us is the same 409 a stale device gets by
  // the ordinary route. **Every push claims**, including one whose key already matches: that is how a
  // normal push learns that a re-key landed underneath it instead of orphaning its own rows.
  const owner = await deps.claimGeneration(account_id, fingerprint, current);
  if (owner !== fingerprint) return refuse(owner);

  // **Validated first, claimed second, emptied third, stored fourth.** A malformed batch must never
  // empty a copy it could not then refill; a reset must never run for a device that lost the claim;
  // and the ceiling below is then measured on an account whose orphaned bytes are already gone, which
  // is what makes a full re-upload fit.
  if (body.reset === true) await deps.resetCopy(account_id);

  const [used, ceiling] = await Promise.all([deps.bytesUsed(account_id), deps.ceiling()]);
  const adding = [...recordRows, ...noteRows]
    .reduce((n, r) => n + String(r.ciphertext ?? "").length, 0);
  if (used + adding > ceiling) {
    // 413, and nothing is stored. A partial write past the ceiling would leave the account able to
    // push neither more nor less, which is the one state a student cannot get themselves out of.
    // The numbers are in the body because the app's own line says them out loud.
    return json(413, {
      error: `this account's cloud copy is full (${used + adding} of ${ceiling} bytes)`,
      bytes_used: used,
      bytes_ceiling: ceiling,
    });
  }

  if (recordRows.length) await deps.saveRecords(recordRows);
  if (noteRows.length) await deps.saveNotes(noteRows);
  return json(200, {
    records: recordRows.length,
    notes: noteRows.length,
    bytes_used: used + adding,
    bytes_ceiling: ceiling,
  });
}
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Write `cloud/supabase/functions/_shared/sync_db.ts`.**

```ts
/**
 * The five PostgREST calls C3 makes, over C1's `Rest`. The only database access this stream adds.
 *
 * **`account_id=eq.<id>` in every query string is the access control.** The service role bypasses
 * RLS — that is what makes an edge function the only writer any table has — so a select on a scoped
 * table that forgets the predicate reads every account's rows. Task 14's scan is the backstop, and
 * this file is small enough to read in one sitting on purpose.
 */
import { Rest, restDelete, restSelect, restUpsert } from "./db.ts";
import { fail } from "./http.ts";

/** **The read lag, and why a cursor alone is not enough.** `seq` and `rev` are assigned at INSERT
 * and become visible at COMMIT, so two overlapping pushes can take 100 and 101 and commit in the
 * other order: a pull landing between the two commits sees 101, the device saves
 * `record_cursor = 101`, and row 100 is never delivered, because every later pull asks for
 * `seq > 101`. One device is mostly safe; two devices pushing in the same second are the case this
 * feature exists for.
 *
 * **What the lag actually bounds, stated honestly.** `received_at` and `updated_at` default to
 * `now()`, which is the **transaction's start**, not its commit — so skipping rows younger than ten
 * seconds bounds *insert-to-commit* time and not commit visibility. A transaction that stayed open
 * longer than the lag *after* its insert could still be stranded. Two things make that safe rather
 * than lucky, and both are named so nobody has to rediscover them: every write here is a **single
 * PostgREST upsert**, which is one statement in one implicit transaction and commits in
 * milliseconds; and Supabase's REST role runs under a `statement_timeout` and an
 * `idle_in_transaction_session_timeout` far below ten seconds, so a transaction that outlives the
 * lag has already been killed. The residual — a bulk write made by hand through `psql` while a
 * device is pulling — is not a path this product has.
 *
 * The cost is a ten-second delivery delay on a twice-a-day sync, which is not a cost. */
export const READ_LAG_SECONDS = 10;

export async function saveRecords(rest: Rest, rows: unknown[]): Promise<void> {
  // A resent batch lands on the rows it landed on the first time: `sync_records_once` is the dedup
  // and `merge-duplicates` is what makes a retry free.
  await restUpsert(rest, "sync_records", rows, "account_id,record_hash");
}

export async function saveNotes(rest: Rest, rows: unknown[]): Promise<void> {
  // A note is current state: the upsert replaces the text, and the table's own trigger stamps a
  // fresh `rev` so a second device learns it changed.
  await restUpsert(rest, "sync_notes", rows, "account_id,note_ref");
}

export async function bytesUsed(rest: Rest, accountId: string): Promise<number> {
  const rows = await restSelect<{ bytes: number }>(
    rest,
    "sync_usage",
    `account_id=eq.${encodeURIComponent(accountId)}&select=bytes&limit=1`,
  );
  return rows[0]?.bytes ?? 0;
}

export interface RecordOut {
  seq: number;
  device: string;
  record_hash: string;
  iv: string;
  ciphertext: string;
}

export interface NoteOut {
  rev: number;
  note_ref: string;
  device: string;
  deleted: boolean;
  iv: string | null;
  ciphertext: string | null;
}

/** Rows that settled at least `READ_LAG_SECONDS` ago, as a PostgREST predicate. */
function settled(now: Date): string {
  return `lt.${new Date(now.getTime() - READ_LAG_SECONDS * 1000).toISOString()}`;
}

export async function readRecords(
  rest: Rest,
  accountId: string,
  after: number,
  limit: number,
  now: Date,
): Promise<RecordOut[]> {
  // **`order=seq.asc` and an explicit `limit`.** PostgREST caps a select at `config.toml`'s
  // `max_rows` (1000) whether or not the caller asked, and offset paging over an unordered result is
  // free to hand the same row back twice — C1's `restSelectAll` says so in its own doc.
  //
  // **`received_at=lt.…`** is the commit-order guard: see `READ_LAG_SECONDS`.
  return await restSelect<RecordOut>(
    rest,
    "sync_records",
    `account_id=eq.${encodeURIComponent(accountId)}&seq=gt.${after}&received_at=${settled(now)}` +
      `&select=seq,device,record_hash,iv,ciphertext&order=seq.asc&limit=${limit}`,
  );
}

export async function readNotes(
  rest: Rest,
  accountId: string,
  after: number,
  limit: number,
  now: Date,
): Promise<NoteOut[]> {
  return await restSelect<NoteOut>(
    rest,
    "sync_notes",
    `account_id=eq.${encodeURIComponent(accountId)}&rev=gt.${after}&updated_at=${settled(now)}` +
      `&select=rev,note_ref,device,deleted,iv,ciphertext&order=rev.asc&limit=${limit}`,
  );
}

/** Every row this account has, gone. **The only delete in this stream**, reached by exactly one
 * path: `POST /sync-push` with `reset: true`, which the device sends on its first push under a new
 * key (P5). `sync_usage`'s trigger decrements as the rows go, so the counter stays honest with no
 * second write. The generation row is NOT deleted here — the same request claims the new one, and a
 * moment with no generation would let another device's push claim it instead. */
export async function resetCopy(rest: Rest, accountId: string): Promise<void> {
  const eq = `account_id=eq.${encodeURIComponent(accountId)}`;
  await restDelete(rest, "sync_records", eq);
  await restDelete(rest, "sync_notes", eq);
}

/** Which key generation this account's copy is sealed under, or `null` if nothing is stored yet. */
export async function generation(rest: Rest, accountId: string): Promise<string | null> {
  const rows = await restSelect<{ key_fingerprint: string }>(
    rest,
    "sync_generation",
    `account_id=eq.${encodeURIComponent(accountId)}&select=key_fingerprint&limit=1`,
  );
  return rows[0]?.key_fingerprint ?? null;
}

/**
 * The four headers `_shared/db.ts` puts on every call, rebuilt here.
 *
 * **Why this file makes two `fetch` calls of its own instead of using C1's helpers.** `restUpsert`
 * hard-codes `Prefer: resolution=merge-duplicates,return=minimal` and `restPatch` hard-codes
 * `Prefer: return=minimal`; the compare-and-set below needs `resolution=ignore-duplicates` on one
 * and `return=representation` on the other, and **`_shared/db.ts` is C1's file** — widening its two
 * helpers to take a `Prefer` would change a signature three streams call. `headers()` in that file is
 * private, so the four fields are spelled again here. Same service role, same content type; nothing
 * about the access path differs.
 */
function syncHeaders(rest: Rest, prefer: string): Record<string, string> {
  return {
    apikey: rest.serviceKey,
    authorization: `Bearer ${rest.serviceKey}`,
    "content-type": "application/json",
    prefer,
  };
}

/** `_shared/db.ts`'s `ok()`, which is private there. The upstream body reaches the log and never the
 * caller, for the reason C1's own comment gives. */
async function okOrThrow(res: Response, what: string): Promise<void> {
  if (res.ok) return;
  console.error(`${what}: ${res.status} ${await res.text()}`);
  throw fail(502, `${what} failed`);
}

/**
 * Claim the account's key generation, and answer **who actually holds it afterwards**.
 *
 * **Compare-and-set, not read-then-write, and that is the whole point of this function.** A
 * `select` followed by an upsert lets two devices with two different keys both read the same answer,
 * both decide they may write, and both store — leaving a copy sealed under two generations, neither
 * device told, and a later restore opening some rows and refusing the rest. The database is the only
 * place that race can be settled, so it is settled there:
 *
 *   * **the first claim of an account's life** is an INSERT with `resolution=ignore-duplicates`, so a
 *     second racer's row is a no-op rather than an overwrite, followed by a re-read that says which
 *     of them got there first;
 *   * **every later claim** — a re-key, and also an ordinary push confirming it still owns the
 *     generation — is a PATCH **filtered on the value this request read** (`key_fingerprint=eq.…`)
 *     with `return=representation`: a row comes back if and only if nothing moved underneath, and an
 *     empty array means somebody else claimed it in between.
 *
 * Returns the fingerprint the account ends up under. The caller compares it against its own and
 * refuses with a 409 when they differ — which is the same answer a stale device gets by the ordinary
 * route, so there is one thing for the device to say and one thing for the student to do.
 */
export async function claimGeneration(
  rest: Rest,
  accountId: string,
  fingerprint: string,
  expected: string | null,
): Promise<string> {
  const stamp = new Date().toISOString();
  if (expected === null) {
    const res = await rest.fetch(`${rest.url}/rest/v1/sync_generation?on_conflict=account_id`, {
      method: "POST",
      headers: syncHeaders(rest, "resolution=ignore-duplicates,return=minimal"),
      body: JSON.stringify([{ account_id: accountId, key_fingerprint: fingerprint, set_at: stamp }]),
    });
    await okOrThrow(res, "claim sync_generation");
  } else {
    const res = await rest.fetch(
      `${rest.url}/rest/v1/sync_generation?account_id=eq.${encodeURIComponent(accountId)}` +
        `&key_fingerprint=eq.${encodeURIComponent(expected)}`,
      {
        method: "PATCH",
        headers: syncHeaders(rest, "return=representation"),
        body: JSON.stringify({ key_fingerprint: fingerprint, set_at: stamp }),
      },
    );
    await okOrThrow(res, "claim sync_generation");
    const moved = await res.json() as Array<{ key_fingerprint: string }>;
    if (moved.length === 1) return moved[0].key_fingerprint;
  }
  // The insert was ignored, or the compare-and-set matched nothing: somebody else owns it, and the
  // only honest answer is whatever is actually stored.
  return (await generation(rest, accountId)) ?? fingerprint;
}
```

- [ ] **Step 5: Write `cloud/supabase/functions/sync-push/index.ts`.**

```ts
import { restFromEnv, restSelect } from "../_shared/db.ts";
import { requireActiveEntitlement } from "../_shared/entitlement.ts";
import { asResponse } from "../_shared/http.ts";
import { bytesUsed, claimGeneration, generation, resetCopy, saveNotes, saveRecords } from "../_shared/sync_db.ts";
import { handle } from "./handler.ts";

/** The ceiling lives in the database (`20260912000100_sync.sql`'s `sync_ceiling_bytes()`, read
 * through the one-row `sync_limits` view), not as a constant here, so changing it is one migration
 * row and not a redeploy. Cached for the life of the isolate: it changes with a migration, never
 * within a request. The fallback is the migration's own number, so a view that has not been applied
 * yet refuses a runaway rather than letting one through. */
let ceiling: number | null = null;

Deno.serve(async (req) => {
  try {
    const rest = restFromEnv();
    return await handle(req, {
      requireEntitled: requireActiveEntitlement,
      bytesUsed: (id) => bytesUsed(rest, id),
      ceiling: async () => {
        if (ceiling === null) {
          const rows = await restSelect<{ ceiling: number }>(rest, "sync_limits", "select=ceiling&limit=1");
          ceiling = Number(rows[0]?.ceiling) || 209715200;
        }
        return ceiling;
      },
      saveRecords: (rows) => saveRecords(rest, rows),
      saveNotes: (rows) => saveNotes(rest, rows),
      resetCopy: (id) => resetCopy(rest, id),
      generation: (id) => generation(rest, id),
      claimGeneration: (id, fp, expected) => claimGeneration(rest, id, fp, expected),
    });
  } catch (e) {
    return asResponse(e);
  }
});
```

### Task 5: `GET /sync-pull`

The second endpoint, and the smaller one. Two cursors, one page, and a `more` flag so a device that has been away for a term catches up over several calls instead of asking for everything at once.

**Files:**
- Create: `cloud/supabase/functions/sync-pull/{handler.ts,handler_test.ts,index.ts}`
- **Hand-off this task needs:** **H10**, applied at Task 4 — nothing further here

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Task 4's `sync_db.ts` (`readRecords`, `readNotes`, `generation`, `READ_LAG_SECONDS`); Task 3's `isFingerprint`; C1's gate, `http.ts`.
- Produces: `GET /sync-pull?key_fingerprint=<8 hex>&records_after=<n>&notes_after=<n>&limit=<n>` → `200 {"records": [...], "notes": [...], "record_cursor": n, "note_cursor": m, "more": bool}`; **400** without a usable fingerprint; **409** `{"error", "key_fingerprint"}` when this machine's key is not the account's, reading nothing. 400 and the gate's 401/402 are **thrown**, so a handler test awaits the rejection; the 405 and the 409 are returned.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test** — `cloud/supabase/functions/sync-pull/handler_test.ts`:

```ts
import { assert, assertEquals } from "@std/assert";
import { handle, MAX_PAGE } from "./handler.ts";

const OK = () => Promise.resolve({ account_id: "acct-1" });
const IV = "AAAAAAAAAAAAAAAA";
const rec = (seq: number) => ({ seq, device: "0123456789abcdef", record_hash: seq.toString(16).padStart(64, "0"), iv: IV, ciphertext: "Zm9v" });
const note = (rev: number) => ({ rev, note_ref: rev.toString(16).padStart(64, "0"), device: "0123456789abcdef", deleted: false, iv: IV, ciphertext: "YmFy" });

const FP = "0a1b2c3d";
const OTHER_FP = "9f8e7d6c";

/** Every request in this file carries the account's own fingerprint unless it says otherwise. */
function pull(query: string) {
  const sep = query.startsWith("?") ? "&" : "?";
  return new Request(`http://127.0.0.1/sync-pull${query}${sep}key_fingerprint=${FP}`, {
    headers: { authorization: "Bearer t" },
  });
}

/** Every refusal in this codebase is a THROWN `Response` (`_shared/http.ts`'s `fail`, and
 * `requireActiveEntitlement`'s rejection); `index.ts`'s `asResponse` is what turns one into a reply.
 * A handler test therefore awaits the rejection, as C1's `telemetry/handler_test.ts:79` does. */
function refusal(req: Request, d: Parameters<typeof handle>[1]): Promise<Response> {
  return handle(req, d).catch((e) => e as Response);
}

/** A fixed clock, so the ten-second read lag is a value a test can see rather than a race. */
const NOW = new Date("2026-09-15T12:00:00.000Z");

/** The account's copy is sealed under `FP` unless a test says otherwise. */
const GEN = () => Promise.resolve(FP as string | null);

Deno.test("a page comes back in cursor order with the cursor it ends on", async () => {
  const res = await handle(pull("?records_after=0&notes_after=0"), {
    requireEntitled: OK,
    readRecords: () => Promise.resolve([rec(7), rec(9)]),
    readNotes: () => Promise.resolve([note(3)]),
    generation: GEN,
    now: () => NOW,
  });
  assertEquals(res.status, 200);
  const body = await res.json();
  assertEquals(body.records.map((r: { seq: number }) => r.seq), [7, 9]);
  assertEquals(body.record_cursor, 9);
  assertEquals(body.note_cursor, 3);
  assertEquals(body.more, false);
});

Deno.test("an empty page holds the cursors where they were", async () => {
  // The device must not rewind. A page with nothing in it answers with the cursor it was given, so
  // a slot that finds nothing new advances nothing and asks the same question next time.
  const res = await handle(pull("?records_after=41&notes_after=17"), {
    requireEntitled: OK,
    readRecords: () => Promise.resolve([]),
    readNotes: () => Promise.resolve([]),
    generation: GEN,
    now: () => NOW,
  });
  const body = await res.json();
  assertEquals([body.record_cursor, body.note_cursor, body.more], [41, 17, false]);
});

Deno.test("a full page says there is more", async () => {
  const many = Array.from({ length: MAX_PAGE }, (_, i) => rec(i + 1));
  const res = await handle(pull(`?records_after=0&notes_after=0&limit=${MAX_PAGE}`), {
    requireEntitled: OK,
    readRecords: () => Promise.resolve(many),
    readNotes: () => Promise.resolve([]),
    generation: GEN,
    now: () => NOW,
  });
  const body = await res.json();
  assertEquals(body.more, true);
  assertEquals(body.record_cursor, MAX_PAGE);
});

Deno.test("the caller's limit is clamped, never trusted", async () => {
  // PostgREST caps a select at `max_rows` (1000) whether or not the caller asked, so a limit above
  // the cap would silently produce a short page that reads as "no more" and strand every row past it.
  let asked = -1;
  await handle(pull("?limit=99999"), {
    requireEntitled: OK,
    readRecords: (_a, _b, limit) => { asked = limit; return Promise.resolve([]); },
    readNotes: () => Promise.resolve([]),
    generation: GEN,
    now: () => NOW,
  });
  assertEquals(asked, MAX_PAGE);
  let low = -1;
  await handle(pull("?limit=0"), {
    requireEntitled: OK,
    readRecords: (_a, _b, limit) => { low = limit; return Promise.resolve([]); },
    readNotes: () => Promise.resolve([]),
    generation: GEN,
    now: () => NOW,
  });
  assertEquals(low, MAX_PAGE);
});

Deno.test("a cursor that is not a number starts from the beginning rather than erroring", async () => {
  let asked = -1;
  await handle(pull("?records_after=yesterday"), {
    requireEntitled: OK,
    readRecords: (_a, after) => { asked = after; return Promise.resolve([]); },
    readNotes: () => Promise.resolve([]),
    generation: GEN,
    now: () => NOW,
  });
  assertEquals(asked, 0);
});

Deno.test("a restore is this endpoint from zero, and nothing else", async () => {
  // Exit gate item 6: there is no third function. A device with an empty vault asks the same
  // question with `records_after=0` and pages to the end.
  let asked = -1;
  await handle(pull("?records_after=0&notes_after=0"), {
    requireEntitled: OK,
    readRecords: (_a, after) => { asked = after; return Promise.resolve([]); },
    readNotes: () => Promise.resolve([]),
    generation: GEN,
    now: () => NOW,
  });
  assertEquals(asked, 0);
});

Deno.test("a device whose key is not the account's is a 409 and reads nothing", async () => {
  // The half that matters most: without it, a device left behind by another machine's re-key is
  // handed rows it cannot decrypt every slot for ever, and the only sentence it can produce reads
  // like data loss. `null` is not a refusal — an account with nothing stored has no generation.
  let read = 0;
  const stale = new Request(`http://127.0.0.1/sync-pull?records_after=0&key_fingerprint=${OTHER_FP}`, {
    headers: { authorization: "Bearer t" },
  });
  const res = await handle(stale, {
    requireEntitled: OK,
    readRecords: () => { read += 1; return Promise.resolve([]); },
    readNotes: () => { read += 1; return Promise.resolve([]); },
    generation: GEN,
    now: () => NOW,
  });
  assertEquals(res.status, 409);
  assertEquals((await res.json()).key_fingerprint, FP);
  assertEquals(read, 0);

  const fresh = await handle(pull("?records_after=0"), {
    requireEntitled: OK,
    readRecords: () => Promise.resolve([]),
    readNotes: () => Promise.resolve([]),
    generation: () => Promise.resolve(null),
    now: () => NOW,
  });
  assertEquals(fresh.status, 200, "an account with nothing stored yet is not a conflict");
});

Deno.test("a pull with no usable key fingerprint is a 400", async () => {
  const bare = new Request("http://127.0.0.1/sync-pull?records_after=0", { headers: { authorization: "Bearer t" } });
  const res = await refusal(bare, {
    requireEntitled: OK,
    readRecords: () => Promise.reject(new Error("must not read")),
    readNotes: () => Promise.reject(new Error("must not read")),
    generation: GEN,
    now: () => NOW,
  });
  assertEquals(res.status, 400);
});

Deno.test("anything but GET is a 405, and the gate answers first", async () => {
  // The 405 is RETURNED (`methodNotAllowed`), the 401 is THROWN (`requireActiveEntitlement`) — two
  // shapes, one reply, and the test has to await each the way it actually arrives.
  const res = await handle(new Request("http://127.0.0.1/sync-pull", { method: "POST" }), {
    requireEntitled: OK,
    readRecords: () => Promise.reject(new Error("must not read")),
    readNotes: () => Promise.reject(new Error("must not read")),
    generation: GEN,
    now: () => NOW,
  });
  assertEquals(res.status, 405);
  const gated = await refusal(pull(""), {
    requireEntitled: () => Promise.reject(new Response(JSON.stringify({ error: "the session is not valid" }), { status: 401 })),
    readRecords: () => Promise.reject(new Error("must not read")),
    readNotes: () => Promise.reject(new Error("must not read")),
    generation: GEN,
    now: () => NOW,
  });
  assertEquals(gated.status, 401);
});

Deno.test("the clock the read lag uses is the handler's, and it reaches the reader", async () => {
  // Review finding I3. `seq` is assigned at INSERT and visible at COMMIT, so two overlapping pushes
  // can commit out of order and a pull between the two commits would step the cursor past the row
  // that has not landed yet. The reader only ever sees rows that settled, and this is the wiring
  // that makes that testable rather than a comment.
  let seen: Date | null = null;
  await handle(pull("?records_after=0"), {
    requireEntitled: OK,
    readRecords: (_a, _b, _c, now) => { seen = now; return Promise.resolve([]); },
    readNotes: () => Promise.resolve([]),
    generation: GEN,
    now: () => NOW,
  });
  assertEquals(seen, NOW);
});
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write `cloud/supabase/functions/sync-pull/handler.ts`.**

```ts
/**
 * `GET /sync-pull` — what another device of this account wrote, sealed. Spec §5.5.
 *
 * **Two cursors, because there are two kinds of thing and they move at different rates:** records
 * are append-only and their cursor is an identity column; a note is current state and its cursor is
 * a sequence the table's own trigger stamps on every write, so a note whose text changed comes back
 * again with a higher `rev`.
 *
 * **A restore is this endpoint from zero.** There is no third function and no separate route: a
 * device with an empty vault asks `records_after=0&notes_after=0` and pages until `more` is false.
 *
 * Rows this device pushed itself come back too, and that is deliberate (precondition P7): the server
 * does not know which device is asking beyond what the bearer token says, and filtering here would
 * need it to. The device skips its own by content MAC, which also covers a re-install on the same
 * machine, where the device token is the same string.
 *
 * **Only settled rows are read** (`sync_db.ts`'s `READ_LAG_SECONDS`): a cursor assigned at INSERT is
 * visible at COMMIT, and two pushes that commit out of order would otherwise strand the lower one
 * behind a cursor the device has already saved.
 */
import { fail, json, methodNotAllowed } from "../_shared/http.ts";
import { NoteOut, RecordOut } from "../_shared/sync_db.ts";
import { isFingerprint } from "../_shared/sync_rows.ts";

/** PostgREST's own `max_rows` in `config.toml` is 1000; asking for more than it will give produces a
 * short page that reads as "no more" and strands everything past it. 500 leaves room and matches the
 * push batch, so a device that is behind catches up in whole round trips. */
export const MAX_PAGE = 500;

export interface Deps {
  requireEntitled: (req: Request) => Promise<{ account_id: string }>;
  readRecords: (accountId: string, after: number, limit: number, now: Date) => Promise<RecordOut[]>;
  readNotes: (accountId: string, after: number, limit: number, now: Date) => Promise<NoteOut[]>;
  /** Which key generation this account's copy is sealed under, or null if nothing is stored yet. */
  generation: (accountId: string) => Promise<string | null>;
  now: () => Date;
}

/** A query parameter as a non-negative integer. Anything else is 0 — a cursor that will not parse
 * means "start from the beginning", which costs one wasted page and never strands a row. */
function cursor(params: URLSearchParams, name: string): number {
  const raw = Number(params.get(name));
  return Number.isSafeInteger(raw) && raw > 0 ? raw : 0;
}

export async function handle(req: Request, deps: Deps): Promise<Response> {
  if (req.method !== "GET") return methodNotAllowed(["GET"]);
  const { account_id } = await deps.requireEntitled(req);
  const params = new URL(req.url).searchParams;
  const recordsAfter = cursor(params, "records_after");
  const notesAfter = cursor(params, "notes_after");
  // **The same generation gate as `/sync-push`, and the pull needs it more.** Without it a device
  // whose key is no longer the account's would be handed rows it cannot decrypt — every slot, for
  // ever — and the only thing it could say is "the copy could not be opened with this key", which
  // reads as data loss and is not. `null` is not a refusal: an account with nothing stored has no
  // generation yet, and a second device pulling from an empty copy is the ordinary first case.
  const fingerprint = params.get("key_fingerprint") ?? "";
  if (!isFingerprint(fingerprint)) throw fail(400, "key_fingerprint must be eight lower-case hex characters");
  const current = await deps.generation(account_id);
  if (current !== null && current !== fingerprint) {
    return json(409, {
      error: `this machine's key is not the one this account's copy is sealed under (the account's key is ${current})`,
      key_fingerprint: current,
    });
  }

  const asked = Number(params.get("limit"));
  const limit = Number.isSafeInteger(asked) && asked > 0 ? Math.min(asked, MAX_PAGE) : MAX_PAGE;

  // One clock for both reads, so the ten-second settle window is the same window on each side and a
  // page cannot straddle it.
  const now = deps.now();
  const [records, notes] = await Promise.all([
    deps.readRecords(account_id, recordsAfter, limit, now),
    deps.readNotes(account_id, notesAfter, limit, now),
  ]);

  return json(200, {
    records: records.map((r) => ({ seq: r.seq, device: r.device, hash: r.record_hash, iv: r.iv, ciphertext: r.ciphertext })),
    notes: notes.map((n) => ({ rev: n.rev, ref: n.note_ref, device: n.device, deleted: n.deleted, iv: n.iv, ciphertext: n.ciphertext })),
    // An empty page holds the cursor where it was: a device that finds nothing new advances nothing
    // and asks the same question next slot.
    record_cursor: records.length ? records[records.length - 1].seq : recordsAfter,
    note_cursor: notes.length ? notes[notes.length - 1].rev : notesAfter,
    more: records.length >= limit || notes.length >= limit,
  });
}
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Write `cloud/supabase/functions/sync-pull/index.ts`.**

```ts
import { restFromEnv } from "../_shared/db.ts";
import { requireActiveEntitlement } from "../_shared/entitlement.ts";
import { asResponse } from "../_shared/http.ts";
import { generation, readNotes, readRecords } from "../_shared/sync_db.ts";
import { handle } from "./handler.ts";

Deno.serve(async (req) => {
  try {
    const rest = restFromEnv();
    return await handle(req, {
      requireEntitled: requireActiveEntitlement,
      readRecords: (id, after, limit, now) => readRecords(rest, id, after, limit, now),
      readNotes: (id, after, limit, now) => readNotes(rest, id, after, limit, now),
      generation: (id) => generation(rest, id),
      now: () => new Date(),
    });
  } catch (e) {
    return asResponse(e);
  }
});
```

### Task 6: The cursor, and what this device sends

The push half of the device. Nothing here opens a socket except the one loopback test at the end; everything else is a pure function of the vault, the key and the cursor.

**Files:**
- Modify: `engine/src/sync.rs` (the cursor and push sections)
- Create: `engine/tests/sync_contract.rs`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Task 2's `SyncKey`, `SyncKey::index`, `IndexKey`, `seal`, `record_hash`, `note_ref`, `device_token`; C2's `cloudmodel::{CloudClient, CloudError}`; `journal::Journal`; `ids::NOTE_FOLDERS`; `ledger::dumps_value`; `pystr::{read_text, write_text}`; `provenance::is_agent`.
- Produces:

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing contract tests** — `engine/tests/sync_contract.rs`. The loopback harness is C2's, verbatim, because a second one would be a second thing to keep true:

```rust
//! What the engine actually sends the sync service, and what it does with every answer.
//!
//! Every test here binds a `TcpListener` to `127.0.0.1:0`, serves its own request from a second
//! thread, and joins that thread before returning. That is not egress: no DNS, no route off the
//! machine, no listener on a routable interface (`CLAUDE.md`; plan 3a's loopback rule). No test
//! reads a real vault, and none touches Credential Manager — `CloudClient::new` takes the token and
//! `build_push` takes the key, so both are exercised without one.
use std::io::{BufRead, BufReader, Read, Write};
use std::net::TcpListener;
use std::path::PathBuf;

use knowlu_engine::cloudmodel::{CloudClient, CloudConfig};
use knowlu_engine::journal::Journal;
use knowlu_engine::sync::{self, Cursor, SyncKey};

/// A fixed 32-byte key, base64. **Not a secret**: a test vector, protecting nothing.
const SYNC_TEST_KEY_NOT_A_SECRET: &str = "bm90LWEtc2VjcmV0LTMyLWJ5dGUtdGVzdC1rZXktISE=";

fn key() -> SyncKey {
    SyncKey::from_base64(SYNC_TEST_KEY_NOT_A_SECRET).expect("the test vector is 32 bytes")
}

struct Loopback {
    base: String,
    handle: Option<std::thread::JoinHandle<Vec<String>>>,
}

impl Loopback {
    fn requests(&mut self) -> Vec<String> {
        self.handle.take().expect("joined once").join().expect("the listener thread did not panic")
    }
}

fn read_request(stream: &std::net::TcpStream) -> String {
    let mut reader = BufReader::new(stream.try_clone().expect("clone the accepted stream"));
    let mut head = String::new();
    let mut length = 0usize;
    loop {
        let mut line = String::new();
        if reader.read_line(&mut line).unwrap_or(0) == 0 {
            break;
        }
        if let Some(rest) = line.to_ascii_lowercase().strip_prefix("content-length:") {
            length = rest.trim().parse().unwrap_or(0);
        }
        let blank = line == "\r\n" || line == "\n";
        head.push_str(&line);
        if blank {
            break;
        }
    }
    let mut body = vec![0u8; length];
    if length > 0 {
        let _ = reader.read_exact(&mut body);
    }
    format!("{head}{}", String::from_utf8_lossy(&body))
}

fn loopback(replies: Vec<(u16, String)>) -> Loopback {
    let listener = TcpListener::bind("127.0.0.1:0").expect("bind the loopback listener");
    let port = listener.local_addr().expect("the listener has an address").port();
    let handle = std::thread::spawn(move || {
        let mut seen = Vec::new();
        for (code, body) in replies {
            let Ok((mut stream, _)) = listener.accept() else { break };
            seen.push(read_request(&stream));
            let response = format!(
                "HTTP/1.1 {code} X\r\ncontent-type: application/json\r\ncontent-length: {}\r\nconnection: close\r\n\r\n{body}",
                body.len()
            );
            let _ = stream.write_all(response.as_bytes());
            let _ = stream.flush();
        }
        seen
    });
    Loopback { base: format!("http://127.0.0.1:{port}/functions/v1"), handle: Some(handle) }
}

fn config(base: &str) -> CloudConfig {
    CloudConfig {
        api_base: base.to_string(),
        anon_key: "anon-not-a-secret".to_string(),
        session_credential_target: "knowlu/test-profile/session".to_string(),
        account_id: "acct-1".to_string(),
    }
}

/// A throwaway vault with one task, one approval and one journal record. Never a fixture in place,
/// never the worktree (ruling R-3a-12).
fn scratch(tag: &str) -> PathBuf {
    let dir = std::env::temp_dir().join(format!(
        "knowlu-c3-{tag}-{}-{:?}",
        std::process::id(),
        std::thread::current().id()
    ));
    let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir);
    for folder in ["tasks", "approvals", "state/journal", "config"] {
        std::fs::create_dir_all(dir.join(folder)).expect("scratch vault");
    }
    std::fs::write(
        dir.join("tasks").join("cs-100-hw-01.md"),
        "---\ntype: task\nid: task_0000000001\nstatus: active\ntitle: CS 100 HW 01\nimportance: 3\n---\n\nbody\n",
    )
    .expect("a note");
    dir
}

#[test]
fn a_push_carries_ciphertext_and_no_path_no_title_and_no_token() {
    // The data-minimisation assertion, at the boundary that matters. If any of these ever appears in
    // a request body, the promise on the switch's own screen is false.
    let dir = scratch("push-shape");
    let mut journal = Journal::new(&dir);
    let ctx = knowlu_engine::write::WriteContext::new("quinn", "dashboard");
    knowlu_engine::write::write_literals(
        &dir,
        "tasks/cs-100-hw-01.md",
        &[("importance".to_string(), "5".to_string())],
        &ctx,
        &mut journal,
        &Default::default(),
    )
    .expect("one write");

    let mut server = loopback(vec![(200, r#"{"records":1,"notes":1,"bytes_used":10,"bytes_ceiling":209715200}"#.to_string())]);
    let client = CloudClient::new(&config(&server.base), "jwt-not-a-secret");
    let (batch, _next) = sync::build_push(&dir, &key(), &Cursor::default(), "acct-1", &mut journal);
    assert!(!batch.records.is_empty() && !batch.notes.is_empty(), "{batch:?}");
    sync::push(&client, &batch).expect("the service answered");

    let sent = server.requests().remove(0);
    assert!(sent.starts_with("POST /functions/v1/sync-push HTTP/1.1"), "{sent}");
    // **Lower case.** `ureq` normalises a header name, whatever case was passed to `.header(...)`,
    // so the request line on the wire reads `authorization:`. This checks the actual bytes sent, not
    // the call-site spelling — C2's own contract test learned the same thing
    // (`engine/tests/cloud_contract.rs`, the comment above its bearer assertion).
    assert!(sent.contains("authorization: Bearer jwt-not-a-secret"));
    for forbidden in [
        "cs-100-hw-01",     // the path
        "CS 100 HW 01",     // the title
        "importance",       // a field name
        "task_0000000001",  // the note's id
        "tasks/",           // any path at all
        "knowlu/test-profile", // the credential target
        SYNC_TEST_KEY_NOT_A_SECRET,
    ] {
        assert!(!sent.contains(forbidden), "the push body carried {forbidden:?}:\n{sent}");
    }
    assert!(sent.contains("\"ciphertext\""));
    assert!(sent.contains("\"device\""));
    let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir);
}

#[test]
fn the_cursor_only_moves_after_the_service_took_the_batch() {
    // A push that fails must not advance anything: the next slot has to send the same rows again.
    let dir = scratch("cursor");
    let mut journal = Journal::new(&dir);
    let before = sync::load_cursor(&dir);
    let (batch, next) = sync::build_push(&dir, &key(), &before, "acct-1", &mut journal);
    assert!(!batch.notes.is_empty());
    assert_ne!(next, before, "the proposed cursor moved");
    // Nothing was saved by `build_push` itself.
    assert_eq!(sync::load_cursor(&dir), before, "build_push must not write the cursor");
    sync::save_cursor(&dir, &next).expect("save");
    assert_eq!(sync::load_cursor(&dir), next);
    // A second build against the saved cursor has nothing to send.
    let (again, _) = sync::build_push(&dir, &key(), &next, "acct-1", &mut journal);
    assert!(again.records.is_empty() && again.notes.is_empty(), "{again:?}");
    let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir);
}

#[test]
fn a_note_that_is_gone_is_pushed_as_a_tombstone_and_then_forgotten() {
    let dir = scratch("tombstone");
    let mut journal = Journal::new(&dir);
    let (_, after_first) = sync::build_push(&dir, &key(), &Cursor::default(), "acct-1", &mut journal);
    sync::save_cursor(&dir, &after_first).expect("save");
    std::fs::remove_file(dir.join("tasks").join("cs-100-hw-01.md")).expect("the note moves away");
    let (batch, next) = sync::build_push(&dir, &key(), &after_first, "acct-1", &mut journal);
    assert_eq!(batch.notes.len(), 1, "{batch:?}");
    assert_eq!(batch.notes[0]["deleted"], serde_json::Value::Bool(true));
    assert!(batch.notes[0].get("ciphertext").is_none(), "a tombstone carries no bytes");
    assert!(!next.notes.contains_key("tasks/cs-100-hw-01.md"), "and the path leaves the cursor");
    let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir);
}

#[test]
fn a_note_too_large_to_seal_is_named_and_left_alone() {
    let dir = scratch("huge");
    let mut journal = Journal::new(&dir);
    let big = format!("---\ntype: task\nid: task_0000000002\nstatus: active\ntitle: big\n---\n\n{}\n", "x".repeat(sync::MAX_NOTE_BYTES + 1));
    std::fs::write(dir.join("tasks").join("big.md"), &big).expect("a big note");
    let (batch, next) = sync::build_push(&dir, &key(), &Cursor::default(), "acct-1", &mut journal);
    assert!(batch.warnings.iter().any(|w| w.contains("big.md") && w.contains("too large")), "{:?}", batch.warnings);
    assert!(!next.notes.contains_key("tasks/big.md"), "an unsent note must not be recorded as sent");
    let index = key().index();
    assert!(batch.notes.iter().all(|n| n["ref"] != serde_json::Value::String(sync::note_ref(&index, "tasks/big.md"))));
    let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir);
}

#[test]
fn a_new_key_re_uploads_everything_and_says_so_once() {
    // Precondition P5. The student turned the switch off and on again, so this device has a key the
    // account's rows were not sealed under. The cursor remembers which key it was built under, and a
    // mismatch throws the whole cursor away: everything is offered again, and `reset` tells the
    // server to drop the rows nobody can open.
    let dir = scratch("rekey");
    let mut journal = Journal::new(&dir);
    let (_, after_first) = sync::build_push(&dir, &key(), &Cursor::default(), "acct-1", &mut journal);
    sync::save_cursor(&dir, &after_first).expect("save");
    assert_eq!(after_first.key_fingerprint, key().fingerprint());

    let (again, _) = sync::build_push(&dir, &key(), &after_first, "acct-1", &mut journal);
    assert!(again.notes.is_empty() && !again.reset, "the same key offers nothing twice: {again:?}");

    let fresh = SyncKey::from_bytes([11u8; 32]);
    let (rekeyed, next) = sync::build_push(&dir, &fresh, &after_first, "acct-1", &mut journal);
    assert!(rekeyed.reset, "a changed key asks the server to empty the old copy");
    assert!(!rekeyed.notes.is_empty(), "and re-offers every note: {rekeyed:?}");
    assert_eq!(next.key_fingerprint, fresh.fingerprint());
    // The device token is keyed too, so it changes with the key — which is correct: this is, for
    // every purpose the server has, a different device.
    assert_ne!(rekeyed.device, again.device);
    let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir);
}

#[test]
fn a_record_a_human_wrote_is_marked_to_be_kept_and_an_agents_is_not() {
    // The one cleartext bit, and the reason it exists: `sync_prune` never deletes a `keep` row, so a
    // restored machine still has the records `journal::human_set` reads (precondition P3).
    let dir = scratch("keepbit");
    let mut journal = Journal::new(&dir);
    let mine = knowlu_engine::write::WriteContext::new("quinn", "dashboard");
    knowlu_engine::write::write_literals(&dir, "tasks/cs-100-hw-01.md", &[("importance".to_string(), "5".to_string())], &mine, &mut journal, &Default::default()).expect("a human write");
    let theirs = knowlu_engine::write::WriteContext::new("agent:knowlu.enrich", "local-runner");
    knowlu_engine::write::write_literals(&dir, "tasks/cs-100-hw-01.md", &[("effort_hours".to_string(), "2.0".to_string())], &theirs, &mut journal, &Default::default()).expect("an agent write");

    let (batch, _) = sync::build_push(&dir, &key(), &Cursor::default(), "acct-1", &mut journal);
    let kept = batch.records.iter().filter(|r| r["keep"] == serde_json::Value::Bool(true)).count();
    let loose = batch.records.iter().filter(|r| r["keep"] == serde_json::Value::Bool(false)).count();
    assert!(kept >= 1, "the human set is marked: {batch:?}");
    assert!(loose >= 1, "the agent's is not: {batch:?}");
    let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir);
}

#[test]
fn a_402_is_a_refusal_the_caller_can_name_and_not_a_panic() {
    let dir = scratch("402");
    let mut journal = Journal::new(&dir);
    let mut server = loopback(vec![(402, r#"{"error":"this account has no active subscription"}"#.to_string())]);
    let client = CloudClient::new(&config(&server.base), "jwt-not-a-secret");
    let (batch, _) = sync::build_push(&dir, &key(), &Cursor::default(), "acct-1", &mut journal);
    let err = sync::push(&client, &batch).expect_err("a 402 is an error");
    assert_eq!(err.label(), "no entitlement");
    let _ = server.requests();
    let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir);
}

#[test]
fn the_key_never_reaches_an_error_string() {
    // A transport error's text is a third party's, and `state/runner-log.md` is written from it.
    let listener = TcpListener::bind("127.0.0.1:0").expect("bind");
    let port = listener.local_addr().expect("addr").port();
    drop(listener);
    let dir = scratch("noport");
    let mut journal = Journal::new(&dir);
    let client = CloudClient::new(&config(&format!("http://127.0.0.1:{port}/functions/v1")), "jwt-super-secret");
    let (batch, _) = sync::build_push(&dir, &key(), &Cursor::default(), "acct-1", &mut journal);
    let err = sync::push(&client, &batch).expect_err("nothing is listening");
    let text = err.to_string();
    assert!(!text.contains("jwt-super-secret"), "{text}");
    assert!(!text.contains(SYNC_TEST_KEY_NOT_A_SECRET), "{text}");
    let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir);
}

#[test]
fn a_vault_with_no_state_folder_is_a_cursor_of_zeros_and_not_an_error() {
    let dir = std::env::temp_dir().join(format!("knowlu-c3-bare-{}", std::process::id()));
    let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir);
    std::fs::create_dir_all(&dir).expect("bare dir");
    assert_eq!(sync::load_cursor(&dir), Cursor::default());
    let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir);
}

#[test]
fn the_cursor_file_is_one_line_of_the_crates_own_json() {
    let dir = scratch("cursorfile");
    let mut c = Cursor::default();
    c.record_cursor = 41;
    c.notes.insert("tasks/b.md".into(), "b".repeat(64));
    c.notes.insert("tasks/a.md".into(), "a".repeat(64));
    sync::save_cursor(&dir, &c).expect("save");
    let text = knowlu_engine::pystr::read_text(&dir.join("state").join(sync::CURSOR_FILE)).expect("read");
    // `ledger::dumps_value`'s separators and sorted keys, like every other JSON this crate writes.
    assert!(text.contains("\"record_cursor\": 41"), "{text}");
    assert!(text.find("tasks/a.md") < text.find("tasks/b.md"), "keys are sorted: {text}");
    assert_eq!(text.lines().count(), 1, "one line: {text}");
    let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir);
}
```
