- [ ] **Step 3: Write the cursor section** of `engine/src/sync.rs`:

```rust
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// The cursor
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// How many rows of each kind travel in one call. The server's own batch cap is the same number, so
/// a device that has been away for a term catches up in whole round trips.
pub const PAGE: usize = 500;

/// `state/sync-cursor.json` — this device's place in the account's copy.
///
/// **In `state/`, and device-local.** `state/` is generated and is not itself synced (only the
/// records under `state/journal/` travel, and they travel sealed), so two devices each keep their
/// own and neither can overwrite the other's. A restored vault carries a stale cursor, which is
/// exactly the right failure: the device re-pulls from an old point, recognises every record by
/// hash, and applies nothing twice.
///
/// It is **not** a judgment log (`CLAUDE.md` §5.6): it holds two integers, a timestamp and a map of
/// note path to content hash, every one of which is already in the vault in plainer form.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct Cursor {
    /// The highest `sync_records.seq` this device has applied.
    pub record_cursor: i64,
    /// The highest `sync_notes.rev` this device has applied.
    pub note_cursor: i64,
    /// The journal `ts` this device has pushed through, inclusive of `boundary`.
    pub pushed_through: String,
    /// The hashes of the records carrying exactly `pushed_through`. **The tie is why this exists**:
    /// a watermark on `ts` alone either re-sends a millisecond's worth of records forever or strands
    /// the ones past the page's cut. This set is only ever as large as one millisecond's traffic.
    pub boundary: Vec<String>,
    /// Vault-relative path → the SHA-256 of the bytes last pushed for it. A path in here whose file
    /// is gone is a tombstone waiting to go up.
    ///
    /// **Plaintext paths, in a vault file.** They are the names of files sitting in the same folder,
    /// so nothing is disclosed that a `dir` would not; it is named here so nobody later assumes this
    /// file is sealed.
    pub notes: BTreeMap<String, String>,
    /// The fingerprint of the key every value above was computed under.
    ///
    /// **This is what makes turning the switch off and on again safe** (precondition P5). Without it
    /// the digest map would still say every unchanged note had been pushed — under the OLD key — and
    /// the new copy would be silently missing every note that had not changed since, which a later
    /// restore would present as a complete vault. A mismatch resets the whole cursor.
    pub key_fingerprint: String,
}

pub const CURSOR_FILE: &str = "sync-cursor.json";

fn cursor_path(vault: &Path) -> PathBuf {
    vault.join("state").join(CURSOR_FILE)
}

/// A missing, unreadable or half-written cursor is `Cursor::default()`. **Never an error**: the
/// worst a lost cursor costs is one wasted page and a re-push the server deduplicates, and a vault
/// that cannot sync because a generated file did not parse would be a vault held hostage by
/// bookkeeping.
pub fn load_cursor(vault: &Path) -> Cursor {
    let Ok(text) = crate::pystr::read_text(&cursor_path(vault)) else { return Cursor::default() };
    let Ok(v) = serde_json::from_str::<Value>(&text) else { return Cursor::default() };
    let n = |k: &str| v.get(k).and_then(Value::as_i64).unwrap_or(0);
    let s = |k: &str| v.get(k).and_then(Value::as_str).unwrap_or("").to_string();
    let list = |k: &str| {
        v.get(k)
            .and_then(Value::as_array)
            .map(|a| a.iter().filter_map(Value::as_str).map(str::to_string).collect())
            .unwrap_or_default()
    };
    let notes = v
        .get("notes")
        .and_then(Value::as_object)
        .map(|m| m.iter().filter_map(|(k, v)| v.as_str().map(|s| (k.clone(), s.to_string()))).collect())
        .unwrap_or_default();
    Cursor {
        record_cursor: n("record_cursor"),
        note_cursor: n("note_cursor"),
        pushed_through: s("pushed_through"),
        boundary: list("boundary"),
        notes,
        key_fingerprint: s("key_fingerprint"),
    }
}

pub fn save_cursor(vault: &Path, cursor: &Cursor) -> Result<(), SyncError> {
    let value = serde_json::json!({
        "record_cursor": cursor.record_cursor,
        "note_cursor": cursor.note_cursor,
        "pushed_through": cursor.pushed_through,
        "boundary": cursor.boundary,
        "key_fingerprint": cursor.key_fingerprint,
        "notes": cursor.notes.iter().map(|(k, v)| (k.clone(), Value::String(v.clone()))).collect::<serde_json::Map<_, _>>(),
    });
    let dir = vault.join("state");
    std::fs::create_dir_all(&dir).map_err(|e| SyncError::Io(e.to_string()))?;
    // `dumps_value`, like every other JSON this crate writes: a cursor written by the engine and one
    // written by the app are the same bytes.
    crate::pystr::write_text(&cursor_path(vault), &crate::ledger::dumps_value(&value))
        .map_err(|e| SyncError::Io(e.to_string()))
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// The push
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// Every note in the vault, as vault-relative POSIX paths, sorted. A folder that does not exist is
/// not an error — `vault-full` has no `archive/` and no `courses/`, and neither does a new vault.
pub fn note_paths(vault: &Path) -> Vec<String> {
    let mut out = Vec::new();
    for folder in crate::ids::NOTE_FOLDERS {
        let Ok(entries) = std::fs::read_dir(vault.join(folder)) else { continue };
        for entry in entries.flatten() {
            let path = entry.path();
            if path.extension().map(|x| x == "md") == Some(true) {
                if let Some(name) = path.file_name().and_then(|n| n.to_str()) {
                    out.push(format!("{folder}/{name}"));
                }
            }
        }
    }
    out.sort();
    out
}

/// Has this machine's key changed since the cursor was written?
///
/// **One predicate, two callers**, because they must agree: `build_push` uses it to throw the cursor
/// away and set `reset`, and `run_lines_with` uses it to skip the pull (there is nothing worth
/// pulling when the copy is about to be replaced, and pulling would fetch rows sealed under the old
/// key). An empty `key_fingerprint` is a cursor written before this field existed, or a first run —
/// neither is a re-key.
pub fn is_rekeyed(cursor: &Cursor, key: &SyncKey) -> bool {
    !cursor.key_fingerprint.is_empty() && cursor.key_fingerprint != key.fingerprint()
}

/// What one call to `/sync-push` carries. `warnings` are lines the caller prints — a note too large
/// to seal, a record too large to seal — never a reason to fail the run.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default, PartialEq)]
pub struct PushBatch {
    pub device: String,
    /// The key generation this batch is sealed under. **Not a secret** — eight hex characters of a
    /// SHA-256 — and the server compares it against the account's so a device left behind by another
    /// machine's re-key is told rather than quietly ignored.
    pub key_fingerprint: String,
    pub records: Vec<Value>,
    pub notes: Vec<Value>,
    /// Empty the account's copy before storing this batch (precondition P5). Set exactly when the
    /// cursor was built under a different key, which is what an off-then-on looks like from here.
    pub reset: bool,
    pub warnings: Vec<String>,
}

/// Build one batch, and the cursor that becomes true **only once the service has taken it**. This
/// function writes nothing: the caller saves the returned cursor after a 200 and throws it away
/// after anything else, which is what makes a failed push cost nothing but a retry.
pub fn build_push(
    vault: &Path,
    key: &SyncKey,
    cursor: &Cursor,
    account_id: &str,
    journal: &mut Journal,
) -> (PushBatch, Cursor) {
    let index = key.index();
    let fingerprint = key.fingerprint();
    // **A key that is not the one this cursor was built under throws the cursor away** (P5). The
    // digest map, the `pushed_through` watermark and both pull cursors were all computed under a key
    // nobody holds any more; keeping any of them would produce a new copy that is silently missing
    // everything unchanged since the switch was last on.
    let rekeyed = is_rekeyed(cursor, key);
    let cursor = if rekeyed { &Cursor::default() } else { cursor };
    let mut batch = PushBatch {
        device: device_token(&index, account_id),
        key_fingerprint: fingerprint.clone(),
        reset: rekeyed,
        ..Default::default()
    };
    if rekeyed {
        batch.warnings.push("sync: the cloud copy is being made again from the start under this machine's new key".to_string());
    }
    let mut next = cursor.clone();
    next.key_fingerprint = fingerprint;

    // ---- records ----
    let already: std::collections::BTreeSet<&str> = cursor.boundary.iter().map(String::as_str).collect();
    let mut kept: Vec<(String, String)> = Vec::new(); // (ts, hash), for the new boundary
    for record in journal.read(None, None) {
        let ts = record.get("ts").and_then(Value::as_str).unwrap_or("").to_string();
        if ts.as_str() < cursor.pushed_through.as_str() {
            continue;
        }
        let hash = record_hash(&index, &record);
        if ts == cursor.pushed_through && already.contains(hash.as_str()) {
            continue;
        }
        if batch.records.len() >= PAGE {
            break;
        }
        let plaintext = crate::ledger::dumps_value(&Value::Object(record.clone()));
        if plaintext.len() > MAX_RECORD_BYTES {
            batch.warnings.push(format!("sync: a journal record at {ts} is too large to send ({} bytes); left in place", plaintext.len()));
            continue;
        }
        // **The one cleartext bit about a record** (precondition P3): a record a human wrote is
        // never pruned, because `journal::human_set` is what judge-once reads and it reads records.
        // `provenance::is_agent` is a `starts_with("agent:")` test, and it is the one this crate
        // already uses everywhere else to answer the same question.
        let op = record.get("op").and_then(Value::as_str).unwrap_or("");
        let human = !crate::provenance::is_agent(record.get("actor").and_then(Value::as_str).unwrap_or(""));
        let keep = human && (op == "set" || op == "create");
        match seal(key, plaintext.as_bytes()) {
            Ok((iv, ciphertext)) => {
                batch.records.push(serde_json::json!({ "hash": hash, "iv": iv, "ciphertext": ciphertext, "keep": keep }));
                kept.push((ts, hash));
            }
            Err(e) => batch.warnings.push(format!("sync: a journal record could not be sealed ({e})")),
        }
    }
    if let Some((last_ts, _)) = kept.last().cloned() {
        next.boundary = kept.iter().filter(|(ts, _)| *ts == last_ts).map(|(_, h)| h.clone()).collect();
        // The boundary carries what has already gone at this exact millisecond, so the same records
        // are not offered again — and the ones past the page's cut still are.
        if cursor.pushed_through == last_ts {
            next.boundary.extend(cursor.boundary.iter().cloned());
            next.boundary.sort();
            next.boundary.dedup();
        }
        next.pushed_through = last_ts;
    }

    // ---- notes ----
    let present = note_paths(vault);
    for rel in &present {
        if batch.notes.len() >= PAGE {
            break;
        }
        let Ok(text) = crate::pystr::read_text(&vault.join(rel)) else {
            batch.warnings.push(format!("sync: {rel} could not be read; left in place"));
            continue;
        };
        if text.len() > MAX_NOTE_BYTES {
            batch.warnings.push(format!("sync: {rel} is too large to send ({} bytes); left in place", text.len()));
            continue;
        }
        let digest = sha256_hex(text.as_bytes());
        if cursor.notes.get(rel).map(String::as_str) == Some(digest.as_str()) {
            continue;
        }
        // The PATH travels inside the envelope, beside the text, because a restore needs it and
        // nothing else does; the row's `ref` is only its hash.
        let plaintext = crate::ledger::dumps_value(&serde_json::json!({ "path": rel, "text": text }));
        match seal(key, plaintext.as_bytes()) {
            Ok((iv, ciphertext)) => {
                batch.notes.push(serde_json::json!({ "ref": note_ref(&index, rel), "iv": iv, "ciphertext": ciphertext }));
                next.notes.insert(rel.clone(), digest);
            }
            Err(e) => batch.warnings.push(format!("sync: {rel} could not be sealed ({e})")),
        }
    }
    // A path the cursor remembers whose file is gone — settled into `archive/`, renamed, deleted by
    // hand — goes up as a tombstone so a restore does not resurrect it, and then leaves the cursor.
    let gone: Vec<String> = cursor.notes.keys().filter(|p| !present.contains(p)).cloned().collect();
    for rel in gone {
        if batch.notes.len() >= PAGE {
            break;
        }
        batch.notes.push(serde_json::json!({ "ref": note_ref(&index, &rel), "deleted": true }));
        next.notes.remove(&rel);
    }

    (batch, next)
}

/// **409 is the account's key generation moving on without this device.** Recognised here rather
/// than by extending C2's `CloudError::label()` — `cloudmodel.rs` is C2's file and this is C3's
/// meaning for one status, not a new word in C2's closed set.
///
/// The `String` is the server's own `error` sentence, which is already clipped to 200 characters and
/// scrubbed of the bearer by `CloudClient::finish`, and which names the account's current
/// fingerprint. **Nothing is parsed out of it**: the device does not need the fingerprint to act —
/// the student compares it on the machine that has the key — and a parser over a sentence is a thing
/// that breaks when the sentence is improved.
pub fn stale_key(error: &crate::cloudmodel::CloudError) -> Option<String> {
    match error {
        crate::cloudmodel::CloudError::Status { code: 409, detail } => Some(detail.clone()),
        _ => None,
    }
}

/// One call. `(records stored, notes stored)` on success; every failure is C2's `CloudError`, whose
/// `label()` is the word the step prints — `no session`, `no entitlement`, `no network` — except a
/// **409**, which is [`stale_key`] and which the caller turns into a named skip and a card.
pub fn push(client: &crate::cloudmodel::CloudClient, batch: &PushBatch) -> Result<(usize, usize), crate::cloudmodel::CloudError> {
    // A `reset` with nothing to send still has to go: it is how the server learns the old copy is
    // unopenable. Only a batch that is empty in every sense is skipped.
    if batch.records.is_empty() && batch.notes.is_empty() && !batch.reset {
        return Ok((0, 0));
    }
    let body = serde_json::json!({
        "device": batch.device,
        "key_fingerprint": batch.key_fingerprint,
        "reset": batch.reset,
        "records": batch.records,
        "notes": batch.notes,
    });
    let reply = client.post("/sync-push", &body)?;
    Ok((
        reply.get("records").and_then(Value::as_u64).unwrap_or(0) as usize,
        reply.get("notes").and_then(Value::as_u64).unwrap_or(0) as usize,
    ))
}
```

### Task 7: The pull, `reconcile`, and the amend card

The half the whole feature is for. A record another device wrote is appended to this device's journal **verbatim**, and the note-level effect is settled by the pure `reconcile::resolve` that has been sitting unused since plan 2 — with the roles reversed, which is the one subtle thing in this task and is spelled out twice below.

**Files:**
- Modify: `engine/src/sync.rs` (the pull and apply sections)
- Test: `engine/tests/sync_contract.rs` (twelve new cases)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `reconcile::resolve`, `write::{write, create, delete, move_note, propose_amendment, find_pending_amendment, WriteContext, WriteOpts}`, `approvals::{AMENDABLE_FIELDS, AMENDABLE_FOLDERS}`, `models::split_frontmatter`, `ids::{read_meta, inside_vault, is_id, NOTE_FOLDERS}`, `journal::{Journal, OPS, VIAS}`, Task 2's `IndexKey`.
- Produces:
  - `sync::ACTOR: &str` = `"agent:knowlu.sync"`
  - `sync::PulledNote { device: String, path: String, text: Option<String> }`
  - `sync::Pulled { records: Vec<(String, Record)>, notes: Vec<PulledNote>, record_cursor: i64, note_cursor: i64, more: bool, warnings: Vec<String> }`
  - `sync::pull(&CloudClient, &SyncKey, i64, i64) -> Result<Pulled, SyncError>`
  - `sync::ApplyReport { records: usize, notes_written: usize, applied: usize, cards: usize, superseded: usize, moved: usize, refused: usize, warnings: Vec<String> }`
  - `sync::record_is_well_formed(&Record) -> Result<(), &'static str>`
  - `sync::apply(&Path, &IndexKey, &Pulled, &WriteContext, &mut Journal, jiff::civil::Date) -> ApplyReport`

**The role reversal, stated once and repeated in the code.** `reconcile::resolve` was written for git, where `checkout --ours` had already put *upstream's* file on disk and the job was to re-apply *this* device's edits onto it. Here the file on disk is **this device's**. So the arguments are handed over the other way round:

| `resolve`'s parameter | git's world | C3's world |
|---|---|---|
| `upstream_meta` | the file after taking upstream | **the local note as it stands on disk** |
| `upstream_records` | the other device's records | **this device's own records since the base** |
| `local_records` | this device's records | **the foreign records that just came down** |
| `upstream_mtime_ts` | upstream's commit time | **the local file's mtime, as a journal `ts`** |

Read the function with that substitution and it does exactly the right thing: `chains` becomes the set of fields the *other* device changed; a field whose on-disk value equals that chain's first `old` means this device never touched it, so the foreign value applies cleanly and **no supersede record is produced**; anything else is a two-sided change, `wins()` decides by later `ts`, and a supersede record names the loser. **A field with a supersede record is this plan's definition of a conflict**, and that is what becomes a card.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests** — **twelve** cases appended to `engine/tests/sync_contract.rs`:

```rust
/// Build a foreign `set` record: another device, another `seq`, a later `ts`.
fn foreign_set(id: &str, path: &str, field: &str, old: serde_json::Value, new: serde_json::Value, ts: &str) -> knowlu_engine::ledger::Record {
    let mut spec = knowlu_engine::journal::NewRecord::new("set", path, "quinn", "dashboard");
    spec.id = Some(id);
    spec.field = Some(field);
    spec.old = old;
    spec.new = new;
    spec.ts = Some(ts.to_string());
    spec.device = Some("OtherDevice".to_string());
    let mut rec = knowlu_engine::journal::make_record(spec).expect("a record");
    rec.insert("seq".into(), serde_json::Value::from(9001));
    rec
}

/// The index key every `apply` call in this file uses. Derived once, like production does.
fn index() -> knowlu_engine::sync::IndexKey {
    key().index()
}

fn pulled(records: Vec<knowlu_engine::ledger::Record>, notes: Vec<sync::PulledNote>) -> sync::Pulled {
    sync::Pulled {
        records: records.into_iter().map(|r| ("fedcba9876543210".to_string(), r)).collect(),
        notes,
        record_cursor: 1,
        note_cursor: 1,
        more: false,
        warnings: Vec::new(),
    }
}

#[test]
fn a_field_only_the_other_device_touched_applies_cleanly_and_files_no_card() {
    let dir = scratch("clean");
    let mut journal = Journal::new(&dir);
    let ctx = knowlu_engine::write::WriteContext::new(sync::ACTOR, "local-runner");
    let rec = foreign_set("task_0000000001", "tasks/cs-100-hw-01.md", "importance", serde_json::json!(3), serde_json::json!(5), "2026-09-14T10:00:00.000Z");
    let report = sync::apply(&dir, &index(), &pulled(vec![rec], vec![]), &ctx, &mut journal, "2026-09-14".parse().unwrap());
    assert_eq!((report.applied, report.cards, report.superseded), (1, 0, 0), "{report:?}");
    let meta = knowlu_engine::ids::read_meta(&dir.join("tasks").join("cs-100-hw-01.md")).expect("the note");
    assert_eq!(knowlu_engine::yaml::get(&meta, "importance").and_then(knowlu_engine::yaml::i64_of), Some(5));
    let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir);
}

#[test]
fn a_foreign_record_is_appended_verbatim_with_its_own_device_and_ts() {
    // The whole reason a restore and a pull do not go through `write`: these records already exist,
    // and rewriting their `ts` or their `device` would destroy the attribution judge-once reads.
    let dir = scratch("verbatim");
    let mut journal = Journal::new(&dir);
    let ctx = knowlu_engine::write::WriteContext::new(sync::ACTOR, "local-runner");
    let rec = foreign_set("task_0000000001", "tasks/cs-100-hw-01.md", "importance", serde_json::json!(3), serde_json::json!(5), "2026-09-14T10:00:00.000Z");
    sync::apply(&dir, &index(), &pulled(vec![rec], vec![]), &ctx, &mut journal, "2026-09-14".parse().unwrap());
    journal.invalidate();
    let kept = journal.read(None, None).into_iter().find(|r| r.get("device").and_then(|v| v.as_str()) == Some("OtherDevice")).expect("the foreign record");
    assert_eq!(kept.get("ts").and_then(|v| v.as_str()), Some("2026-09-14T10:00:00.000Z"));
    assert_eq!(kept.get("seq").and_then(|v| v.as_i64()), Some(9001));
    assert_eq!(kept.get("actor").and_then(|v| v.as_str()), Some("quinn"));
    let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir);
}

#[test]
fn a_record_this_device_already_has_is_not_applied_twice() {
    let dir = scratch("dedupe");
    let mut journal = Journal::new(&dir);
    let ctx = knowlu_engine::write::WriteContext::new(sync::ACTOR, "local-runner");
    let rec = foreign_set("task_0000000001", "tasks/cs-100-hw-01.md", "importance", serde_json::json!(3), serde_json::json!(5), "2026-09-14T10:00:00.000Z");
    let first = sync::apply(&dir, &index(), &pulled(vec![rec.clone()], vec![]), &ctx, &mut journal, "2026-09-14".parse().unwrap());
    assert_eq!(first.records, 1);
    let second = sync::apply(&dir, &index(), &pulled(vec![rec], vec![]), &ctx, &mut journal, "2026-09-14".parse().unwrap());
    assert_eq!((second.records, second.applied, second.cards), (0, 0, 0), "{second:?}");
    let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir);
}

#[test]
fn a_field_both_devices_moved_becomes_one_amend_card_and_not_a_silent_merge() {
    // VISION, and §5.5's own sentence. The card is the ordinary `kind: amend` card the deck already
    // answers, through the ordinary `propose_amendment` path, so the fifteen-a-day cap applies.
    let dir = scratch("conflict");
    let mut journal = Journal::new(&dir);
    let mine = knowlu_engine::write::WriteContext::new("quinn", "dashboard");
    knowlu_engine::write::write_literals(&dir, "tasks/cs-100-hw-01.md", &[("importance".to_string(), "4".to_string())], &mine, &mut journal, &Default::default()).expect("my edit");
    let ctx = knowlu_engine::write::WriteContext::new(sync::ACTOR, "local-runner");
    let rec = foreign_set("task_0000000001", "tasks/cs-100-hw-01.md", "importance", serde_json::json!(3), serde_json::json!(5), "2036-09-14T10:00:00.000Z");
    let report = sync::apply(&dir, &index(), &pulled(vec![rec], vec![]), &ctx, &mut journal, "2026-09-14".parse().unwrap());
    assert_eq!(report.cards, 1, "{report:?}");
    // The note keeps what this device had until the card is answered: never a silent merge.
    let meta = knowlu_engine::ids::read_meta(&dir.join("tasks").join("cs-100-hw-01.md")).expect("the note");
    assert_eq!(knowlu_engine::yaml::get(&meta, "importance").and_then(knowlu_engine::yaml::i64_of), Some(4));
    let card = std::fs::read_dir(dir.join("approvals")).expect("approvals").flatten()
        .map(|e| e.path()).find(|p| p.file_name().map(|n| n.to_string_lossy().starts_with("amend-")).unwrap_or(false))
        .expect("one amend card");
    let text = knowlu_engine::pystr::read_text(&card).expect("the card");
    assert!(text.contains("kind: amend"), "{text}");
    assert!(text.contains("from: 4") && text.contains("to: 5"), "{text}");
    // A second pull of the same record must not mint a second card for the same field set.
    let again = foreign_set("task_0000000001", "tasks/cs-100-hw-01.md", "importance", serde_json::json!(3), serde_json::json!(6), "2036-09-14T11:00:00.000Z");
    let second = sync::apply(&dir, &index(), &pulled(vec![again], vec![]), &ctx, &mut journal, "2026-09-14".parse().unwrap());
    assert_eq!(second.cards, 0, "an identical pending proposal already IS the re-proposal");
    let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir);
}

#[test]
fn a_conflict_this_device_won_writes_nothing_and_files_no_card() {
    // `reconcile::resolve` fills `apply` only when the side passed as `local_records` wins, and with
    // the roles reversed that side is the foreign one. A foreign write that is OLDER than this
    // device's therefore leaves the note alone and produces a supersede record and nothing else —
    // which is right: nothing changed here, so there is nothing to propose.
    let dir = scratch("i-won");
    let mut journal = Journal::new(&dir);
    let mine = knowlu_engine::write::WriteContext::new("quinn", "dashboard");
    knowlu_engine::write::write_literals(&dir, "tasks/cs-100-hw-01.md", &[("importance".to_string(), "4".to_string())], &mine, &mut journal, &Default::default()).expect("my edit");
    let ctx = knowlu_engine::write::WriteContext::new(sync::ACTOR, "local-runner");
    let rec = foreign_set("task_0000000001", "tasks/cs-100-hw-01.md", "importance", serde_json::json!(3), serde_json::json!(5), "2016-09-14T10:00:00.000Z");
    let report = sync::apply(&dir, &index(), &pulled(vec![rec], vec![]), &ctx, &mut journal, "2026-09-14".parse().unwrap());
    assert_eq!((report.applied, report.cards), (0, 0), "{report:?}");
    assert_eq!(report.superseded, 1, "the losing write is still on the record");
    let meta = knowlu_engine::ids::read_meta(&dir.join("tasks").join("cs-100-hw-01.md")).expect("the note");
    assert_eq!(knowlu_engine::yaml::get(&meta, "importance").and_then(knowlu_engine::yaml::i64_of), Some(4));
    let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir);
}

#[test]
fn two_devices_that_set_the_same_value_produce_no_card_and_no_write() {
    // The degenerate conflict: both moved the field, to the same place. There is nothing to apply
    // and nothing to decide, and a card reading `from: 5  to: 5` would be noise in the one deck that
    // must never carry any.
    let dir = scratch("same-value");
    let mut journal = Journal::new(&dir);
    let mine = knowlu_engine::write::WriteContext::new("quinn", "dashboard");
    knowlu_engine::write::write_literals(&dir, "tasks/cs-100-hw-01.md", &[("importance".to_string(), "5".to_string())], &mine, &mut journal, &Default::default()).expect("my edit");
    let ctx = knowlu_engine::write::WriteContext::new(sync::ACTOR, "local-runner");
    let rec = foreign_set("task_0000000001", "tasks/cs-100-hw-01.md", "importance", serde_json::json!(3), serde_json::json!(5), "2036-09-14T10:00:00.000Z");
    let report = sync::apply(&dir, &index(), &pulled(vec![rec], vec![]), &ctx, &mut journal, "2026-09-14".parse().unwrap());
    assert_eq!((report.applied, report.cards), (0, 0), "{report:?}");
    let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir);
}

#[test]
fn a_conflict_on_a_field_no_card_could_ever_apply_takes_the_later_write_and_says_so() {
    // `approvals::validate_amendment` refuses a card whose field is outside AMENDABLE_FIELDS, so a
    // card for `title` would sit in the deck and fail forever. Reconcile's own rule decides it, the
    // loser keeps its supersede record, and one line names the note and the field.
    let dir = scratch("uncardable");
    let mut journal = Journal::new(&dir);
    let mine = knowlu_engine::write::WriteContext::new("quinn", "dashboard");
    knowlu_engine::write::write_literals(&dir, "tasks/cs-100-hw-01.md", &[("title".to_string(), "\"Mine\"".to_string())], &mine, &mut journal, &Default::default()).expect("my edit");
    let ctx = knowlu_engine::write::WriteContext::new(sync::ACTOR, "local-runner");
    let rec = foreign_set("task_0000000001", "tasks/cs-100-hw-01.md", "title", serde_json::json!("CS 100 HW 01"), serde_json::json!("Theirs"), "2036-09-14T10:00:00.000Z");
    let report = sync::apply(&dir, &index(), &pulled(vec![rec], vec![]), &ctx, &mut journal, "2026-09-14".parse().unwrap());
    assert_eq!(report.cards, 0);
    assert_eq!(report.superseded, 1);
    assert!(report.warnings.iter().any(|w| w.contains("title") && w.contains("cs-100-hw-01")), "{:?}", report.warnings);
    let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir);
}

#[test]
fn a_note_this_device_has_never_seen_arrives_whole() {
    let dir = scratch("newnote");
    let mut journal = Journal::new(&dir);
    let ctx = knowlu_engine::write::WriteContext::new(sync::ACTOR, "local-runner");
    let text = "---\ntype: task\nid: task_0000000077\nstatus: active\ntitle: From the other laptop\n---\n\nbody\n";
    let note = sync::PulledNote { device: "fedcba9876543210".into(), path: "tasks/other.md".into(), text: Some(text.to_string()) };
    let report = sync::apply(&dir, &index(), &pulled(vec![], vec![note]), &ctx, &mut journal, "2026-09-14".parse().unwrap());
    assert_eq!(report.notes_written, 1, "{report:?}");
    assert_eq!(knowlu_engine::pystr::read_text(&dir.join("tasks").join("other.md")).expect("read"), text);
    // And a second arrival of the same bytes is not a second write.
    let note = sync::PulledNote { device: "fedcba9876543210".into(), path: "tasks/other.md".into(), text: Some(text.to_string()) };
    let second = sync::apply(&dir, &index(), &pulled(vec![], vec![note]), &ctx, &mut journal, "2026-09-14".parse().unwrap());
    assert_eq!(second.notes_written, 0);
    let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir);
}

#[test]
fn a_pulled_record_that_is_not_a_record_never_reaches_the_ledger() {
    // `state/journal/**` is a byte-identical contract with existing vaults (`CLAUDE.md`), and an
    // envelope is only as trustworthy as the key that opened it — which is this machine's own, but a
    // corrupt row, a future version of this code, or a bug on the other device can all produce
    // something that decrypts and is still not a journal record. Every pulled record is checked
    // before it is appended, and a bad one is dropped with a named line, never written.
    let dir = scratch("badrecord");
    let mut journal = Journal::new(&dir);
    let ctx = knowlu_engine::write::WriteContext::new(sync::ACTOR, "local-runner");
    let good = foreign_set("task_0000000001", "tasks/cs-100-hw-01.md", "importance", serde_json::json!(3), serde_json::json!(5), "2026-09-14T10:00:00.000Z");

    let mut bad_op = good.clone();
    bad_op.insert("op".into(), serde_json::json!("frobnicate"));
    let mut bad_via = good.clone();
    bad_via.insert("via".into(), serde_json::json!("smuggled"));
    let mut bad_ts = good.clone();
    bad_ts.insert("ts".into(), serde_json::json!("yesterday"));
    let mut bad_path = good.clone();
    bad_path.insert("path".into(), serde_json::json!("../../outside.md"));
    let mut bad_folder = good.clone();
    bad_folder.insert("path".into(), serde_json::json!("state/journal/2026-01-01.jsonl"));
    let mut bad_id = good.clone();
    bad_id.insert("id".into(), serde_json::json!("not-an-id"));

    let report = sync::apply(
        &dir,
        &index(),
        &pulled(vec![bad_op, bad_via, bad_ts, bad_path, bad_folder, bad_id], vec![]),
        &ctx,
        &mut journal,
        "2026-09-14".parse().unwrap(),
    );
    assert_eq!(report.records, 0, "{report:?}");
    assert_eq!(report.refused, 6, "{report:?}");
    journal.invalidate();
    assert!(
        journal.read(None, None).iter().all(|r| r.get("device").and_then(|v| v.as_str()) != Some("OtherDevice")),
        "nothing foreign reached the ledger",
    );
    // …and the good one still does.
    let ok = sync::apply(&dir, &index(), &pulled(vec![good], vec![]), &ctx, &mut journal, "2026-09-14".parse().unwrap());
    assert_eq!((ok.records, ok.refused), (1, 0), "{ok:?}");
    let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir);
}

#[test]
fn a_pulled_delete_or_move_may_not_escape_the_vault_either() {
    // `write::delete` and `write::move_note` both take a vault-relative string that came out of an
    // envelope. `write::resolve_target` and `ids::inside_vault` would refuse a traversal inside
    // `write`, but the guard belongs where the value arrives — and a `move` names its destination in
    // `new`, which nothing downstream checks at all.
    let dir = scratch("badmove");
    let mut journal = Journal::new(&dir);
    let ctx = knowlu_engine::write::WriteContext::new(sync::ACTOR, "local-runner");
    let mut mv = knowlu_engine::journal::NewRecord::new("move", "tasks/cs-100-hw-01.md", "quinn", "dashboard");
    mv.id = Some("task_0000000001");
    mv.old = serde_json::json!("tasks/cs-100-hw-01.md");
    mv.new = serde_json::json!("../../outside.md");
    mv.ts = Some("2026-09-14T10:00:00.000Z".to_string());
    mv.device = Some("OtherDevice".to_string());
    let mut rec = knowlu_engine::journal::make_record(mv).expect("a record");
    rec.insert("seq".into(), serde_json::Value::from(9002));

    let report = sync::apply(&dir, &index(), &pulled(vec![rec], vec![]), &ctx, &mut journal, "2026-09-14".parse().unwrap());
    assert_eq!(report.moved, 0, "{report:?}");
    assert!(report.warnings.iter().any(|w| w.contains("refused")), "{:?}", report.warnings);
    assert!(dir.join("tasks").join("cs-100-hw-01.md").exists(), "and the note is where it was");
    assert!(!dir.parent().map(|p| p.join("outside.md").exists()).unwrap_or(false));
    let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir);
}

#[test]
fn a_pulled_note_may_not_escape_the_vault() {
    let dir = scratch("escape");
    let mut journal = Journal::new(&dir);
    let ctx = knowlu_engine::write::WriteContext::new(sync::ACTOR, "local-runner");
    for bad in ["../outside.md", "tasks/../../outside.md", "C:/Windows/outside.md", "state/journal/2026-01-01.jsonl"] {
        let note = sync::PulledNote { device: "fedcba9876543210".into(), path: bad.into(), text: Some("---\nid: task_0000000078\n---\n".into()) };
        let report = sync::apply(&dir, &index(), &pulled(vec![], vec![note]), &ctx, &mut journal, "2026-09-14".parse().unwrap());
        assert_eq!(report.notes_written, 0, "{bad} was written");
        assert!(report.warnings.iter().any(|w| w.contains("refused")), "{bad}: {:?}", report.warnings);
    }
    assert!(!dir.parent().map(|p| p.join("outside.md").exists()).unwrap_or(false));
    let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir);
}

#[test]
fn a_pull_whose_rows_none_of_them_open_is_a_refusal_and_not_an_empty_answer() {
    // The wrong recovery code has to fail loudly. Some rows failing is a warning; every row failing
    // is the key being wrong, and "nothing to restore" would be the worst possible way to say it.
    let body = knowlu_engine::ledger::dumps_value(&serde_json::json!({
        "records": [{ "seq": 1, "device": "fedcba9876543210", "hash": "a".repeat(64), "iv": "AAAAAAAAAAAAAAAA", "ciphertext": "Zm9vYmFyYmF6" }],
        "notes": [],
        "record_cursor": 1, "note_cursor": 0, "more": false
    }));
    let mut server = loopback(vec![(200, body)]);
    let client = CloudClient::new(&config(&server.base), "jwt-not-a-secret");
    let err = sync::pull(&client, &key(), 0, 0).expect_err("nothing opened");
    assert_eq!(err.label(), "the copy could not be opened with this key");
    let _ = server.requests();
}
```

```rust
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// The pull
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// Who writes a note that arrived from the cloud. An agent, because this device did not decide it —
/// and `judged` is never set, so nothing here touches judge-once. The record that *does* protect
/// judge-once is the foreign one, appended verbatim with its own `actor`.
pub const ACTOR: &str = "agent:knowlu.sync";

/// One note as it came down: its path, and its text — or `None` for a tombstone, which exists so a
/// **restore** does not resurrect a note that was archived or renamed on another machine.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct PulledNote {
    pub device: String,
    pub path: String,
    pub text: Option<String>,
}

/// One page of `/sync-pull`, opened.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default, PartialEq)]
pub struct Pulled {
    /// `(device token, the record as it was written on the device that wrote it)`.
    pub records: Vec<(String, Record)>,
    pub notes: Vec<PulledNote>,
    pub record_cursor: i64,
    pub note_cursor: i64,
    pub more: bool,
    pub warnings: Vec<String>,
}

fn opened_note(key: &SyncKey, row: &Value) -> Result<PulledNote, SyncError> {
    let device = row.get("device").and_then(Value::as_str).unwrap_or("").to_string();
    if row.get("deleted").and_then(Value::as_bool).unwrap_or(false) {
        // A tombstone carries no bytes, so it carries no path either — its `ref` is all there is,
        // and `restore` matches on that.
        return Ok(PulledNote { device, path: String::new(), text: None });
    }
    let iv = row.get("iv").and_then(Value::as_str).ok_or(SyncError::Shape("a note row has no iv"))?;
    let ct = row.get("ciphertext").and_then(Value::as_str).ok_or(SyncError::Shape("a note row has no ciphertext"))?;
    let plain = open(key, iv, ct)?;
    let v: Value = serde_json::from_slice(&plain).map_err(|_| SyncError::Shape("a note envelope is not JSON"))?;
    let path = v.get("path").and_then(Value::as_str).ok_or(SyncError::Shape("a note envelope has no path"))?;
    let text = v.get("text").and_then(Value::as_str).ok_or(SyncError::Shape("a note envelope has no text"))?;
    Ok(PulledNote { device, path: path.to_string(), text: Some(text.to_string()) })
}

/// Open one `/sync-pull` reply. **Takes a reply, not a socket**, so Task 9's replay test can hand it
/// one it built rather than standing up a server to prove something about the replay.
///
/// A row that will not open is a **warning**; `pull` below turns "nothing opened at all" into a
/// refusal, because that is the wrong recovery code and it has to fail loudly.
pub fn pulled_from_reply(reply: &Value, key: &SyncKey) -> Result<Pulled, SyncError> {
    let mut out = Pulled {
        record_cursor: reply.get("record_cursor").and_then(Value::as_i64).unwrap_or(0),
        note_cursor: reply.get("note_cursor").and_then(Value::as_i64).unwrap_or(0),
        more: reply.get("more").and_then(Value::as_bool).unwrap_or(false),
        ..Default::default()
    };
    for row in reply.get("records").and_then(Value::as_array).cloned().unwrap_or_default() {
        let device = row.get("device").and_then(Value::as_str).unwrap_or("").to_string();
        let (Some(iv), Some(ct)) = (row.get("iv").and_then(Value::as_str), row.get("ciphertext").and_then(Value::as_str)) else {
            out.warnings.push("sync: a record row carried no envelope".to_string());
            continue;
        };
        match open(key, iv, ct).ok().and_then(|p| serde_json::from_slice::<Value>(&p).ok()) {
            Some(Value::Object(record)) => out.records.push((device, record)),
            _ => out.warnings.push("sync: one record could not be opened with this key".to_string()),
        }
    }
    for row in reply.get("notes").and_then(Value::as_array).cloned().unwrap_or_default() {
        match opened_note(key, &row) {
            Ok(note) => out.notes.push(note),
            Err(_) => out.warnings.push("sync: one note could not be opened with this key".to_string()),
        }
    }
    Ok(out)
}

/// One page, fetched and opened. **A row that will not open is a warning; a page where NOTHING opens
/// is a refusal** — that is the wrong recovery code, and "there is nothing to restore" would be the
/// worst possible way to say so.
pub fn pull(
    client: &crate::cloudmodel::CloudClient,
    key: &SyncKey,
    after_records: i64,
    after_notes: i64,
) -> Result<Pulled, SyncError> {
    let reply = client
        .get(&format!(
            "/sync-pull?key_fingerprint={}&records_after={after_records}&notes_after={after_notes}&limit={PAGE}",
            key.fingerprint()
        ))
        .map_err(|e| match stale_key(&e) {
            Some(theirs) => SyncError::StaleKey(theirs),
            None => SyncError::Io(e.to_string()),
        })?;
    let offered = reply.get("records").and_then(Value::as_array).map(Vec::len).unwrap_or(0)
        + reply.get("notes").and_then(Value::as_array).map(Vec::len).unwrap_or(0);
    let mut out = pulled_from_reply(&reply, key)?;
    // The cursors fall back to what was asked for, not to zero: an empty page must never rewind a
    // device that has already applied a thousand records.
    if reply.get("record_cursor").is_none() {
        out.record_cursor = after_records;
    }
    if reply.get("note_cursor").is_none() {
        out.note_cursor = after_notes;
    }
    if offered > 0 && out.records.is_empty() && out.notes.is_empty() {
        return Err(SyncError::Crypto("every row"));
    }
    Ok(out)
}
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Write the apply section.**

```rust
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Applying what came down
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct ApplyReport {
    /// Foreign journal records appended to this device's ledger.
    pub records: usize,
    /// Notes written whole, because this device had never seen them.
    pub notes_written: usize,
    /// Fields written through `write` after reconciliation.
    pub applied: usize,
    /// `kind: amend` cards filed for a field both devices moved.
    pub cards: usize,
    /// Supersede records appended for a losing value. Nothing is ever dropped.
    pub superseded: usize,
    /// Notes settled into `archive/` or relocated because another device did.
    pub moved: usize,
    /// Pulled rows dropped before they touched anything: a record that is not a record, a path that
    /// is not a note path. Each one also produces a named warning.
    pub refused: usize,
    pub warnings: Vec<String>,
}

fn str_of(rec: &Record, key: &str) -> String {
    rec.get(key).and_then(Value::as_str).unwrap_or("").to_string()
}

/// Is `rel` a path this stream is allowed to write inside `vault`?
///
/// The **one** implementation of that question, used by `apply` for pulled notes, for a pulled
/// record's `path`, for a `move`'s destination, and by `restore`. Three tests, in this order: it
/// resolves inside the vault, it is under one of `ids::NOTE_FOLDERS`, and it ends in `.md`. The
/// second is what keeps a pulled row out of `state/journal/`, which the first would happily allow.
pub fn is_note_path(vault: &Path, rel: &str) -> bool {
    !rel.is_empty()
        && rel.ends_with(".md")
        && crate::ids::NOTE_FOLDERS.iter().any(|f| rel.starts_with(&format!("{f}/")))
        && crate::ids::inside_vault(vault, &vault.join(rel)).is_ok()
}

/// Is this decrypted object a journal record this device may append **verbatim**?
///
/// `state/journal/**` is a byte-identical contract with existing vaults (`CLAUDE.md`), and `apply`
/// writes to it through `JsonlLedger::append` without `write`'s own construction path — so this is
/// the only thing standing between a malformed row and that file. An envelope is only as trustworthy
/// as the key that opened it, which is this machine's own; what it is not proof against is a bug on
/// the other device, a future version of this format, or a corrupt row that happens to decrypt.
///
/// The path is checked by the caller, which knows the vault. Everything else is here.
pub fn record_is_well_formed(record: &Record) -> Result<(), &'static str> {
    let ts = record.get("ts").and_then(Value::as_str).unwrap_or("");
    if ts.parse::<jiff::Timestamp>().is_err() {
        return Err("its ts is not a timestamp");
    }
    if !crate::journal::OPS.contains(&str_of(record, "op").as_str()) {
        return Err("its op is not one of journal::OPS");
    }
    if !crate::journal::VIAS.contains(&str_of(record, "via").as_str()) {
        return Err("its via is not one of journal::VIAS");
    }
    if str_of(record, "actor").is_empty() {
        return Err("it has no actor");
    }
    if str_of(record, "device").is_empty() {
        return Err("it has no device");
    }
    match record.get("id") {
        None | Some(Value::Null) => {}
        Some(Value::String(id)) if crate::ids::is_id(id) => {}
        _ => return Err("its id is not an opaque note id"),
    }
    Ok(())
}

/// The journal's own `ts` for a file's modification time, so `reconcile`'s synthetic "this was an
/// external write" contender can be compared against a record's `ts`.
fn mtime_ts(path: &Path) -> String {
    std::fs::metadata(path)
        .and_then(|m| m.modified())
        .ok()
        .and_then(|t| t.duration_since(std::time::UNIX_EPOCH).ok())
        .and_then(|d| jiff::Timestamp::from_second(d.as_secs() as i64).ok())
        .map(|t| crate::journal::now_ts(Some(t)))
        .unwrap_or_default()
}

/// Apply one opened page.
///
/// **The role reversal, and it is the only subtle thing here.** `reconcile::resolve` was written for
/// git, where `checkout --ours` had already put *upstream's* file on disk and the job was to
/// re-apply *this* device's edits onto it. Here the file on disk is **this** device's, so the
/// arguments go over the other way round: `upstream_meta` is the local note, `upstream_records` are
/// this device's own records, and `local_records` are the foreign ones. Read the function with that
/// substitution and it does exactly the right thing — a field whose on-disk value equals the foreign
/// chain's first `old` is a field this device never touched, so the foreign value applies cleanly
/// and no supersede record is produced. **A field WITH a supersede record is a field both sides
/// moved**, and that is this plan's definition of a conflict.
pub fn apply(
    vault: &Path,
    index: &IndexKey,
    pulled: &Pulled,
    ctx: &WriteContext,
    journal: &mut Journal,
    today: jiff::civil::Date,
) -> ApplyReport {
    let mut report = ApplyReport::default();

    // 1. Everything this device already has, by content MAC. This is also what stops a device
    //    applying its own rows coming back down — which covers a re-install on the same machine,
    //    where the device token is the same string (precondition P7).
    let mut known: std::collections::BTreeSet<String> =
        journal.read(None, None).iter().map(|r| record_hash(index, r)).collect();

    // 2. Foreign records, **checked and then appended VERBATIM**. `journal.ledger.append` and not
    //    `journal.append`: the latter stamps a fresh `seq`, and these records already carry the
    //    `seq` the device that wrote them gave them. See the fidelity ledger for why rewriting
    //    `ts`/`device`/`seq` would break judge-once rather than merely look untidy.
    //
    //    **Checked first, and this is the only thing standing between a malformed row and
    //    `state/journal/**`** — a file `CLAUDE.md` calls a byte-identical contract with existing
    //    vaults. An envelope is only as trustworthy as the key that opened it, which is this
    //    machine's own; what it is not proof against is a bug on the other device, a future version
    //    of this format, or a corrupt row that happens to decrypt. A refused row is a named line and
    //    is never written.
    let mut foreign_by_path: BTreeMap<String, Vec<Record>> = BTreeMap::new();
    for (_, record) in &pulled.records {
        let hash = record_hash(index, record);
        if known.contains(&hash) {
            continue;
        }
        if let Err(why) = record_is_well_formed(record) {
            report.refused += 1;
            report.warnings.push(format!("sync: refused a pulled record — {why}"));
            continue;
        }
        let rel = str_of(record, "path");
        if !is_note_path(vault, &rel) {
            report.refused += 1;
            report.warnings.push(format!("sync: refused a pulled record — its path is not a note path"));
            continue;
        }
        if let Err(e) = journal.ledger.append(record) {
            report.warnings.push(format!("sync: a record could not be journalled ({e})"));
            continue;
        }
        known.insert(hash);
        report.records += 1;
        foreign_by_path.entry(rel).or_default().push(record.clone());
    }
    journal.invalidate();

    // 3. Notes this device has never seen, written whole. A note that already exists is NOT
    //    overwritten from the cloud — its fields are settled below, one at a time, so a two-sided
    //    change becomes a card instead of a file replacement.
    let mut arrived: BTreeMap<String, String> = BTreeMap::new();
    for note in &pulled.notes {
        let Some(text) = &note.text else { continue };
        if note.path.is_empty() {
            continue;
        }
        let target = vault.join(&note.path);
        if !is_note_path(vault, &note.path) {
            report.refused += 1;
            report.warnings.push(format!("sync: refused a note at {} — outside the note folders", note.path));
            continue;
        }
        arrived.insert(note.path.clone(), text.clone());
        if target.exists() {
            continue;
        }
        if let Some(parent) = target.parent() {
            let _ = std::fs::create_dir_all(parent);
        }
        match crate::pystr::write_text(&target, text) {
            Ok(()) => report.notes_written += 1,
            Err(e) => report.warnings.push(format!("sync: {} could not be written ({e})", note.path)),
        }
    }

    // 4. Every note the foreign records touched, settled field by field.
    for (rel, records) in &foreign_by_path {
        if rel.is_empty() {
            continue;
        }
        let path = vault.join(rel);
        // A `delete` or a `move` from another device: one `write` call here, so THIS device's own
        // journal records the move it made. Skipped, with a line, when the file is already gone —
        // never an error, because arriving twice is the normal shape of a retry.
        if let Some(op) = records.iter().map(|r| str_of(r, "op")).find(|op| op == "delete" || op == "move") {
            if !path.exists() {
                continue;
            }
            let done = if op == "delete" {
                crate::write::delete(vault, rel, ctx, journal).map(|_| ())
            } else {
                // **The destination came out of an envelope and nothing downstream checks it.**
                // `write::move_note` calls `ids::inside_vault` on it, which catches a traversal, but
                // it would happily accept `state/journal/2026-01-01.jsonl` — so the same three-way
                // guard the notes get is applied here, where the value arrives.
                let to = records.iter().find(|r| str_of(r, "op") == "move").map(|r| str_of(r, "new")).unwrap_or_default();
                if !is_note_path(vault, &to) {
                    report.refused += 1;
                    report.warnings.push(format!("sync: refused a move of {rel} — its destination is not a note path"));
                    continue;
                }
                crate::write::move_note(vault, rel, &to, ctx, journal).map(|_| ())
            };
            match done {
                Ok(()) => report.moved += 1,
                Err(e) => report.warnings.push(format!("sync: {rel} could not be settled ({e})")),
            }
            continue;
        }
        let Some(meta) = crate::ids::read_meta(&path) else { continue };
        let note_id = crate::yaml::get(&meta, "id").and_then(crate::yaml::text);
        // **Not filtered by a merge base, and deliberately.** The git path filtered both sides to
        // `ts > merge-base` because it had one; here the boundary is the cursor, and `reconcile`
        // uses this list only through `latest_by_field` — to find the record whose `new` is the
        // value now on disk. An older record cannot win that lookup, and including everything makes
        // the contender MORE likely to be found rather than less, which is the safe direction: a
        // contender that is not found becomes a synthetic "this was an external write", which is
        // true but says less.
        let mine: Vec<Record> = note_id
            .as_deref()
            .map(|id| journal.records_for(id, None))
            .unwrap_or_default()
            .into_iter()
            .filter(|r| str_of(r, "device") == crate::journal::device_name())
            .collect();
        // The role reversal, as documented above.
        let settled = crate::reconcile::resolve(&meta, &mine, records, &mtime_ts(&path), note_id.as_deref(), rel, &ctx.via);

        // A field with a supersede record is a field both sides moved.
        let conflicted: std::collections::BTreeSet<String> = settled
            .supersede
            .iter()
            .map(|r| str_of(r, "field"))
            .filter(|f| !f.is_empty())
            .collect();
        let folder = rel.split('/').next().unwrap_or("");
        let cardable = crate::approvals::AMENDABLE_FOLDERS.contains(&folder);

        let mut to_write: Vec<(String, serde_yaml_ng::Value)> = Vec::new();
        let mut to_card: Vec<(String, serde_yaml_ng::Value, serde_yaml_ng::Value)> = Vec::new();
        // **`Resolution.apply` holds a field only when the side passed as `local_records` won**
        // (`reconcile.rs`: `if local_wins { res.apply.insert(…) }`), and with the roles reversed that
        // side is the FOREIGN one. So a conflict this device won appears in `supersede` and not in
        // `apply` — nothing is written, no card is filed, and that is correct: this device's value
        // already stands, the loser is on the record, and there is nothing to propose.
        for (field, winner) in &settled.apply {
            let from = crate::yaml::get(&meta, field).cloned().unwrap_or(serde_yaml_ng::Value::Null);
            let to = crate::yaml::from_json(winner);
            if from == to {
                // Both devices set the same value independently. There is nothing to apply and
                // nothing to decide; the supersede record below still names which write lost.
                continue;
            }
            let scalars = !matches!(from, serde_yaml_ng::Value::Null | serde_yaml_ng::Value::Sequence(_) | serde_yaml_ng::Value::Mapping(_))
                && !matches!(to, serde_yaml_ng::Value::Null | serde_yaml_ng::Value::Sequence(_) | serde_yaml_ng::Value::Mapping(_));
            if conflicted.contains(field) && cardable && crate::approvals::AMENDABLE_FIELDS.contains(&field.as_str()) && scalars {
                to_card.push((field.clone(), from, to));
            } else {
                if conflicted.contains(field) {
                    // `validate_amendment` would refuse a card for this field forever, so reconcile's
                    // own rule decides it — later `ts` wins — and the loser keeps its supersede
                    // record. The line is what makes that visible rather than silent.
                    report.warnings.push(format!("sync: {rel}: {field} changed on two devices; took the later write (no card is possible for this field)"));
                }
                to_write.push((field.clone(), to));
            }
        }

        if !to_write.is_empty() {
            match crate::write::write(vault, rel, &to_write, ctx, journal, &WriteOpts::default()) {
                Ok(res) => report.applied += res.written.len(),
                Err(e) => report.warnings.push(format!("sync: {rel} could not be updated ({e})")),
            }
        }
        for mut rec in settled.supersede {
            match journal.append(&mut rec) {
                Ok(()) => report.superseded += 1,
                Err(e) => report.warnings.push(format!("sync: a supersede record could not be written ({e:?})")),
            }
        }
        if !to_card.is_empty() {
            let fields: std::collections::BTreeSet<String> = to_card.iter().map(|(f, _, _)| f.clone()).collect();
            // An identical pending proposal already IS the re-proposal — `write_literals` makes the
            // same argument for the judge-once path, and the fifteen-a-day cap is what makes it
            // matter here: a first pull after a long absence must not bury the deck.
            if crate::write::find_pending_amendment(vault, rel, &fields).is_some() {
                continue;
            }
            match crate::write::propose_amendment(vault, &path, &meta, &to_card, ctx, journal, None, today) {
                Ok(_) => report.cards += 1,
                Err(e) => report.warnings.push(format!("sync: {rel} could not be proposed ({e})")),
            }
        }
        let _ = arrived.get(rel); // the pulled text is the other device's; this device's file stands.
    }

    report
}
```

Add `use crate::write::{WriteContext, WriteOpts};` and `use crate::journal::Journal;` to the module's imports.

### Task 8: `knowlu-engine sync`, the slot, and the named skips

The command. Everything above becomes one subcommand that always exits 0 and says, in one short line each, exactly what happened or why nothing did.

**Files:**
- Modify: `engine/src/sync.rs` (the command section)
- Test: `engine/tests/sync_contract.rs` (six new cases)
- **Hand-offs this task needs:** **H2** (`engine/src/main.rs`'s `Sync` subcommand), **H7a** (`app/src/scheduler.rs`'s `slot_argv`) — both applied by the controller beside this task

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `cloudmodel::resolve`, Tasks 6 and 7.
- Produces:
  - `sync::Direction { Pull, Push, Both }` with `Direction::parse(&str) -> Option<Direction>`
  - `sync::Totals { pulled_records, pulled_notes, applied, cards, superseded, pushed_records, pushed_notes, more, skipped: Option<String>, blocked: Option<String>, errors: Vec<String> }`
  - `sync::STALE_KEY_INFO: &str` — the `close_key` of the one `info/` item this module opens
  - `sync::run_lines_with(&Path, Direction, &str, Option<&str>) -> (i32, Vec<String>, Totals)` — **the return code is always 0**
  - `sync::run_lines(&Path, Direction, &str, Option<&str>) -> (i32, Vec<String>)` — the printing twin, and one line of it
  - `sync::is_configured(&Path) -> bool`

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests** — four cases appended to `engine/tests/sync_contract.rs`:

```rust
#[test]
fn a_vault_with_no_account_says_so_and_exits_zero() {
    let dir = scratch("noaccount");
    let (code, lines) = sync::run_lines(&dir, sync::Direction::Both, "cli", None);
    assert_eq!(code, 0);
    assert_eq!(lines, vec!["sync (skipped: no account)".to_string()]);
    let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir);
}

#[test]
fn a_vault_with_an_account_and_no_key_says_it_is_not_turned_on_and_exits_zero() {
    // The switch IS the key's presence (Interfaces contract 3). On this machine there is no
    // credential at the derived target, so this is the path a student who has not turned sync on
    // takes twice a day — and it must be a sentence, not a failure, and the RIGHT sentence.
    let dir = scratch("nokey");
    std::fs::write(
        dir.join("config").join("cloud.yaml"),
        "api_base: 'https://example.invalid/functions/v1'\nanon_key: 'anon'\nsession_credential_target: 'knowlu/c3-no-such-profile/session'\naccount_id: 'acct-1'\n",
    )
    .expect("cloud.yaml");
    let (code, lines) = sync::run_lines(&dir, sync::Direction::Both, "cli", None);
    assert_eq!(code, 0);
    // **Exactly "not turned on", not "no session".** The three checks run `load` → `load_key` →
    // `resolve`, so the common answer — a student who has not switched the copy on — is the one they
    // read, and a signed-out machine is not reported as a subscription problem. Reversing the order
    // makes this assertion impossible to write, which is how the review found it.
    assert_eq!(lines, vec!["sync (skipped: not turned on)".to_string()], "{lines:?}");
    let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir);
}

#[test]
fn every_refusal_is_a_named_line_and_never_a_non_zero_exit() {
    // The property `judge` has and the one this step must have: a non-zero exit sets
    // `RunSummary.engine_ok = false`, which paints the tray amber and puts the slot into retry
    // backoff twice a day forever.
    let dir = scratch("exitzero");
    for yaml in [
        "",                                       // an empty file
        "api_base: 'https://example.invalid'\n",  // three keys missing
        "not: yaml: at: all\n",                   // unparseable
    ] {
        std::fs::write(dir.join("config").join("cloud.yaml"), yaml).expect("cloud.yaml");
        let (code, lines) = sync::run_lines(&dir, sync::Direction::Both, "cli", None);
        assert_eq!(code, 0, "{yaml:?} -> {lines:?}");
        assert!(!lines.is_empty(), "{yaml:?} said nothing");
    }
    let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir);
}

#[test]
fn a_re_keyed_slot_pulls_nothing_and_is_not_a_failure() {
    // The first slot after the switch went off and on again. The cursor remembers the old key, so
    // the whole vault is about to go up under the new one — and pulling first would fetch rows
    // sealed under a key this machine no longer has, fail to open them, and paint the sync line
    // amber on the one slot where the re-key actually succeeded.
    let dir = scratch("rekey-slot");
    let mut journal = Journal::new(&dir);
    let old = SyncKey::from_bytes([1u8; 32]);
    let new = SyncKey::from_bytes([2u8; 32]);
    let mut cursor = Cursor::default();
    cursor.key_fingerprint = old.fingerprint();
    assert!(sync::is_rekeyed(&cursor, &new), "a different key is a re-key");
    assert!(!sync::is_rekeyed(&cursor, &old), "and the same one is not");
    assert!(!sync::is_rekeyed(&Cursor::default(), &new), "nor is a first run");
    // And the batch it produces says so to the server.
    let (batch, _) = sync::build_push(&dir, &new, &cursor, "acct-1", &mut journal);
    assert!(batch.reset);
    assert_eq!(batch.key_fingerprint, new.fingerprint());
    let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir);
}

#[test]
fn a_stale_key_is_a_named_skip_and_a_notice_and_never_an_error() {
    // **The account's other device.** One machine re-keyed and re-uploaded; this one's key is fine
    // and its vault is fine, but the copy is no longer sealed with it. The run says so once, opens
    // one `info/` item the student can act on, pushes nothing, and stays green — a retry fixes
    // nothing here, so a failing slot would only paint the tray amber twice a day for ever.
    let refused = knowlu_engine::cloudmodel::CloudError::Status {
        code: 409,
        detail: "this machine's key is not the one this account's copy is sealed under (the account's key is 0a1b2c3d)".to_string(),
    };
    let detail = sync::stale_key(&refused).expect("a 409 is a stale key");
    assert!(detail.contains("0a1b2c3d"), "the account's fingerprint travels in the sentence: {detail}");
    assert_eq!(sync::stale_key(&knowlu_engine::cloudmodel::CloudError::Status { code: 402, detail: String::new() }), None);
    assert_eq!(
        knowlu_engine::sync::SyncError::StaleKey(String::new()).label(),
        "this machine's key is not the account's; enter your recovery code in Settings",
    );

    // The notice is opened once, not once a slot, and it is an ordinary `info/` note.
    let dir = scratch("stale-notice");
    let mut journal = Journal::new(&dir);
    let ctx = knowlu_engine::write::WriteContext::new(sync::ACTOR, "local-runner");
    let item = knowlu_engine::info::NewInfo {
        title: "This machine cannot read your cloud copy",
        kind: "heads-up",
        body: "…",
        opened_by: sync::ACTOR,
        close_key: Some(sync::STALE_KEY_INFO),
        expires: None,
    };
    knowlu_engine::info::open_info(&dir, &item, &ctx, Some(&mut journal), None).expect("the notice");
    let open = knowlu_engine::info::list_info(&dir);
    assert_eq!(open.len(), 1);
    assert_eq!(
        knowlu_engine::yaml::get(&open[0], "close_key").and_then(knowlu_engine::yaml::text).as_deref(),
        Some(sync::STALE_KEY_INFO),
    );
    // …and a successful run closes it by that key.
    let closed = knowlu_engine::info::close_info(&dir, Some(sync::STALE_KEY_INFO), None, sync::ACTOR, &ctx, Some(&mut journal), None)
        .expect("close");
    assert_eq!(closed.len(), 1);
    assert!(knowlu_engine::info::list_info(&dir).is_empty());
    let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir);
}

#[test]
fn the_direction_parses_the_three_words_and_nothing_else() {
    assert_eq!(sync::Direction::parse("pull"), Some(sync::Direction::Pull));
    assert_eq!(sync::Direction::parse("push"), Some(sync::Direction::Push));
    assert_eq!(sync::Direction::parse("both"), Some(sync::Direction::Both));
    assert_eq!(sync::Direction::parse("sideways"), None);
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the command section.**

```rust
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// The command
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum Direction {
    Pull,
    Push,
    Both,
}

impl Direction {
    pub fn parse(word: &str) -> Option<Direction> {
        match word {
            "pull" => Some(Direction::Pull),
            "push" => Some(Direction::Push),
            "both" => Some(Direction::Both),
            _ => None,
        }
    }
    fn pulls(self) -> bool {
        matches!(self, Direction::Pull | Direction::Both)
    }
    fn pushes(self) -> bool {
        matches!(self, Direction::Push | Direction::Both)
    }
}

/// Does this vault have an account at all? The app reads it to decide whether a step is worth
/// running; `run_lines` answers the same question for itself and does not depend on the answer.
pub fn is_configured(vault: &Path) -> bool {
    crate::cloudmodel::load(vault).is_some()
}

/// What one sync did, for a caller that renders it rather than prints it. [`run_lines`] is the
/// printing twin and the two are one function: parsing a sentence back into numbers is how a display
/// drifts away from what happened.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct Totals {
    pub pulled_records: usize,
    pub pulled_notes: usize,
    pub applied: usize,
    pub cards: usize,
    pub superseded: usize,
    pub pushed_records: usize,
    pub pushed_notes: usize,
    pub more: bool,
    /// The word after `sync (skipped: …)` when nothing ran at all.
    pub skipped: Option<String>,
    /// Set when the account's copy has moved to a key this machine does not have. **Not an error**:
    /// the slot is green, the tray stays calm, and what the student acts on is the `info/` item this
    /// run opens. `errors` stays empty so a retry backoff never starts for something retrying cannot
    /// fix.
    pub blocked: Option<String>,
    pub errors: Vec<String>,
}

/// The `close_key` of the one `info/` item this module ever opens. One item, reopened by nobody,
/// closed by the run that finds the problem gone.
pub const STALE_KEY_INFO: &str = "sync:key-generation";

/// Open the "this machine cannot read your cloud copy" item, once.
///
/// **A line in `state/runner-log.md` is not a thing a student reads.** VISION's rule is that a
/// decision surfaces where decisions are made; this one is not a decision but it is an action, so it
/// goes to the "Good to know" deck through the same `info::open_info` every other notice uses —
/// journalled like any other note, closed by its key when the next sync succeeds, and never opened
/// twice because `list_info` is checked first.
fn open_stale_key_notice(vault: &Path, detail: &str, ctx: &WriteContext, journal: &mut Journal) -> Option<String> {
    let already = crate::info::list_info(vault).iter().any(|m| {
        crate::yaml::get(m, "close_key").and_then(crate::yaml::text).as_deref() == Some(STALE_KEY_INFO)
    });
    if already {
        return None;
    }
    let body = format!(
        "Your cloud copy was started again on another machine, under a new key. Nothing is lost: this \
         vault is intact and the copy is intact. This machine cannot read or add to it until it has \
         the same key. Open Settings, and under Cloud copy enter the recovery code from the machine \
         that has it. ({detail})"
    );
    let item = crate::info::NewInfo {
        title: "This machine cannot read your cloud copy",
        kind: "heads-up",
        body: &body,
        opened_by: ACTOR,
        close_key: Some(STALE_KEY_INFO),
        expires: None,
    };
    crate::info::open_info(vault, &item, ctx, Some(journal), None).err().map(|e| e.to_string())
}

/// One sync, printed. **Always returns 0** (cloud design §5.5; the same rule `judge` has and for the
/// same reason): no account, no key, no session, no entitlement, no network, a 429, a 5xx — every
/// one is a named line on stdout and a slot that stays green.
pub fn run_lines(vault: &Path, direction: Direction, via: &str, run_id: Option<&str>) -> (i32, Vec<String>) {
    let (code, lines, _) = run_lines_with(vault, direction, via, run_id);
    (code, lines)
}

/// [`run_lines`], with the numbers. **Always returns 0.**
///
/// **Pull first, then push.** A field another device set this morning has to be in the note before
/// `rank` orders the day, and `slot_argv` puts this step first for exactly that reason (hand-off
/// H7a). The push then carries everything written since the last sync.
///
/// **The three checks are in this order, and the order is the message.** `load` says whether this
/// vault has an account at all; `load_key` says whether the student has turned sync on — which is
/// the common answer and has to be the one they read; and only then does `resolve` reach Credential
/// Manager for the session. The other order reports a signed-out machine and a pre-C1 vault as
/// `no session` before "not turned on" is ever considered, and saves a credential read twice a day
/// on every machine that never turns this on.
pub fn run_lines_with(vault: &Path, direction: Direction, via: &str, run_id: Option<&str>) -> (i32, Vec<String>, Totals) {
    let mut lines = Vec::new();
    let mut totals = Totals::default();
    let skip = |word: &str| {
        (0, vec![format!("sync (skipped: {word})")], Totals { skipped: Some(word.to_string()), ..Default::default() })
    };

    let Some(cfg) = crate::cloudmodel::load(vault) else { return skip("no account") };
    let key = match load_key(&cfg) {
        Ok(key) => key,
        Err(e) => return skip(e.label()),
    };
    let client = match crate::cloudmodel::resolve(vault) {
        Ok(client) => client,
        Err(why) => return skip(why.label()),
    };

    let index = key.index();
    let mut cursor = load_cursor(vault);
    let mut ctx = WriteContext::new(ACTOR, via);
    ctx.run_id = run_id.map(str::to_string);
    let mut journal = Journal::new(vault);
    let today = jiff::Zoned::now().with_time_zone(crate::cli::vault_zone(vault)).date();

    // **The fingerprint check happens HERE, before the pull, and that is the whole of why the
    // re-key slot is green.** `build_push` makes the same comparison and would reach it too, but a
    // page pulled beforehand would be rows sealed under the old key: they would fail to open, land
    // in `totals.errors`, and paint the sync line amber on the one slot where the re-key actually
    // succeeded. There is nothing to pull on a re-key — the copy is about to be replaced — so the
    // pull is skipped by name.
    let rekeyed = is_rekeyed(&cursor, &key);

    if direction.pulls() && rekeyed {
        lines.push("sync: this machine has a new key; nothing is pulled and the whole vault goes up again".to_string());
    } else if direction.pulls() {
        // One page per run. A device that has been away catches up over several slots rather than
        // holding the slot open for a term's worth of rows — and `more` is said out loud so a
        // reader knows the next slot has work left.
        match pull(&client, &key, cursor.record_cursor, cursor.note_cursor) {
            Ok(page) => {
                lines.extend(page.warnings.iter().cloned());
                let report = apply(vault, &index, &page, &ctx, &mut journal, today);
                cursor.record_cursor = page.record_cursor;
                cursor.note_cursor = page.note_cursor;
                if let Err(e) = save_cursor(vault, &cursor) {
                    lines.push(format!("sync: the cursor could not be saved ({e})"));
                    totals.errors.push(format!("the cursor could not be saved ({e})"));
                }
                totals.pulled_records = report.records;
                totals.pulled_notes = report.notes_written;
                totals.applied = report.applied;
                totals.cards = report.cards;
                totals.superseded = report.superseded;
                totals.more = page.more;
                lines.push(format!(
                    "sync pulled {} record(s), {} note(s): {} field(s) applied, {} card(s), {} superseded{}",
                    report.records,
                    report.notes_written,
                    report.applied,
                    report.cards,
                    report.superseded,
                    if page.more { ", more next slot" } else { "" }
                ));
                lines.extend(report.warnings.iter().cloned());
            }
            // **The account's copy has moved to a key this machine does not have.** Not an error,
            // and not something a retry fixes: one named line, one `info/` item the student can act
            // on, and nothing pushed — pushing would fill the copy with rows nobody can open.
            Err(SyncError::StaleKey(detail)) => {
                let word = "this machine's key is not the account's";
                lines.push(format!("sync (skipped: {word})"));
                totals.blocked = Some(word.to_string());
                if let Some(e) = open_stale_key_notice(vault, &detail, &ctx, &mut journal) {
                    lines.push(format!("sync: the notice could not be written ({e})"));
                }
                return (0, lines, totals);
            }
            Err(e) => {
                lines.push(format!("sync pull: {e}"));
                totals.errors.push(e.to_string());
            }
        }
    }

    if direction.pushes() {
        let (batch, next) = build_push(vault, &key, &cursor, client.account_id(), &mut journal);
        lines.extend(batch.warnings.iter().cloned());
        match push(&client, &batch) {
            Ok((records, notes)) => {
                // The cursor moves only now. A failed push costs a retry and nothing else.
                if let Err(e) = save_cursor(vault, &next) {
                    lines.push(format!("sync: the cursor could not be saved ({e})"));
                    totals.errors.push(format!("the cursor could not be saved ({e})"));
                }
                totals.pushed_records = records;
                totals.pushed_notes = notes;
                lines.push(format!("sync pushed {records} record(s), {notes} note(s)"));
            }
            Err(e) => match stale_key(&e) {
                // The same answer from the other half. A device can reach it here rather than on the
                // pull when its own cursor is empty — a fresh install of the app on a machine whose
                // key predates another device's re-key.
                Some(detail) => {
                    let word = "this machine's key is not the account's";
                    lines.push(format!("sync (skipped: {word})"));
                    totals.blocked = Some(word.to_string());
                    if let Some(e) = open_stale_key_notice(vault, &detail, &ctx, &mut journal) {
                        lines.push(format!("sync: the notice could not be written ({e})"));
                    }
                }
                None => {
                    lines.push(format!("sync push: {e}"));
                    totals.errors.push(e.to_string());
                }
            },
        }
    }

    // A sync that got all the way through is a sync whose key is the account's, so the notice — if
    // one was ever opened — is answered. `close_info` by key closes every item carrying it and is a
    // no-op when there are none.
    if totals.blocked.is_none() && totals.errors.is_empty() {
        let _ = crate::info::close_info(vault, Some(STALE_KEY_INFO), None, ACTOR, &ctx, Some(&mut journal), None);
    }

    (0, lines, totals)
}
```
