### Task 9: Restore — a fixture vault's journal replayed into an empty folder

The test the whole feature is judged by, and the one recorded exception to "every note write goes through `write`".

**Files:**
- Modify: `engine/src/sync.rs` (the restore section)
- Create: `engine/tests/sync_replay.rs`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Task 7's `Pulled`, `pull`, `pulled_from_reply`, `is_note_path` and `record_is_well_formed`; `ledger::JsonlLedger`; `pystr::write_text`; `cli::run` and `PINNED_FIXTURE_DATE` (the rank comparison).
- Produces:
  - `sync::RestoreReport { notes: usize, records: usize, warnings: Vec<String> }`
  - `sync::restore(&Path, &Pulled, &[String]) -> Result<RestoreReport, SyncError>` — the third argument is the **seed allowlist**
  - `sync::restore_all(&Path, &CloudClient, &SyncKey, &[String]) -> Result<RestoreReport, SyncError>` — pages `/sync-pull` from zero to the end
  - `sync::unexpected_notes(&Path, &[String]) -> Vec<String>`

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing round-trip test** — `engine/tests/sync_replay.rs`:

```rust
//! The round trip, end to end, with no network: a fixture vault's notes and journal are sealed, fed
//! through a loopback server that stores what it is given, pulled back into an empty folder, and
//! compared.
//!
//! **The notes are compared byte for byte.** The journal is compared as a SET OF RECORDS rather than
//! as file bytes, because `JsonlLedger::append` writes `pystr::NEWLINE` (CRLF on Windows) and the
//! fixture's own day files are whatever they are — and because the records, not the bytes, are what
//! the journal is (`ledger.read` sorts them into one order whatever order they arrived in).
use std::collections::BTreeSet;
use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};

use knowlu_engine::journal::Journal;
use knowlu_engine::sync::{self, Cursor, SyncKey};

const SYNC_TEST_KEY_NOT_A_SECRET: &str = "bm90LWEtc2VjcmV0LTMyLWJ5dGUtdGVzdC1rZXktISE=";

fn key() -> SyncKey {
    SyncKey::from_base64(SYNC_TEST_KEY_NOT_A_SECRET).expect("the test vector is 32 bytes")
}

fn copy_tree(from: &Path, to: &Path) {
    std::fs::create_dir_all(to).expect("mkdir");
    for entry in std::fs::read_dir(from).expect("read_dir").flatten() {
        let (src, dst) = (entry.path(), to.join(entry.file_name()));
        if src.is_dir() {
            copy_tree(&src, &dst);
        } else {
            std::fs::copy(&src, &dst).expect("copy");
        }
    }
}

fn temp(tag: &str) -> PathBuf {
    let dir = std::env::temp_dir().join(format!("knowlu-c3-replay-{tag}-{}", std::process::id()));
    let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir);
    dir
}

/// Everything the source vault would push, opened straight back into a `Pulled` — the wire without
/// the wire. The envelope itself is under test in `sync_contract.rs`; this test is about the replay.
fn round_trip(source: &Path, key: &SyncKey) -> sync::Pulled {
    let mut journal = Journal::new(source);
    let (batch, _) = sync::build_push(source, key, &Cursor::default(), "acct-1", &mut journal);
    assert!(batch.warnings.is_empty(), "{:?}", batch.warnings);
    let body = serde_json::json!({
        "records": batch.records.iter().enumerate().map(|(i, r)| serde_json::json!({
            "seq": i + 1, "device": batch.device, "hash": r["hash"], "iv": r["iv"], "ciphertext": r["ciphertext"]
        })).collect::<Vec<_>>(),
        "notes": batch.notes.iter().enumerate().map(|(i, n)| serde_json::json!({
            "rev": i + 1, "device": batch.device, "ref": n["ref"],
            "deleted": n.get("deleted").cloned().unwrap_or(serde_json::Value::Bool(false)),
            "iv": n.get("iv").cloned().unwrap_or(serde_json::Value::Null),
            "ciphertext": n.get("ciphertext").cloned().unwrap_or(serde_json::Value::Null)
        })).collect::<Vec<_>>(),
        "record_cursor": batch.records.len(), "note_cursor": batch.notes.len(), "more": false
    });
    sync::pulled_from_reply(&body, key).expect("every row opens with the key that sealed it")
}

fn records_of(vault: &Path) -> BTreeSet<String> {
    let mut journal = Journal::new(vault);
    journal
        .read(None, None)
        .into_iter()
        .map(|r| knowlu_engine::ledger::dumps_value(&serde_json::Value::Object(r)))
        .collect()
}

fn notes_of(vault: &Path) -> Vec<(String, Vec<u8>)> {
    sync::note_paths(vault)
        .into_iter()
        .map(|rel| (rel.clone(), std::fs::read(vault.join(&rel)).expect("read a note")))
        .collect()
}

#[test]
fn a_fixture_vaults_notes_and_journal_replay_into_an_empty_folder() {
    let source = temp("source");
    copy_tree(Path::new("tests/fixtures/vault-full"), &source);
    let dest = temp("dest");
    std::fs::create_dir_all(&dest).expect("an empty folder");

    let page = round_trip(&source, &key());
    let report = sync::restore(&dest, &page, &[]).expect("restore into an empty folder");

    // Every note, byte for byte, at the same path.
    let before = notes_of(&source);
    let after = notes_of(&dest);
    assert_eq!(before.len(), after.len(), "note count");
    assert_eq!(report.notes, before.len());
    for ((rel_a, bytes_a), (rel_b, bytes_b)) in before.iter().zip(after.iter()) {
        assert_eq!(rel_a, rel_b);
        assert_eq!(bytes_a, bytes_b, "{rel_a} differs after a restore");
    }

    // Every journal record, as a record.
    let records_before = records_of(&source);
    let records_after = records_of(&dest);
    assert_eq!(records_after, records_before, "the journal did not survive the round trip");
    assert_eq!(report.records, records_before.len());

    let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&source);
    let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dest);
}

#[test]
fn a_restored_vault_ranks_the_same_day_as_the_one_it_came_from() {
    // The property that makes a restore a restore. `rank` is deterministic over the vault's bytes,
    // so two vaults with the same bytes render the same `today.md` — and this is the cheapest place
    // to notice if the replay ever loses a field.
    let source = temp("rank-source");
    copy_tree(Path::new("tests/fixtures/vault-full"), &source);
    let dest = temp("rank-dest");
    std::fs::create_dir_all(&dest).expect("an empty folder");
    // **What a restore does NOT carry, and what a wizard-made vault has instead.** Sync moves notes
    // and journal records; `config/` and `profile/` are the student's settings and are written by
    // the wizard, and `state/` is generated. Three of those generated files are `rank` INPUTS rather
    // than outputs — `state/calendar.md` (the busy-time snapshot `calfeed` falls back to),
    // `state/events.md` (the roster) and `state/events-seen.md` — and `golden-today-full.md` proves
    // both contribute to the rendered day: its line 3 reads "Capacity today: 6.0h (template 8.25h −
    // 2.25h calendar)" and its line 5 "Events: 3 in today's digest". A real restore regenerates them
    // on the next slot from the account's own feeds; this test is comparing two renders of the same
    // day, so it copies them and says why rather than weakening the assertion to "both files exist".
    for folder in ["config", "profile"] {
        if source.join(folder).is_dir() {
            copy_tree(&source.join(folder), &dest.join(folder));
        }
    }
    std::fs::create_dir_all(dest.join("state")).expect("state");
    for name in ["calendar.md", "events.md", "events-seen.md"] {
        if source.join("state").join(name).is_file() {
            std::fs::copy(source.join("state").join(name), dest.join("state").join(name)).expect("a rank input");
        }
    }
    sync::restore(&dest, &round_trip(&source, &key()), &[]).expect("restore");

    let pinned = knowlu_engine::PINNED_FIXTURE_DATE;
    let a = knowlu_engine::cli::run(&source, Some(pinned), "manual", None).expect("rank the source");
    let b = knowlu_engine::cli::run(&dest, Some(pinned), "manual", None).expect("rank the restore");
    let text_a = knowlu_engine::pystr::read_text(&a.output).expect("today.md");
    let text_b = knowlu_engine::pystr::read_text(&b.output).expect("today.md");
    assert_eq!(text_a, text_b, "a restored vault must rank the same day");

    let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&source);
    let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dest);
}

#[test]
fn a_restore_refuses_a_note_it_did_not_put_there() {
    // The bound on the one exception to "every note write goes through `write`": a restore only ever
    // materialises bytes into a vault whose every note it put there itself. Anything else would
    // overwrite a student's work with a copy that does not know about it.
    let dest = temp("occupied");
    std::fs::create_dir_all(dest.join("tasks")).expect("mkdir");
    std::fs::write(dest.join("tasks").join("mine.md"), "---\nid: task_0000000001\n---\n").expect("a note");
    let err = sync::restore(&dest, &sync::Pulled::default(), &[]).expect_err("must refuse");
    assert!(err.to_string().contains("did not put there"), "{err}");
    assert!(err.to_string().contains("tasks/mine.md"), "and it names one: {err}");
    assert!(dest.join("tasks").join("mine.md").exists(), "and it touched nothing");
    let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dest);
}

#[test]
fn a_restore_tolerates_exactly_the_seeds_the_wizard_just_wrote() {
    // **The defect the first review found, as a test.** `scaffold::create_vault` seeds three kinds
    // of note before `restore_into` is ever called — `archive/_migrated.md`,
    // `tasks/get-to-know-knowlu.md` and one `courses/<slug>.md` per confirmed course — so a flat
    // emptiness check would refuse every real restore. The caller names what it put there; anything
    // else is still a refusal.
    let dest = temp("seeded");
    for folder in ["archive", "tasks", "courses"] {
        std::fs::create_dir_all(dest.join(folder)).expect("mkdir");
    }
    let seeds: Vec<String> = ["archive/_migrated.md", "tasks/get-to-know-knowlu.md", "courses/cs-100.md"]
        .iter()
        .map(|p| p.to_string())
        .collect();
    for rel in &seeds {
        std::fs::write(dest.join(rel), "---\nid: task_0000000002\n---\n").expect("a seed");
    }
    sync::restore(&dest, &sync::Pulled::default(), &seeds).expect("the seeds are tolerated");

    // One note the caller did not name, and it refuses again.
    std::fs::write(dest.join("tasks").join("stranger.md"), "---\nid: task_0000000003\n---\n").expect("a stranger");
    assert!(sync::restore(&dest, &sync::Pulled::default(), &seeds).is_err());
    let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dest);
}

#[test]
fn a_restored_note_overwrites_the_seed_at_the_same_path() {
    // Which copy wins when the account has its own `tasks/get-to-know-knowlu.md`: the account's,
    // because it is the one with the student's history behind it.
    let dest = temp("collide");
    std::fs::create_dir_all(dest.join("tasks")).expect("mkdir");
    let rel = "tasks/get-to-know-knowlu.md".to_string();
    std::fs::write(dest.join(&rel), "---\nid: task_0000000004\n---\n\nthe seed\n").expect("a seed");
    let theirs = "---\ntype: task\nid: task_0000000009\nstatus: active\ntitle: Get to know Knowlu\n---\n\ntheirs\n";
    let page = sync::Pulled {
        notes: vec![sync::PulledNote { device: "fedcba9876543210".into(), path: rel.clone(), text: Some(theirs.to_string()) }],
        ..Default::default()
    };
    let report = sync::restore(&dest, &page, &[rel.clone()]).expect("restore");
    asserteq_notes(&dest, &rel, theirs, report.notes);
    let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dest);
}

/// One assertion, named, because the message matters more than the line it is on.
fn asserteq_notes(dest: &Path, rel: &str, expected: &str, written: usize) {
    assert_eq!(written, 1);
    assert_eq!(
        knowlu_engine::pystr::read_text(&dest.join(rel)).expect("read"),
        expected,
        "the account's copy must win a path collision with a seed",
    );
}

#[test]
fn a_tombstoned_note_is_not_resurrected_by_a_restore() {
    let source = temp("tomb-source");
    copy_tree(Path::new("tests/fixtures/vault-full"), &source);
    let mut journal = Journal::new(&source);
    let (_, after) = sync::build_push(&source, &key(), &Cursor::default(), "acct-1", &mut journal);
    let victim = sync::note_paths(&source).into_iter().next().expect("at least one note");
    std::fs::remove_file(source.join(&victim)).expect("it goes away");
    let (second, _) = sync::build_push(&source, &key(), &after, "acct-1", &mut journal);
    assert!(second.notes.iter().any(|n| n["deleted"] == serde_json::Value::Bool(true)), "{second:?}");
    let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&source);
}
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Run it and watch it fail.**

Run: `cargo test -p knowlu-engine --test sync_replay`
Expected: FAIL — `cannot find function 'restore' in module 'sync'`.

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the restore section.**

```rust
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Restore
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct RestoreReport {
    pub notes: usize,
    pub records: usize,
    pub warnings: Vec<String>,
}

/// Every note in `vault` that is **not** in `tolerate`. The bound on the one exception below,
/// checked before a single byte is written.
///
/// **Why an allowlist rather than "the vault is empty".** The only caller is the picker's restore
/// route, and by the time it runs `scaffold::create_vault` has already seeded the vault it just
/// made: `archive/_migrated.md` (which `passes::detect_external` needs from the vault's first day),
/// `tasks/get-to-know-knowlu.md`, and one `courses/<slug>.md` per course the wizard confirmed. A
/// flat emptiness test would refuse every real restore — and a restore that deleted the seeds first
/// would have to delete their `create` records too, or leave the journal describing files that are
/// not there. So the caller says what it put there, and `restore` refuses anything else. A restored
/// note whose path collides with a seed **overwrites** it, which is right: the account's copy is the
/// one with the student's history behind it.
pub fn unexpected_notes(vault: &Path, tolerate: &[String]) -> Vec<String> {
    note_paths(vault).into_iter().filter(|p| !tolerate.contains(p)).collect()
}

/// Materialise one opened page into a vault that has no notes.
///
/// **The one recorded exception to `CLAUDE.md`'s "every note write goes through `write`"** (fidelity
/// ledger). Every record here **already exists** and carries the `ts`, the `device` and the `seq` of
/// the machine that made it; every note's bytes are the bytes that machine wrote. Putting them
/// through `write` would fabricate a second `create` record, stamped with *this* device's name and
/// *today's* `ts`, for a note created three months ago on another laptop — and `journal::human_set`,
/// which is what judge-once reads, would then answer with the restore instead of with the student's
/// own decision. The invariant exists to make attribution complete; re-journalling a restore is the
/// one way to break it while appearing to obey it.
///
/// Bounded three ways: the destination may hold **no note its caller did not name in `tolerate`**
/// (`unexpected_notes` walks all six of `ids::NOTE_FOLDERS`, so this is not a `tasks/`-only test),
/// every path is checked by `is_note_path`, and a tombstone writes nothing.
pub fn restore(vault: &Path, page: &Pulled, tolerate: &[String]) -> Result<RestoreReport, SyncError> {
    let strangers = unexpected_notes(vault, tolerate);
    if !strangers.is_empty() {
        return Err(SyncError::Io(format!(
            "{} already holds {} note(s) this restore did not put there ({}); a restore only ever fills a new vault",
            vault.display(),
            strangers.len(),
            strangers.first().map(String::as_str).unwrap_or(""),
        )));
    }
    materialise(vault, page)
}

/// The bytes half, shared by the first page and every page after it (there is no second copy of
/// this loop). `restore` is this plus the allowlist check; `restore_more` is this and nothing else.
fn materialise(vault: &Path, page: &Pulled) -> Result<RestoreReport, SyncError> {
    let mut report = RestoreReport { warnings: page.warnings.clone(), ..Default::default() };
    for note in &page.notes {
        let Some(text) = &note.text else { continue };
        if !is_note_path(vault, &note.path) {
            report.warnings.push(format!("sync: refused a note at {} — outside the note folders", note.path));
            continue;
        }
        let target = vault.join(&note.path);
        if let Some(parent) = target.parent() {
            std::fs::create_dir_all(parent).map_err(|e| SyncError::Io(e.to_string()))?;
        }
        crate::pystr::write_text(&target, text).map_err(|e| SyncError::Io(e.to_string()))?;
        report.notes += 1;
    }
    let ledger = crate::ledger::JsonlLedger::new(vault.join("state").join("journal"));
    for (_, record) in &page.records {
        if let Err(why) = record_is_well_formed(record) {
            report.warnings.push(format!("sync: refused a pulled record — {why}"));
            continue;
        }
        if !is_note_path(vault, &str_of(record, "path")) {
            report.warnings.push("sync: refused a pulled record — its path is not a note path".to_string());
            continue;
        }
        match ledger.append(record) {
            Ok(()) => report.records += 1,
            Err(e) => report.warnings.push(format!("sync: a record could not be journalled ({e})")),
        }
    }
    Ok(report)
}

/// Every page, from zero, into a vault that has no notes. What the picker's restore route calls.
///
/// The cursor is saved at the end so the first ordinary slot after a restore asks for what came
/// after, not for all of it again — and if that save fails, the next slot re-pulls and recognises
/// every record by hash, which is why this is a warning and not an error.
pub fn restore_all(
    vault: &Path,
    client: &crate::cloudmodel::CloudClient,
    key: &SyncKey,
    tolerate: &[String],
) -> Result<RestoreReport, SyncError> {
    let mut total = RestoreReport::default();
    let mut cursor = load_cursor(vault);
    let mut first = true;
    loop {
        let page = pull(client, key, cursor.record_cursor, cursor.note_cursor)?;
        let more = page.more;
        cursor.record_cursor = page.record_cursor;
        cursor.note_cursor = page.note_cursor;
        // The allowlist check belongs to the FIRST page only: by the second, the vault holds what
        // this restore has just written, and checking again would refuse its own work.
        let report = if first { restore(vault, &page, tolerate)? } else { materialise(vault, &page)? };
        first = false;
        total.notes += report.notes;
        total.records += report.records;
        total.warnings.extend(report.warnings);
        if !more {
            break;
        }
    }
    // The key this copy was opened with is the key the cursor is now under, so the next ordinary
    // push does not read a fingerprint mismatch and re-upload everything it has just downloaded.
    cursor.key_fingerprint = key.fingerprint();
    if let Err(e) = save_cursor(vault, &cursor) {
        total.warnings.push(format!("sync: the cursor could not be saved after the restore ({e})"));
    }
    Ok(total)
}
```

- [ ] **Step 1: Nothing to add to the engine.** `sync::run_lines_with` and `sync::Totals` are Task 8's, written there rather than here so this task consumes a shipped interface instead of rewriting one two tasks old (review finding M10). Re-read the two before starting; the shapes this task depends on are:

```rust
pub struct Totals {
    pub pulled_records: usize,
    pub pulled_notes: usize,
    pub applied: usize,
    pub cards: usize,
    pub superseded: usize,
    pub pushed_records: usize,
    pub pushed_notes: usize,
    pub more: bool,
    /// The word after `sync (skipped: …)` when nothing ran at all.
    pub skipped: Option<String>,
    pub errors: Vec<String>,
}

/// Always returns 0. `run_lines` is the printing twin and calls straight through to it.
pub fn run_lines_with(vault: &Path, direction: Direction, via: &str, run_id: Option<&str>) -> (i32, Vec<String>, Totals);
```

**Quoted, not written here** — the body is Task 8's and this is the shape this task reads. Two of its
properties matter to the code below: it **never returns a non-zero code**, so `run_here`'s `ok` comes
from `totals.errors` and not from an exit status; and a run that did nothing at all fills
`totals.skipped` with the word the line carries, which is what the page's row shows when the switch
is off.


- [ ] **Step 2: Write the failing app test** — `app/tests/sync.rs`:

```rust
//! The switch, and the one rule the published privacy policy already wrote down for us: **off by
//! default**.
//!
//! **This file touches the real Credential Manager**, so it carries its own file-scoped lock, a
//! generated test id, and a `Drop` guard that deletes what it wrote (`CLAUDE.md`; the argument
//! `app/tests/account.rs::CREDMAN_LOCK` makes — Windows races parallel `CredWriteW`/`CredReadW`
//! calls and answers a spurious `ERROR_NOT_FOUND`).
use std::path::PathBuf;
use std::sync::Mutex;

use knowlu::state::ConsoleState;

static CREDMAN_LOCK: Mutex<()> = Mutex::new(());

/// Deletes whatever it names when it goes out of scope, however the test ends.
struct Scratch(String);
impl Drop for Scratch {
    fn drop(&mut self) {
        let _ = knowlu::credentials::delete(&knowlu::sync::key_target_for(&self.0));
    }
}

fn profile_id() -> String {
    format!("profile_{:010x}", std::process::id() as u64 * 7919 + 1)
}

fn scratch_vault(tag: &str) -> PathBuf {
    let dir = std::env::temp_dir().join(format!("knowlu-c3-app-{tag}-{}", std::process::id()));
    let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir);
    std::fs::create_dir_all(dir.join("config")).expect("mkdir");
    std::fs::create_dir_all(dir.join("tasks")).expect("mkdir");
    std::fs::write(dir.join("config").join("planning.yaml"), "slice_hours: 1.5\n").expect("planning");
    dir
}

#[test]
fn sync_is_off_until_a_key_exists() {
    // `site/privacy.html`: "a separate switch, off by default". The switch IS the key's presence —
    // there is no settings field to fall out of step with it — so this drives `is_on` against a real
    // `ConsoleState` rather than asserting that a credential this test just deleted is absent, which
    // would pass with `app/src/sync.rs` empty.
    let _lock = CREDMAN_LOCK.lock().unwrap_or_else(|e| e.into_inner());
    let dir = scratch_vault("off-by-default");
    let data = std::env::temp_dir().join(format!("knowlu-c3-off-{}", std::process::id()));
    let cs = ConsoleState::open(dir.clone(), data);
    let id = cs.settings.lock().unwrap().profile_id.clone();
    let _guard = Scratch(id.clone());
    let _ = knowlu::credentials::delete(&knowlu::sync::key_target_for(&id));
    assert!(!knowlu::sync::is_on(&cs), "a fresh profile has no key and therefore no cloud copy");

    knowlu::sync::turn_on(&id).expect("on");
    assert!(knowlu::sync::is_on(&cs), "and the key's presence is the whole of the switch");
    knowlu::sync::turn_off(&id).expect("off");
    assert!(!knowlu::sync::is_on(&cs));
    let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir);
}

#[test]
fn turning_it_on_writes_one_key_and_hands_back_a_code_that_decodes_to_it() {
    let _lock = CREDMAN_LOCK.lock().unwrap_or_else(|e| e.into_inner());
    let id = profile_id();
    let _guard = Scratch(id.clone());
    let (code, fingerprint) = knowlu::sync::turn_on(&id).expect("a fresh key");
    assert!(knowlu::credentials::exists(&knowlu::sync::key_target_for(&id)));
    let key = knowlu_engine::sync::SyncKey::from_recovery_code(&code).expect("the code decodes");
    assert_eq!(key.fingerprint(), fingerprint);
    // Idempotent: asking again returns the SAME key, never a second one. A second key would make
    // every row pushed under the first unreadable, which is a data loss dressed as a feature.
    let (again, same) = knowlu::sync::turn_on(&id).expect("already on");
    assert_eq!((again, same), (code, fingerprint));
}

#[test]
fn turning_it_off_removes_the_key_and_says_the_copy_stays() {
    let _lock = CREDMAN_LOCK.lock().unwrap_or_else(|e| e.into_inner());
    let id = profile_id();
    let _guard = Scratch(id.clone());
    knowlu::sync::turn_on(&id).expect("on");
    knowlu::sync::turn_off(&id).expect("off");
    assert!(!knowlu::credentials::exists(&knowlu::sync::key_target_for(&id)));
    // And off twice is not an error: a student who clicks twice has not done anything wrong.
    knowlu::sync::turn_off(&id).expect("off again");
}

#[test]
fn a_staged_recovery_code_moves_onto_the_profile_that_is_born_with_it() {
    // The pending target, and the argument `account::move_session` makes: the profile id is derived
    // from a vault path that does not exist while the wizard is running.
    let _lock = CREDMAN_LOCK.lock().unwrap_or_else(|e| e.into_inner());
    let id = profile_id();
    let _guard = Scratch(id.clone());
    let key = knowlu_engine::sync::SyncKey::from_bytes([3u8; 32]);
    knowlu::sync::stage_key(&key.to_recovery_code()).expect("stage");
    knowlu::sync::move_pending_key(&id).expect("move");
    assert!(knowlu::credentials::exists(&knowlu::sync::key_target_for(&id)));
    assert!(!knowlu::credentials::exists(knowlu::sync::PENDING_TARGET));
    let _ = knowlu::credentials::delete(knowlu::sync::PENDING_TARGET);
}

#[test]
fn a_code_that_is_not_a_code_is_refused_before_anything_is_written() {
    let _lock = CREDMAN_LOCK.lock().unwrap_or_else(|e| e.into_inner());
    let _ = knowlu::credentials::delete(knowlu::sync::PENDING_TARGET);
    assert!(knowlu::sync::stage_key("not a code").is_err());
    assert!(!knowlu::credentials::exists(knowlu::sync::PENDING_TARGET));
}

#[test]
fn adopting_a_key_writes_it_and_resets_the_cursor_so_the_next_push_is_not_a_reset() {
    // **The account's other device, fixed.** The student read the recovery code off the machine that
    // has it. Writing the key alone would not be enough: the cursor still names the OLD fingerprint,
    // so the very next `build_push` would see a mismatch, send `reset: true`, and destroy the copy
    // this call exists to join. `adopt_key` rewrites the cursor as well, which is the whole
    // difference between adopting a generation and claiming one.
    let _lock = CREDMAN_LOCK.lock().unwrap_or_else(|e| e.into_inner());
    let dir = scratch_vault("adopt");
    let id = profile_id();
    let _guard = Scratch(id.clone());
    // A cursor written under some other key, with a note already recorded as pushed.
    let mut before = knowlu_engine::sync::Cursor::default();
    before.key_fingerprint = "deadbeef".to_string();
    before.record_cursor = 41;
    before.notes.insert("tasks/a.md".into(), "a".repeat(64));
    knowlu_engine::sync::save_cursor(&dir, &before).expect("save");

    let theirs = knowlu_engine::sync::SyncKey::from_bytes([13u8; 32]);
    let fingerprint = knowlu::sync::adopt_key(&dir, &id, &theirs.to_recovery_code()).expect("adopt");
    assert_eq!(fingerprint, theirs.fingerprint());
    assert!(knowlu::credentials::exists(&knowlu::sync::key_target_for(&id)));

    let after = knowlu_engine::sync::load_cursor(&dir);
    assert_eq!(after.key_fingerprint, theirs.fingerprint());
    assert_eq!((after.record_cursor, after.note_cursor), (0, 0), "both pull cursors start again");
    assert!(after.notes.is_empty(), "and nothing is remembered as pushed under the old key");
    assert!(!knowlu_engine::sync::is_rekeyed(&after, &theirs), "so the next push is not a reset");
    let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir);
}

#[test]
fn a_code_that_is_not_a_code_adopts_nothing() {
    let _lock = CREDMAN_LOCK.lock().unwrap_or_else(|e| e.into_inner());
    let dir = scratch_vault("adopt-bad");
    let id = profile_id();
    let _guard = Scratch(id.clone());
    let _ = knowlu::credentials::delete(&knowlu::sync::key_target_for(&id));
    assert!(knowlu::sync::adopt_key(&dir, &id, "not a code").is_err());
    assert!(!knowlu::credentials::exists(&knowlu::sync::key_target_for(&id)));
    let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir);
}

#[test]
fn a_vault_with_no_account_reports_off_rather_than_an_error() {
    let dir = scratch_vault("noaccount");
    let cs = ConsoleState::open(dir.clone(), std::env::temp_dir().join(format!("knowlu-c3-data-{}", std::process::id())));
    assert!(!knowlu::sync::is_on(&cs));
    let out = knowlu::sync::run_here(&cs, knowlu_engine::sync::Direction::Both);
    assert!(out.ok, "a vault with no account is not a failure: {out:?}");
    assert!(out.lines.iter().any(|l| l.contains("skipped")), "{out:?}");
    let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir);
}
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Write `app/src/sync.rs`.**

```rust
//! The half of sync the app owns (C3, Task 10): the switch, the key, the recovery code, the status
//! the page renders, and the export row.
//!
//! **Off by default, one switch, its own screen.** `site/privacy.html` said so before this existed:
//! *"If that ever changes it will be a separate switch, off by default, with its own screen
//! explaining what and why."* This module is that switch, and **the key's presence IS the switch** —
//! there is no `Settings` field, for the reason `account::cache_path` and `onboarding::offer_marker`
//! both give: `state::Settings` has no `#[serde(default)]` on any field, so a new one would silently
//! reset a student's backup folder and autostart choice on their first launch after the update.
//!
//! **This module never computes anything about the vault.** It generates a key, writes it to
//! Credential Manager, and calls `knowlu_engine::sync`. Every note write is the engine's.
//!
//! **No `#![cfg(windows)]` here, and none on its `pub mod` line either** (one gate, not two): the
//! module calls `crate::credentials`, which *is* `#[cfg(windows)]`, exactly as `onboarding.rs` does.
//! This crate is Windows-only in practice and says so in one place.

use std::path::Path;

use serde_json::{json, Value};
use tauri::State;

use crate::state::ConsoleState;
use knowlu_engine::sync::{Direction, SyncKey, Totals};

/// Where a recovery code waits between the picker's restore dialog and the vault being born — the
/// same shape and the same reason as `account::PENDING_TARGET`.
pub const PENDING_TARGET: &str = "knowlu/pending/sync-key";

/// `knowlu/<profile_id>/sync-key`. The engine derives the same string from
/// `config/cloud.yaml`'s `session_credential_target` (`knowlu_engine::sync::key_target`); this is
/// the app's side of that one contract, and `the_two_sides_derive_the_same_target` pins them.
pub fn key_target_for(profile_id: &str) -> String {
    crate::credentials::target_for(profile_id, "sync-key")
}

/// What the page's sync line reads. Copied into `topline.sync` verbatim by `commands.rs`, never
/// recomputed there (console spec §3.1).
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default, PartialEq, Eq, serde::Serialize)]
pub struct SyncStatus {
    pub on: bool,
    /// The key's eight-hex fingerprint. **Not the key**, and not reversible.
    pub fingerprint: Option<String>,
    pub last_at: Option<String>,
    pub pushed: usize,
    pub pulled: usize,
    pub cards: usize,
    /// Set when the account's copy has moved to a key this machine does not have. **Not an error**
    /// — the slot is green and the tray is calm — but it needs saying on the page as well as in the
    /// `info/` item the engine opened, because it is the one sync state the student has to act on.
    pub blocked: Option<String>,
    pub last_error: Option<String>,
}

pub fn is_on(cs: &ConsoleState) -> bool {
    let id = cs.settings.lock().map(|s| s.profile_id.clone()).unwrap_or_default();
    !id.is_empty() && crate::credentials::exists(&key_target_for(&id))
}

/// Make this profile's key, or hand back the one it already has. **Idempotent on purpose**: a
/// second key would make every row pushed under the first unreadable, which is a data loss dressed
/// as a feature. Returns `(recovery code, fingerprint)`.
pub fn turn_on(profile_id: &str) -> Result<(String, String), String> {
    let target = key_target_for(profile_id);
    if let Ok(existing) = knowlu_engine::wincred::read_credential(&target) {
        if let Ok(key) = SyncKey::from_base64(existing.password.expose()) {
            return Ok((key.to_recovery_code(), key.fingerprint()));
        }
    }
    let key = SyncKey::generate().map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    // `UserName` is the fingerprint — not a secret, and it is what lets a screen say which key a
    // profile holds without reading the blob.
    crate::credentials::write(&target, &key.fingerprint(), &key.to_base64())?;
    Ok((key.to_recovery_code(), key.fingerprint()))
}

/// Stop syncing, and forget the key.
///
/// **What happens to the copy already in the account, precisely** (precondition P5, answer (a)). The
/// rows stay where they are, because deleting a student's only off-machine copy on a switch flip
/// would be the worst possible reading of "off" — but the key that opens them is gone from this
/// machine, so unless the student kept the recovery code those rows are bytes nobody can read. The
/// next time the switch goes on, the device mints a fresh key, notices the fingerprint in
/// `state/sync-cursor.json` no longer matches, and sends `reset: true` with its first push: the
/// server drops the orphaned rows (which also frees their share of the ceiling) and the whole vault
/// goes up again under the new key. **The screen says this in one sentence**, and it is the sentence
/// Quinn reads under P2.
///
/// The cursor is deliberately **not** deleted here: `build_push` compares fingerprints and resets
/// itself, which is one mechanism instead of two, and it also covers the case this function never
/// sees — a vault restored on a new machine under a different key.
///
/// Off twice is not an error: a student who clicks twice has not done anything wrong.
pub fn turn_off(profile_id: &str) -> Result<(), String> {
    let target = key_target_for(profile_id);
    if !crate::credentials::exists(&target) {
        return Ok(());
    }
    crate::credentials::delete(&target)
}

/// Put the account's existing key on **this** machine, without starting a new copy.
///
/// **The difference from `turn_on`, and it is the whole of why this function exists.** `turn_on`
/// mints a key and leaves the cursor alone, so the next `build_push` sees a fingerprint mismatch and
/// sends `reset: true` — it is *claiming* a new generation. This is the opposite: the student is
/// *adopting* the generation the account already has, because another machine re-keyed and this one
/// was left behind (the `info/` item the sync step opened says so). Writing the key and leaving the
/// cursor would make the very next push a `reset` and destroy the copy this call exists to join.
///
/// So the cursor is rewritten to `Cursor::default()` carrying the new fingerprint: no mismatch, no
/// `reset`, both pull cursors at zero, and an empty digest map. The device then pulls the whole copy
/// and offers everything it has — which is free, because the account key is the same one the other
/// device used, so identical records MAC identically and `sync_records_once` absorbs them.
pub fn adopt_key(vault: &Path, profile_id: &str, code: &str) -> Result<String, String> {
    let key = knowlu_engine::sync::SyncKey::from_recovery_code(code).map_err(|e| e.label().to_string())?;
    crate::credentials::write(&key_target_for(profile_id), &key.fingerprint(), &key.to_base64())?;
    let cursor = knowlu_engine::sync::Cursor { key_fingerprint: key.fingerprint(), ..Default::default() };
    knowlu_engine::sync::save_cursor(vault, &cursor).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    Ok(key.fingerprint())
}

/// Park a typed recovery code before there is a profile to key it to. Refuses anything that is not
/// a code **before** it writes, so a typo never leaves a half-made credential behind.
pub fn stage_key(code: &str) -> Result<String, String> {
    let key = SyncKey::from_recovery_code(code).map_err(|e| e.label().to_string())?;
    crate::credentials::write(PENDING_TARGET, &key.fingerprint(), &key.to_base64())?;
    Ok(key.fingerprint())
}

/// Read, write the new one, delete the old — in that order, never delete-then-write. The argument
/// `account::move_session` makes, for the same kind of credential.
pub fn move_pending_key(profile_id: &str) -> Result<(), String> {
    let to = key_target_for(profile_id);
    let from = knowlu_engine::wincred::read_credential(PENDING_TARGET)
        .map_err(|_| "no recovery code was staged for this vault".to_string())?;
    crate::credentials::write(&to, &from.username, from.password.expose())?;
    let _ = crate::credentials::delete(PENDING_TARGET);
    Ok(())
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct RunOutcome {
    pub ok: bool,
    pub last_error: Option<String>,
    pub lines: Vec<String>,
    pub totals: Totals,
}

/// One sync, **in this process**, not as a child.
///
/// `knowlu_engine::sync` is library code with no model, no prompt and no child of its own, and the
/// app already links the engine — spawning `knowlu-engine.exe` to call a pure function would buy a
/// process boundary and nothing else. The SLOT still runs it as a child, because the slot runs every
/// step that way and a uniform step list is worth more than a saved process (hand-off H7).
///
/// Takes `vault_io` for the whole call, like every other vault-touching path (`ConsoleState`'s own
/// doc), and is never called while `cs.lock` is held.
pub fn run_here(cs: &ConsoleState, direction: Direction) -> RunOutcome {
    let _io = cs.vault_io.lock().unwrap_or_else(|e| e.into_inner());
    let (_, lines, totals) = knowlu_engine::sync::run_lines_with(&cs.vault, direction, "dashboard", None);
    let last_error = totals.errors.first().cloned();
    let out = RunOutcome { ok: last_error.is_none(), last_error: last_error.clone(), lines, totals: totals.clone() };
    let mut status = cs.sync.lock().unwrap_or_else(|e| e.into_inner());
    *status = SyncStatus {
        on: is_on(cs),
        fingerprint: status.fingerprint.clone(),
        last_at: Some(knowlu_engine::journal::now_ts(None)),
        pushed: totals.pushed_records + totals.pushed_notes,
        pulled: totals.pulled_records + totals.pulled_notes,
        cards: totals.cards,
        blocked: totals.blocked.clone(),
        last_error,
    };
    out
}

/// What a restore did, so `create_vault_in` can tell "nothing was there" from "it went wrong".
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct Restored {
    pub notes: usize,
    pub records: usize,
    /// True when the account's copy is genuinely empty. **Not a failure** — see `restore_into`.
    pub empty: bool,
}

/// Pull the account's whole copy into a vault that has just been created. The picker's restore
/// route, and its only caller (hand-off H8b).
///
/// **The allowlist is this machine's own seeds, and it is computed here rather than hard-coded.**
/// `scaffold::create_vault` has already run: it wrote `archive/_migrated.md`,
/// `tasks/get-to-know-knowlu.md` and one `courses/<slug>.md` per confirmed course. This vault is
/// seconds old and nothing else has written to it, so **every note present right now is one of those
/// seeds** — which makes `note_paths` the exact allowlist, with no second list to keep in step with
/// `seed_writes`. `restore` then refuses anything else, which is the property that matters: a
/// restore never fills a vault that holds a student's work.
pub fn restore_into(vault: &Path) -> Result<Restored, String> {
    let cfg = knowlu_engine::cloudmodel::load(vault).ok_or("this vault has no account")?;
    let key = knowlu_engine::sync::load_key(&cfg).map_err(|e| e.label().to_string())?;
    let client = knowlu_engine::cloudmodel::resolve(vault).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    let seeds = knowlu_engine::sync::note_paths(vault);
    let report = knowlu_engine::sync::restore_all(vault, &client, &key, &seeds).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    // **An empty copy is not a failure of vault creation** (review finding I5). A student who signs
    // in on a second machine before the first has ever pushed would otherwise lose the whole
    // nine-panel wizard run and be told, accurately and uselessly, that there was nothing there. The
    // vault they just made is correct; the finish panel says the copy is empty and that the other
    // machine's next sync will fill this one. A wrong recovery code is a different thing and is
    // still an error: `restore_all` returns `SyncError::Crypto` when nothing opens.
    Ok(Restored { notes: report.notes, records: report.records, empty: report.notes == 0 && report.records == 0 })
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Commands
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// The switch's state, and — only when asked — the recovery code.
///
/// **Re-showing the code is deliberate.** The key is already on this machine and the console is
/// already open behind a signed-in session; refusing to show it again would mean a student who lost
/// the paper has no way back into their own backup, which is worse than the nothing it protects
/// against.
#[tauri::command(async)]
pub fn sync_state(cs: State<'_, ConsoleState>, reveal: Option<bool>) -> Value {
    let id = cs.settings.lock().map(|s| s.profile_id.clone()).unwrap_or_default();
    let target = key_target_for(&id);
    let on = crate::credentials::exists(&target);
    let mut code = Value::Null;
    let mut fingerprint = Value::Null;
    if on {
        if let Ok(cred) = knowlu_engine::wincred::read_credential(&target) {
            if let Ok(key) = SyncKey::from_base64(cred.password.expose()) {
                fingerprint = json!(key.fingerprint());
                if reveal == Some(true) {
                    code = json!(key.to_recovery_code());
                }
            }
        }
    }
    let status = cs.sync.lock().map(|s| s.clone()).unwrap_or_default();
    json!({
        "ok": true, "error": Value::Null,
        "on": on, "fingerprint": fingerprint, "recovery_code": code,
        "account": knowlu_engine::cloudmodel::load(&cs.vault).is_some(),
        "last_at": status.last_at, "last_error": status.last_error, "blocked": status.blocked,
        "pushed": status.pushed, "pulled": status.pulled, "cards": status.cards,
    })
}

#[tauri::command(async)]
pub fn set_sync(cs: State<'_, ConsoleState>, on: bool) -> Value {
    let id = cs.settings.lock().map(|s| s.profile_id.clone()).unwrap_or_default();
    if id.is_empty() {
        return json!({ "ok": false, "error": "this profile has no id yet", "on": false, "recovery_code": Value::Null });
    }
    if knowlu_engine::cloudmodel::load(&cs.vault).is_none() {
        return json!({ "ok": false, "error": "sign in first — a cloud copy lives in your account", "on": false, "recovery_code": Value::Null });
    }
    if !on {
        return match turn_off(&id) {
            Ok(()) => json!({ "ok": true, "error": Value::Null, "on": false, "recovery_code": Value::Null, "fingerprint": Value::Null }),
            Err(e) => json!({ "ok": false, "error": e, "on": true, "recovery_code": Value::Null }),
        };
    }
    match turn_on(&id) {
        Ok((code, fingerprint)) => {
            cs.sync.lock().unwrap_or_else(|e| e.into_inner()).on = true;
            json!({ "ok": true, "error": Value::Null, "on": true, "recovery_code": code, "fingerprint": fingerprint })
        }
        Err(e) => json!({ "ok": false, "error": e, "on": false, "recovery_code": Value::Null }),
    }
}

/// *Export my data* (Quinn, 2026-09-14) — `GET /account/export`, C1's, written to a file the student
/// picks. **It replaces "ask at support@"** in `site/privacy.html`'s access-rights paragraph, which
/// Task 13 rewrites.
///
/// The vault is not in it, and the page says why: the notes were never on our servers in a form
/// anyone but this machine can read, and the folder is the best export there is.
#[tauri::command(async)]
pub fn export_data(app: tauri::AppHandle, cs: State<'_, ConsoleState>) -> Value {
    let body = (|| -> Result<String, String> {
        let cfg = crate::account::cloud_config(&cs.vault)?;
        let auth = crate::account::auth_base(&cfg.api_base)?;
        let token = crate::account::valid_access_token_at(
            &auth,
            &cfg.anon_key,
            &cfg.session_credential_target,
            jiff::Timestamp::now().as_second(),
        )?;
        // No `check_api_base` here: `cloud_config` already calls it AND refuses an `api_base`
        // naming a different host than this build talks to (`app/src/account.rs`, R-C1-59 I1). A
        // second call would read as a guard this caller needs, and it does not.
        let agent: ureq::Agent = ureq::Agent::config_builder()
            .timeout_global(Some(std::time::Duration::from_secs(60)))
            .http_status_as_error(false)
            .build()
            .into();
        let mut res = agent
            .get(&format!("{}/account/export", cfg.api_base.trim_end_matches('/')))
            .header("authorization", &format!("Bearer {token}"))
            .call()
            .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
        let status = res.status().as_u16();
        // 8 MiB: an export is account rows, not note text, and a cap is what keeps a runaway reply
        // from filling memory on a laptop.
        let text = res.body_mut().with_config().limit(8 << 20).read_to_string().map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
        match status {
            200..=299 => Ok(text),
            401 => Err("sign in again, then try once more".to_string()),
            other => Err(format!("your account could not be exported ({other})")),
        }
    })();
    let text = match body {
        Ok(t) => t,
        Err(e) => return json!({ "ok": false, "error": e, "path": Value::Null }),
    };
    let name = format!("knowlu-export-{}.json", jiff::Zoned::now().date());
    let mut dialog = rfd::FileDialog::new().set_title("Save your Knowlu export").set_file_name(&name);
    if let Some(w) = tauri::Manager::get_webview_window(&app, "main") {
        dialog = dialog.set_parent(&w);
    }
    let Some(path) = dialog.save_file() else {
        return json!({ "ok": true, "error": Value::Null, "path": Value::Null });
    };
    match std::fs::write(&path, text) {
        Ok(()) => json!({ "ok": true, "error": Value::Null, "path": path.to_string_lossy() }),
        Err(e) => json!({ "ok": false, "error": e.to_string(), "path": Value::Null }),
    }
}

/// Take the account's key on this machine, from the code the student read off the machine that has
/// it. The action the `info/` item and the sync line both point at.
#[tauri::command(async)]
pub fn set_sync_key(cs: State<'_, ConsoleState>, code: String) -> Value {
    let id = cs.settings.lock().map(|s| s.profile_id.clone()).unwrap_or_default();
    if id.is_empty() {
        return json!({ "ok": false, "error": "this profile has no id yet", "fingerprint": Value::Null });
    }
    match adopt_key(&cs.vault, &id, &code) {
        Ok(fingerprint) => {
            let mut status = cs.sync.lock().unwrap_or_else(|e| e.into_inner());
            status.on = true;
            status.fingerprint = Some(fingerprint.clone());
            // Cleared here rather than waiting for the next slot: the student has just done the one
            // thing that fixes it, and a row that still says "blocked" reads as "it did not work".
            // The `info/` item closes itself on the next successful sync, which is the real proof.
            status.blocked = None;
            json!({ "ok": true, "error": Value::Null, "fingerprint": fingerprint })
        }
        Err(e) => json!({ "ok": false, "error": e, "fingerprint": Value::Null }),
    }
}

/// The vault-less window's one command: park a typed recovery code for the vault the wizard is about
/// to make. It reaches Credential Manager and never this machine's vault folder, which is why
/// `scripts/wizard-check.py`'s `BEFORE_FINISH_OK` may hold it (hand-off H8).
#[tauri::command(async)]
pub fn stage_restore_key(code: String) -> Value {
    match stage_key(&code) {
        Ok(fingerprint) => json!({ "ok": true, "error": Value::Null, "fingerprint": fingerprint }),
        Err(e) => json!({ "ok": false, "error": e, "fingerprint": Value::Null }),
    }
}
```

`app/static/console.js`, the copy and the wiring — **the words are P2's and Quinn reads them**:

```javascript
  // ---- C3 Task 10: the cloud copy, its switch and its own screen (cloud design §5.5).
  // `site/privacy.html` promised this shape before the feature existed: a separate switch, off by
  // default, with its own screen explaining what and why. These five sentences ARE that screen, and
  // they are the page's copy of a promise the policy makes in its own words — the two are read
  // together at every change (P2).
  var SYNC_LEDE = "Knowlu can keep a copy of your notes and their history in your account, so a lost or replaced laptop is an afternoon and not a term.";
  // Shown instead of the lede when the copy has moved to a key this machine does not have. The
  // engine has already opened a Good-to-know item saying the same thing; this is the screen it sends
  // the student to.
  var SYNC_BLOCKED = "Your cloud copy was started again on another machine, under a new key. Nothing is lost \u2014 this vault and the copy are both intact \u2014 but this machine cannot read or add to the copy until it has the same key. Enter the recovery code from the machine that has it.";
  var SYNC_POINTS = [
    "The copy is encrypted on this machine, with a key that is generated here and never sent to us. We store it; we cannot read it.",
    "Your recovery code is that key. It is the only way to open the copy on a new machine, and nobody can send it to you — not even us.",
    "What goes up: your notes, and the record of every change to them. Not your passwords, which stay in Windows, and not your calendar links.",
    "Turning this off later stops the copying and forgets the key on this machine. What is already in your account stays until you use Delete my data — but without your recovery code nobody can open it, and switching back on starts a fresh copy.",
    "Your local backup folder keeps working either way. It is the copy for a mistake; this is the copy for a lost machine."
  ];
  function renderSyncRow(s) {
    var on = s && s.on;
    EL("set-sync-state").textContent = !s || !s.account
      ? "sign in to use a cloud copy"
      : s && s.blocked ? ("needs the account's key \u00b7 this machine has " + (s.fingerprint || "?"))
      : on ? ("on \u00b7 key " + (s.fingerprint || "?")) : "off \u2014 this machine only";
    EL("set-sync-on").hidden = on;
    EL("set-sync-code").hidden = !on;
    EL("set-sync-off").hidden = !on;
    EL("set-sync-on").disabled = !(s && s.account);
  }
  function openSyncScreen(code, blocked) {
    EL("sync-lede").textContent = blocked ? SYNC_BLOCKED : SYNC_LEDE;
    EL("sync-adopt-row").hidden = !blocked;
    EL("sync-points").innerHTML = SYNC_POINTS.map(function (p) { return "<li>" + h(p) + "</li>"; }).join("");
    EL("sync-error").textContent = "";
    EL("sync-code").textContent = code || "";
    EL("sync-code-row").hidden = !code;
    EL("sync-saved-row").hidden = !code;
    EL("sync-saved").checked = false;
    EL("sync-go").hidden = !!code;
    EL("sync-cancel").disabled = !!code;
    EL("syncon").hidden = false;
  }
  EL("set-sync-on").addEventListener("click", function () { openSyncScreen(null, false); });
  EL("set-sync-code").addEventListener("click", function () {
    invoke("sync_state", { reveal: true }).then(function (s) { if (s && s.ok) { openSyncScreen(s.recovery_code, !!s.blocked); } }).catch(function () {});
  });
  EL("set-sync-off").addEventListener("click", function () {
    invoke("set_sync", { on: false }).then(function (s) { renderSyncRow({ account: true, on: s.on }); }).catch(function () {});
  });
  EL("sync-go").addEventListener("click", function () {
    EL("sync-go").disabled = true;
    invoke("set_sync", { on: true }).then(function (s) {
      EL("sync-go").disabled = false;
      if (!s.ok) { EL("sync-error").textContent = s.error; return; }
      openSyncScreen(s.recovery_code, false);
      renderSyncRow({ account: true, on: true, fingerprint: s.fingerprint });
    }).catch(function () { EL("sync-go").disabled = false; });
  });
  EL("sync-saved").addEventListener("change", function () { EL("sync-cancel").disabled = !EL("sync-saved").checked; });
  EL("sync-copy").addEventListener("click", function () { invoke("copy_text", { text: EL("sync-code").textContent }).catch(function () {}); });
  // The action the "this machine cannot read your cloud copy" notice points at. **`set_sync_key`,
  // not `set_sync`**: turning the switch off and on would mint a NEW key and start the copy again
  // from this machine, which is the opposite of what the student is trying to do.
  EL("sync-adopt-go").addEventListener("click", function () {
    EL("sync-error").textContent = "";
    invoke("set_sync_key", { code: EL("sync-adopt-code").value }).then(function (r) {
      if (!r.ok) { EL("sync-error").textContent = r.error; return; }
      EL("sync-adopt-code").value = "";
      EL("syncon").hidden = true;
      renderSyncRow({ account: true, on: true, fingerprint: r.fingerprint });
    }).catch(function () { EL("sync-error").textContent = "that code could not be saved on this machine"; });
  });
  EL("sync-cancel").addEventListener("click", function () { EL("syncon").hidden = true; });
  EL("set-export-go").addEventListener("click", function () {
    EL("set-export-note").textContent = "\u2026";
    invoke("export_data", {}).then(function (r) {
      EL("set-export-note").textContent = !r.ok ? r.error : (r.path ? "saved" : "");
    }).catch(function () { EL("set-export-note").textContent = "could not export"; });
  });
```

- [ ] **Step 8: Pin the screen in `app/tests/static_assets.rs`.**

```rust
#[test]
fn the_cloud_copy_has_a_switch_a_screen_and_the_five_sentences() {
    // `site/privacy.html`: "a separate switch, off by default, with its own screen explaining what
    // and why". This is the executable half of that sentence — the policy's other half is
    // `site.rs::the_policy_describes_the_cloud_copy_switch`.
    let html = read("index.html");
    let js = read("console.js");
    assert!(html.contains("id=\"set-sync\"") && html.contains("id=\"set-sync-on\""), "the switch");
    assert!(html.contains("id=\"syncon\"") && html.contains("id=\"sync-code\""), "its own screen");
    assert!(html.contains("id=\"sync-saved\""), "and the checkbox that gates leaving it");
    assert!(html.contains("id=\"set-export\"") && html.contains("id=\"set-export-go\""), "the export row");
    // The account's other devices: the screen has somewhere to put the code, and the page has a way
    // to say why. `set_sync_key`, never `set_sync` — turning the switch off and on would mint a new
    // key and start the copy again from this machine.
    assert!(html.contains("id=\"sync-adopt-row\"") && html.contains("id=\"sync-adopt-code\""), "the adopt row");
    assert!(js.contains("\"set_sync_key\""), "and the command it calls");
    assert!(js.contains("var SYNC_BLOCKED = "), "the screen says what a stale key is");
    assert!(js.contains("cloud copy needs this machine"), "and the sync line does too");
    assert!(js.contains("var SYNC_POINTS = ["), "the screen's words are in one place");
    // Five sentences, not a heading. Counted inside the array's own slice so reformatting the file
    // cannot change the answer — a brittle whitespace count would fail on a `deno fmt`-shaped edit
    // that changed nothing anyone reads.
    let block = js.split("var SYNC_POINTS = [").nth(1).and_then(|s| s.split("];").next()).expect("the array");
    assert_eq!(block.matches('"').count() / 2, 5, "five sentences: {block}");
    for phrase in [
        "never sent to us",
        "We store it; we cannot read it",
        "the only way to open the copy on a new machine",
        "switching back on starts a fresh copy",
    ] {
        assert!(js.contains(phrase), "the screen must say: {phrase}");
    }
    // And it still carries no network literal (console spec §7) — every endpoint is Rust's.
    assert!(!js.contains("sync-push") && !js.contains("sync-pull"), "the page names no endpoint");
}
```

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Task 10's `stage_restore_key`, `move_pending_key`, `restore_into`; `onboarding::create_vault_in`.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test** — two cases in `app/tests/onboarding.rs`:

```rust
#[test]
fn a_restore_plan_that_staged_no_code_fails_before_it_makes_a_vault() {
    // The order that matters: `create_vault_in` moves the session, then the key, then pulls — and
    // any failure removes the folder it just made, so a student never ends up with a half-made
    // vault they then have to be told about.
    let _lock = CREDMAN_LOCK.lock().unwrap_or_else(|e| e.into_inner());
    let _ = knowlu::credentials::delete(knowlu::sync::PENDING_TARGET);
    let home = tmp("restore-nokey-home");
    let root = tmp("restore-nokey-root");
    let mut plan = base_plan(false);
    plan.restore = true;
    let out = knowlu::onboarding::create_vault_in(&root, &home, "Restored", &plan);
    assert_eq!(out["ok"], false, "{out}");
    // Either refusal is correct and both remove the folder: with no pending SESSION the sign-in
    // check fires first, and with one but no pending KEY the recovery-code check does. The property
    // under test is that nothing is left behind, and the message names which.
    let error = out["error"].as_str().unwrap_or_default().to_string();
    assert!(error.contains("recovery code") || error.contains("sign in again"), "{error}");
    assert!(!home.join("Knowlu").join("Restored").exists(), "nothing half-made");
}

#[test]
fn an_empty_cloud_copy_is_not_a_failure_of_making_a_vault() {
    // Review finding I5, at the level a unit test can reach it: `restore_all` on an account with no
    // rows returns a report of zeros, and `restore_into` turns that into `empty`, not `Err`. The
    // `create_vault_in` arm that keeps the vault is one `match` on this value (hand-off H8b) and the
    // page's sentence is pinned by `static_assets.rs` below.
    let dest = tmp("empty-copy");
    std::fs::create_dir_all(dest.join("tasks")).expect("mkdir");
    let page = knowlu_engine::sync::Pulled::default();
    let report = knowlu_engine::sync::restore(&dest, &page, &[]).expect("an empty page is not an error");
    assert_eq!((report.notes, report.records), (0, 0));
    // …and a wrong key IS an error, which is the distinction the arm rests on: `pull` refuses when
    // a page offered rows and none of them opened (`SyncError::Crypto`), which `restore_into`
    // propagates and `create_vault_in` rolls back on.
    assert_eq!(knowlu_engine::sync::SyncError::Crypto("every row").label(), "the copy could not be opened with this key");
    let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dest);
}

#[test]
fn a_plan_without_the_flag_never_touches_the_pending_key() {
    // An ordinary wizard finish must not consume a staged code — a student who started a restore,
    // backed out and made a fresh vault instead would otherwise lose the code silently.
    let _lock = CREDMAN_LOCK.lock().unwrap_or_else(|e| e.into_inner());
    let key = knowlu_engine::sync::SyncKey::from_bytes([5u8; 32]);
    knowlu::sync::stage_key(&key.to_recovery_code()).expect("stage");
    let home = tmp("restore-off-home");
    let root = tmp("restore-off-root");
    let mut plan = base_plan(false);
    plan.restore = false;
    let _ = knowlu::onboarding::create_vault_in(&root, &home, "Fresh", &plan);
    assert!(knowlu::credentials::exists(knowlu::sync::PENDING_TARGET), "the staged code survives");
    let _ = knowlu::credentials::delete(knowlu::sync::PENDING_TARGET);
}
```

`tmp`, `base_plan` and `CREDMAN_LOCK` are the file's own helpers (`app/tests/onboarding.rs:11`, `:341`, `:236`). **`base_plan` gains one line** in the same commit — `restore: false,` after `offer_inference` — because `WizardPlan` has no `Default` and every field is named there.

- [ ] **Step 4: Wire it** — `app/static/console.js`, beside the picker's other two handlers:

```javascript
  // R-C1-59 answered (HANDOFF §4, Quinn 2026-09-14): the picker's third door. It does NOT restore
  // here — it stages the code and starts the ordinary wizard, which signs in, subscribes, names the
  // vault and creates it exactly as it always does; the copy is pulled at Finish. One flow, one set
  // of panels, and no second place where a vault can be made.
  var RESTORE_LEDE = "Sign in on the next screens with the same account, and Knowlu will fill the new vault from your encrypted copy. You will need the recovery code you saved when you turned the copy on \u2014 without it nobody can open the copy, including us.";
  EL("pick-restore").addEventListener("click", function () {
    EL("restore-lede").textContent = RESTORE_LEDE;
    EL("restore-error").textContent = "";
    EL("restore-code").value = "";
    EL("restore").hidden = false;
  });
  EL("restore-cancel").addEventListener("click", function () { EL("restore").hidden = true; });
  EL("restore-go").addEventListener("click", function () {
    var code = EL("restore-code").value;
    EL("restore-go").disabled = true;
    invoke("stage_restore_key", { code: code }).then(function (r) {
      EL("restore-go").disabled = false;
      if (!r.ok) { EL("restore-error").textContent = r.error; return; }
      EL("restore").hidden = true;
      return invoke("launch_state", {}).then(function (l) { startWizard(l || {}, { restore: true }); });
    }).catch(function () { EL("restore-go").disabled = false; EL("restore-error").textContent = "that code could not be saved on this machine"; });
  });
```

`startWizard` gains a second, optional argument; it sets `wizPlan.restore = !!(opts && opts.restore)` and, when it is set, the finish panel's summary carries one extra line — *"Your cloud copy will be restored into this vault."* — so the student sees what Finish is about to do before they press it. **And when Finish comes back**, the same panel says which of the two things happened:

```javascript
  // Review finding I5: an empty copy is not a failure, and the sentence is the only place the
  // student learns the difference between "nothing was there" and "it did not work".
  var RESTORE_EMPTY = "Your cloud copy is empty \u2014 the next sync from your other machine will fill this one.";
  function afterFinish(env) {
    if (env && env.ok && env.restored && env.restored.empty) { EL("wiz-finish-note").textContent = RESTORE_EMPTY; }
  }
```

which means `finish_or_roll_back`'s envelope carries one more key on this route — `"restored": { "notes": n, "records": m, "empty": bool }` — set from `create_vault_in`'s `restored` (hand-off H8b) and absent on every other path.

```rust
#[test]
fn the_picker_offers_both_restores_and_says_what_each_one_needs() {
    // `HANDOFF.md` §4 (Quinn, 2026-09-14) and spec §5.5 are two different restores — the local
    // `Backups` mirror and the account's encrypted copy — and precondition P6 puts both on the
    // picker. Each says the one thing a student has to understand about it.
    let html = read("index.html");
    let js = read("console.js");
    let picker = html.split("id=\"picker\"").nth(1).and_then(|s| s.split("</section>").next()).expect("the picker");
    assert!(picker.contains("id=\"pick-restore\""), "the cloud door");
    assert!(picker.contains("id=\"pick-restore-backup\""), "and the local one");
    assert!(html.contains("id=\"restore\"") && html.contains("id=\"restore-code\""), "where the code is typed");
    assert!(html.contains("id=\"restore-backup\"") && html.contains("id=\"restore-backup-name\""), "and where the folder is named");
    assert!(js.contains("\"stage_restore_key\"") && js.contains("\"restore_vault\""), "both routes have a caller");
    assert!(js.contains("including us"), "the cloud one says nobody can send them the code");
    assert!(js.contains("only read, never changed"), "the local one says the backup is not touched");
    // The wizard still has NINE panels: a restore is the same wizard with a flag, not a tenth panel.
    assert!(js.contains("var PANELS = [\"welcome\", \"account\", \"subscribe\", \"vault\", \"calendars\", \"logins\", \"gmail\", \"slots\", \"finish\"];"));
}

#[test]
fn the_finish_panel_says_when_the_cloud_copy_was_empty() {
    // Review finding I5: an empty copy keeps the vault, and the only honest place to say so is the
    // screen the student is already looking at.
    let js = read("console.js");
    assert!(js.contains("your cloud copy is empty"), "the finish panel names the empty case");
    assert!(js.contains("will fill this one"), "and says what happens next");
}
```

`app/static/console.js`, beside the cloud one — **`restore_vault` already exists and is registered in the wizard window's handler list; this is the caller it never had**:

```javascript
  // `HANDOFF.md` §4, Quinn 2026-09-14, the half that is about the LOCAL mirror. `restore_vault_in`
  // copies `<backup>\<profile>\vault\` to a new folder under `%USERPROFILE%\Knowlu` and uses the
  // COPY — the backup is only ever read (`app/tests/onboarding.rs` hashes it either side and proves
  // it). No recovery code and no account: this is the copy that is already on this machine.
  var RESTORE_BACKUP_LEDE = "Point Knowlu at your Backups folder and it will copy the vault out of it into a new setup. Your backup is only read, never changed \u2014 and this needs no recovery code, because the copy is already on this machine.";
  EL("pick-restore-backup").addEventListener("click", function () {
    EL("restore-backup-lede").textContent = RESTORE_BACKUP_LEDE;
    EL("restore-backup-error").textContent = "";
    EL("restore-backup-name").value = "";
    EL("restore-backup").hidden = false;
  });
  EL("restore-backup-cancel").addEventListener("click", function () { EL("restore-backup").hidden = true; });
  EL("restore-backup-go").addEventListener("click", function () {
    var name = (EL("restore-backup-name").value || "").trim();
    if (!name) { EL("restore-backup-error").textContent = "give this setup a name first"; return; }
    invoke("pick_folder", { title: "Choose your Knowlu Backups folder" }).then(function (r) {
      if (!r || !r.path) { return; }
      return invoke("restore_vault", { backup: r.path, name: name, plan: backupRestorePlan() }).then(function (a) {
        if (!a.ok) { EL("restore-backup-error").textContent = a.error; return; }
        EL("restore-backup").hidden = true;
        return invoke("open_profile", { id: a.profile.id });
      });
    }).catch(function () { EL("restore-backup-error").textContent = "that folder could not be read"; });
  });
  // `restore_vault` takes a `WizardPlan` because it shares `finish_or_roll_back` with the wizard.
  // A restored vault already HAS its `config/`, so the only fields that reach anything are the
  // profile's own settings — autostart, and the offer marker the finish panel sets. The rest are the
  // struct's required fields with the values a restored vault does not use.
  function backupRestorePlan() {
    return { ics_url: null, personal_calendar: null, timezone: launch.tz || "America/Chicago",
             slots: ["12:00", "18:00"], zybooks: false, vhl: false, autostart: true, restore: false };
  }
```

**One thing this step does NOT do:** it does not turn the cloud copy on for the restored vault. A mirror is a local copy of a vault that may never have had an account; the student signs in and switches the copy on from settings, like any other install.

### Task 12: Git leaves the product

**Precondition P4** is a one-line go, asked when this task is reached. §4.4 has said this since the spec was signed: *"What the app stops doing: git commit/push/rebase (`history.rs`)"*, and §4.1: *"The vault is not a git repository and the app never assumes git exists."*

**Files:**
- Delete: `engine/src/history.rs`
- Modify: `engine/tests/no_console.rs`, `app/static/console.js`, `app/tests/commands.rs`
- Create: `app/tests/no_git.rs`
- **Hand-offs this task needs:** **H1b** (the `lib.rs` removal), **H6b** (`app/src/state.rs` and `app/src/commands.rs`), **H7b** (`app/src/scheduler.rs`'s two sync steps and the housekeeping loop), **H8c** (`app/src/main.rs`'s startup thread — the one the first review found missing). All four are applied by the controller as commits beside this task.

**Interfaces:**
- Produces: nothing new. This task removes.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test** — `app/tests/no_git.rs`:

```rust
//! Git is not part of this product (cloud design §4.1, §4.4).
//!
//! **There is no `git2` and there never was.** `history.rs` drove the `git` *executable*, through
//! `git_with("git", …)` → `Command::new(program)` (`engine/src/history.rs:29`, `:39`, `:42`), so a
//! dependency-graph check alone would have passed on every day the app was still spawning a process
//! every sixty seconds. And the app never wrote `Command::new("git")` either: it called into the
//! engine. So this scans for **the names the app actually used** — `history::`, `refresh_history`,
//! `run_sync`, `git_sha` — plus the spawn and the crates, because an assertion that was never true
//! of anything passes vacuously and says nothing.
use std::path::Path;

fn sources(dir: &Path) -> Vec<(String, String)> {
    let mut out = Vec::new();
    for entry in std::fs::read_dir(dir).unwrap_or_else(|e| panic!("{}: {e}", dir.display())).flatten() {
        let path = entry.path();
        if path.extension().map(|x| x == "rs") == Some(true) {
            let name = path.file_name().map(|n| n.to_string_lossy().to_string()).unwrap_or_default();
            out.push((name, std::fs::read_to_string(&path).unwrap_or_default()));
        }
    }
    assert!(out.len() >= 10, "the scan found suspiciously few source files in {}", dir.display());
    out
}

#[test]
fn no_git_process_is_spawned_for_a_vault() {
    // The app used to run `git rev-parse` and five `git status`-shaped calls on a sixty-second
    // housekeeping tick, against a folder that is not a repository and has not been one since §4.1.
    let src = Path::new(env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR")).join("src");
    let mut scanned = 0;
    for (name, text) in sources(&src) {
        let code = text.split("#[cfg(test)]").next().unwrap_or("");
        for forbidden in ["Command::new(\"git\")", "history::", "refresh_history", "refresh_head", "run_sync", "git_sha"] {
            assert!(!code.contains(forbidden), "app/src/{name} still names {forbidden}");
        }
        scanned += 1;
    }
    // A guard that cannot fail is worse than no guard: `engine/tests/no_console.rs` makes the same
    // argument about its own floor.
    assert!(scanned >= 14, "the scan found suspiciously few app source files: {scanned}");
}

#[test]
fn the_engines_git_transport_is_gone_too() {
    // `history.rs` spawned through `git_with("git", …)`, not `Command::new("git")` — so the name to
    // look for on the engine side is the helper, and it is the one that would come back if anyone
    // re-added the module. `runs::git_sha` stays and is named here so its survival is deliberate.
    let src = Path::new(env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR")).join("..").join("engine").join("src");
    for (name, text) in sources(&src) {
        let code = text.split("#[cfg(test)]").next().unwrap_or("");
        assert!(!code.contains("git_with"), "engine/src/{name} still drives the git executable");
        assert!(!code.contains("SyncLock"), "engine/src/{name} still holds history.rs's lock");
    }
    let runs = std::fs::read_to_string(src.join("runs.rs")).expect("runs.rs");
    assert!(runs.contains("pub fn git_sha"), "runs::git_sha stays: a run record's `sha` is a contract");
}

#[test]
fn the_history_module_is_gone_and_nothing_declares_it() {
    let engine = Path::new(env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR")).join("..").join("engine");
    assert!(!engine.join("src").join("history.rs").exists(), "engine/src/history.rs is still here");
    let lib = std::fs::read_to_string(engine.join("src").join("lib.rs")).expect("lib.rs");
    assert!(!lib.contains("pub mod history"), "lib.rs still declares it");
    // `childproc` outlives it: `runs::git_sha` and `runtime.rs` both spawn children, and a GUI
    // application's child must never flash a console window.
    assert!(lib.contains("pub mod childproc"), "childproc must stay");
}

#[test]
fn no_git_library_is_in_either_manifest() {
    for (name, manifest) in [
        ("app/Cargo.toml", include_str!("../Cargo.toml")),
        ("Cargo.toml", include_str!("../../Cargo.toml")),
        ("engine/Cargo.toml", include_str!("../../engine/Cargo.toml")),
    ] {
        for forbidden in ["git2", "libgit2", "gix", "gitoxide"] {
            assert!(!manifest.contains(forbidden), "`{forbidden}` must not be a dependency ({name}): git is not part of this product");
        }
    }
}

#[test]
fn the_page_says_nothing_about_a_repository() {
    let js = std::fs::read_to_string(Path::new(env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR")).join("static").join("console.js")).expect("console.js");
    for word in ["is_repo", "has_remote", "auto-sync", "auto_sync", "conflict — auto-sync stopped", "pending push", "engine_newer"] {
        assert!(!js.contains(word), "the page still renders {word}, which no longer exists");
    }
}
```

`app/static/console.js` — `renderSyncLine`'s **first six lines of body** (the comment block above the function, and the five `if`/`else if` branches from `if (!s.is_repo)` through `else { … "synced" … }`) are replaced by the block below; **everything from `if (b.last_error)` to the closing brace is unchanged except one deleted line**, and that line is `if (t.engine_newer) { … }`:

```javascript
  // C3: the topline's cloud-copy/backup/scheduler line. `t.sync` is `app/src/sync.rs`'s
  // `SyncStatus`, copied into the envelope by `commands.rs` and never computed here. Every source
  // object (t.sync, t.backup, t.scheduler) can be ABSENT — `|| {}` and truthiness checks must never
  // throw on a missing key.
  function renderSyncLine(state) {
    var t = state.topline, s = t.sync || {}, b = t.backup || {}, bits = [];
    // Four states, because after C3 there are four: no account, not turned on, something went
    // wrong, or up to date. A conflict is no longer a state this line reports — it is a card in the
    // deck, which is where a decision belongs (cloud design §5.5, VISION).
    if (!s.on) { bits.push('<span class="calm">this machine only</span>'); }
    // **Before `last_error`, and amber rather than crit.** The copy has moved to a key this machine
    // does not have: nothing is lost, nothing is failing, and there is exactly one thing to do.
    else if (s.blocked) { bits.push('<span class="amber">cloud copy needs this machine\u2019s key \u2014 open Settings</span>'); }
    else if (s.last_error) { bits.push('<span class="amber">' + h(s.last_error.split("\n")[0]) + "</span>"); }
    else if (s.cards > 0) { bits.push('<span class="amber">' + s.cards + " change" + (s.cards === 1 ? "" : "s") + " to review</span>"); }
    else { bits.push('<span class="calm">cloud copy up to date</span>'); }
    // ---- unchanged from here to the end of the function, minus the `engine_newer` line ----
    if (b.last_error) { bits.push('<span class="amber">backup: ' + h(b.last_error.split("\n")[0]) + "</span>"); }
    // …the backup, startup_missed, last_slot and scheduler bits exactly as they are on `main`…
  }
```

The one deleted line, so the diff is unambiguous:

```javascript
    if (t.engine_newer) { bits.push('<span class="amber">engine newer than console</span>'); }
```

```rust
#[test]
fn sync_on_a_vault_with_no_account_is_calm_and_backup_needs_a_folder() {
    let v = scratch("sync");
    let cs = ConsoleState::open(v.clone(), std::env::temp_dir().join(format!("qo-sync-data-{}", std::process::id())));
    let env = sync_inner(&cs, "today").unwrap();
    // A vault with no account is a named skip, not a failure — the same rule the slot's step has.
    assert_eq!(env["ok"], true, "{env}");
    assert_eq!(env["state"]["topline"]["sync"]["on"], false);
    let env = backup_now_inner(&cs, "today").unwrap();
    assert_eq!(env["ok"], false); assert!(env["error"].as_str().unwrap().contains("backup folder"));
    let bk = std::env::temp_dir().join(format!("qo-sync-bk-{}", std::process::id()));
    let mut patch = serde_json::Map::new(); patch.insert("backup_dir".into(), json!(bk.to_string_lossy()));
    assert_eq!(set_settings_inner(&cs, patch).unwrap()["ok"], true);
    let env = backup_now_inner(&cs, "today").unwrap();
    assert_eq!(env["ok"], true, "{env}");
    assert_eq!(env["state"]["topline"]["backup"]["behind_days"], 0);
    assert!(bk.join(&cs.settings.lock().unwrap().profile_id).join("vault/tasks").is_dir());
    let mut bad = serde_json::Map::new(); bad.insert("profile_id".into(), json!("me"));
    assert_eq!(set_settings_inner(&cs, bad).unwrap()["ok"], false, "only backup_dir and autostart are settable");
}
```

### Task 13: The two capability URLs leave the vault

C2's fidelity ledger named C3 as the stream that does this. Its stated reason — *"together with the journal sync that gives the device somewhere else to get the URL from"* — is corrected on the record in this plan's own ledger: journal sync gives the device nowhere else to get it, `/ingest-calendar` and `/ingest-ics` do, and have since C2. The reason to do it **now** is that `config/ingest.yaml` holds, in plain text a student can read, edit and paste into a support ticket, a URL that reads their whole timetable — and it diverges from the account's copy the moment they re-generate the secret address.

**Files:**
- Modify: `site/privacy.html`, `app/static/console.js` (the `PRIVACY` var), `engine/tests/site.rs` (its `PRIVACY` const, plus one new case), `app/tests/scaffold.rs`, `app/tests/scheduler.rs`, `engine/tests/sync_contract.rs`
