- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests** — `app/tests/scaffold.rs`:

```rust
#[test]
fn a_vault_with_an_account_carries_no_capability_url() {
    // C3, and §9's Alabama SPII line: a capability URL is a credential in all but name, and the one
    // place it belongs is the account, encrypted, where `PUT /account/sources` already puts it.
    let dest = temp("no-capability-url");
    let mut plan = plan_for(&dest);
    plan.account_id = "acc-1".into();   // `plan_for`'s own default; named here because it is the point
    plan.ics_url = Some("https://lms.example.invalid/feed/secret-capability.ics".into());
    plan.personal_calendar = Some("https://calendar.example.invalid/private-abc/basic.ics".into());
    let yaml = ingest_yaml(&plan).expect("ingest.yaml");
    assert!(!yaml.contains("secret-capability"), "{yaml}");
    assert!(!yaml.contains("private-abc"), "{yaml}");
    assert!(yaml.contains("ics_url: ''"), "the key stays, empty, so `ingest` still parses it: {yaml}");
    assert!(yaml.contains("- name: personal\n    ics_url: 'cloud:personal'"), "{yaml}");
}

#[test]
fn a_vault_with_no_account_still_carries_its_own_urls() {
    // A friend who has not signed in — and every pre-C1 install until the overlay adopts it — still
    // has nowhere else to keep the feed. Nothing about that path changes.
    let dest = temp("keeps-its-urls");
    let mut plan = plan_for(&dest);
    plan.account_id = String::new();
    plan.ics_url = Some("https://lms.example.invalid/feed/secret-capability.ics".into());
    plan.personal_calendar = Some("https://calendar.example.invalid/private-abc/basic.ics".into());
    let yaml = ingest_yaml(&plan).expect("ingest.yaml");
    assert!(yaml.contains("secret-capability"), "{yaml}");
    assert!(yaml.contains("private-abc"), "{yaml}");
}
```

(`temp` and `plan_for` are the file's own helpers, `app/tests/scaffold.rs:6` and `:15`; `plan_for` already sets `account_id: "acc-1"`, which is why the second test clears it explicitly.)

```rust
#[test]
fn a_cloud_vault_runs_ingest_even_with_no_url_in_the_vault() {
    // The other half of the removal: with the URL gone, `has_ics_url` has to answer yes for a vault
    // that has an account, or the step is skipped forever and the LMS feed never arrives.
    let v = scratch("cloud-ingest");
    std::fs::write(v.join("config").join("ingest.yaml"), "timezone: America/Chicago\nics_url: ''\n").expect("ingest.yaml");
    assert_eq!(ics_state(&v), IcsState::NoUrl);
    std::fs::write(
        v.join("config").join("cloud.yaml"),
        "api_base: 'https://x.example.invalid/functions/v1'\nanon_key: 'anon'\nsession_credential_target: 'knowlu/p/session'\naccount_id: 'acct-1'\n",
    ).expect("cloud.yaml");
    assert_eq!(ics_state(&v), IcsState::Cloud);
    assert!(has_ics_url(&v));
    let exe = Path::new("knowlu-engine.exe");
    let steps = slot_argv(&v, exe, &JudgePlan::Skip("judge (skipped: no entitlement)"));
    assert_eq!(steps[0].1[0], "sync", "sync runs first");
    assert!(steps.iter().any(|(_, a)| a[0] == "ingest"), "{steps:?}");
    let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&v);
}
```

(`scratch` is the file's own helper, `app/tests/scheduler.rs:11`; `ics_state`, `IcsState`, `has_ics_url`, `slot_argv` and `JudgePlan` are already in its `use` list at line 2, and `IcsState::Cloud` is new.)

and `engine/tests/sync_contract.rs`, for H3:

```rust
#[test]
fn a_cloud_vault_with_no_url_exits_zero_when_the_service_cannot_be_reached() {
    // Hand-off H3, and the test is red until it lands. `ingest` exits 1 on a blank `ics_url` and has
    // since the Python engine; with the URL living in the account that is now the NORMAL shape, and
    // a non-zero exit would paint the tray amber twice a day for a student on a train. A vault with
    // NEITHER an account NOR a URL keeps the old exit 1: that is a misconfigured vault.
    let dir = scratch("h3");
    std::fs::write(dir.join("config").join("ingest.yaml"), "timezone: America/Chicago\nics_url: ''\n").expect("ingest.yaml");
    let (code, lines) = knowlu_engine::ingest::run_lines(&dir, "cli", None, None);
    assert_eq!(code, 1, "no account and no url is still a misconfigured vault: {lines:?}");

    std::fs::write(
        dir.join("config").join("cloud.yaml"),
        "api_base: 'https://x.example.invalid/functions/v1'\nanon_key: 'anon'\nsession_credential_target: 'knowlu/c3-no-such-profile/session'\naccount_id: 'acct-1'\n",
    ).expect("cloud.yaml");
    let (code, lines) = knowlu_engine::ingest::run_lines(&dir, "cli", None, None);
    assert_eq!(code, 0, "{lines:?}");
    assert!(lines[0].contains("could not be reached"), "{lines:?}");
    let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir);
}
```

- **`site/privacy.html:13`, `site/index.html:16` (hand-off H12), `engine/tests/site.rs:15`'s `const PRIVACY` and `app/static/console.js:1371`'s `var PRIVACY` — four copies of one sentence** — all become this, character for character. It has to be true either way the switch is set:

  > Your vault stays on this machine. Knowlu's servers hold your account, the judgments they make for you, and what you correct — and, if you switch on a cloud copy, an encrypted copy of your notes that only your machine holds the key to. Nothing here is ever sold or shared.

- The *"No note bodies"* bullet becomes:

  > **Note bodies, only if you switch them on.** The text of your tasks and notes stays in the folder on your machine unless you turn on a cloud copy in Settings. That switch is off until you use it, it has a screen of its own explaining exactly this, and what it sends is encrypted on your machine with a key we never receive and cannot reconstruct — we store a blob we cannot open. Turning it off stops the copying; *Delete my data* removes what is already there.

- The **Export** `<li>` in the rights list (`site/privacy.html:96`, not a `<p>`) loses its last two sentences and gains:

  > Use **Export my data** in Settings to save it yourself, any time. Your notes are not in it because we cannot read ours: the plain files in your own folder are the export, and the cloud copy is a blob only your recovery code opens.

#[test]
fn the_policy_describes_the_cloud_copy_switch() {
    // The published policy pre-committed the shape of this feature before it existed ("a separate
    // switch, off by default, with its own screen explaining what and why — and no such switch
    // exists today"). C3 is the change it anticipated, so the two are read together and pinned to

        "a screen of its own",
        "a key we never receive",
        "Export my data",
    ] {

    }
    assert!(!policy.contains("no such switch exists today"), "the policy still says the switch does not exist");
}

- [ ] **Step 2: The frozen references.** `git status --porcelain --untracked-files=all engine/tests/fixtures/` → **empty**. The eight Python-written references and the three Rust-generated surface references are untouched; Task 3's one new committed vector lives in `cloud/`, not in `engine/tests/fixtures/`, and Task 9's replay copies a fixture rather than writing into one.

- [ ] **Step 3: The account-scoping scan — C3's own, in C3's own file.** Not "extend C2's or add C3's": C2's `judge_db_test.ts` is C2's file and widening it would put two streams in one test, so C3 adds the case below to `cloud/supabase/functions/_shared/sync_rows_test.ts`, which C3 owns. It scans `sync_db.ts` — the only file in this stream that queries anything — so that a `restSelect`, `restUpsert`, `restPatch` or `restDelete` naming `sync_records`, `sync_notes` or `sync_usage` **without** `account_id=eq.` fails the suite. The service role bypasses RLS; this scan is the backstop and it is cheap:

```ts
Deno.test("every sync query is scoped to one account", async () => {
  const src = await Deno.readTextFile(new URL("./sync_db.ts", import.meta.url));
  for (const table of ["sync_records", "sync_notes", "sync_usage"]) {
    for (const call of src.matchAll(new RegExp(`"${table}",\\s*\`([^\`]*)\``, "g"))) {
      assert(call[1].includes("account_id=eq."), `${table}: a query without account_id=eq.: ${call[1]}`);
    }
  }
  // **The two one-off `fetch` calls are scanned too**, because they do not go through C1's helpers
  // and so do not match the shape above. `claimGeneration`'s insert is the one URL in this file with
  // no `account_id=eq.` — it carries the id in the row it posts, which the `on_conflict` then keys
  // on — so it is named as the exception rather than quietly slipping past the scan.
  for (const call of src.matchAll(/rest\.fetch\(\s*`([^`]*)`/g)) {
    const url = call[1];
    if (url.includes("?on_conflict=account_id")) continue;
    assert(url.includes("account_id=eq."), `a raw fetch without account_id=eq.: ${url}`);
  }
  assertEquals([...src.matchAll(/rest\.fetch\(/g)].length, 2, "exactly two raw fetches, both in claimGeneration");
});
```

- [ ] **Step 6: Docs.** `HANDOFF.md` gains a `▶ C3 DONE <date>` block: the two endpoints live on staging, the cursor and ceiling numbers as deployed, the restore proven end to end on a scratch profile with its note and record counts, the line count `history.rs` took with it, and **what production still needs**:
  - the migration pushed to `knowlu-prod`, and `select public.sync_ceiling_bytes()` read back;
  - the two functions deployed there, and the `knowlu-sync-prune` cron job confirmed active;
  - **the privacy policy and the wizard's `PRIVACY` sentence republished** — P2, and the one item that must not lag the release;
  - **the lawyer's read** of the new note-bodies paragraph, with P5's list;
  - **`sync_usage` is a counter maintained by triggers**: if a bulk `delete from sync_records` is ever run by hand, run `update public.sync_usage set bytes = 0` and let the next push rebuild it, or the ceiling will refuse a push for bytes that are not there;
  - **the ceiling and the retention window are P3's recommended values**, not measured ones — revisit both after the first ten accounts have a term of history;
  - **`/sync-pull` returns a device's own rows** and the device filters them by content MAC (precondition P7, answer (a)). That is one wasted page per slot for a single-device account that has just pushed. A `device=neq.` predicate would remove it and is the first lever to reach for if the pull ever costs anything;
  - **the claim and the row writes are two requests, not one transaction.** `claimGeneration` is a compare-and-set, so nothing is ever emptied or stored by a device that lost the claim — but a re-key landing in the milliseconds between a winning claim and its `saveRecords` still orphans that one batch, and the device learns about it on its next push rather than never. PostgREST cannot put writes to two tables in one transaction; closing it completely means a server-side function taking the batch and the fingerprint together. Reach for that if two-device accounts ever become common;
  - **the key generation has never been exercised by two real machines.** `sync_generation`, the 409, the named skip, the Good-to-know item and `set_sync_key` are all covered by tests, but the sequence they exist for — laptop re-keys, desktop is refused, student reads the code off the laptop and types it into the desktop — has only ever been played by one machine. It is the first thing to run with a real pair;
  - **a second device has never been run** — every test here is one machine playing both parts. The first real two-device pair is a thing to watch, and the amend-card path is what to watch. `READ_LAG_SECONDS` is the guard against the one hazard only two devices produce (a `seq` taken before a commit that lands after a pull), and it has never been exercised by two real pushes either;
  - **the `keep` bit is one bit of metadata per record**, and it is the price of judge-once surviving a restore (P3). If the retention window is ever changed to "keep everything", the column and the bit can go;
  - **`apply` MACs the whole local journal** to build its `known` set, and `build_push` reads the ledger again in the same run. Fine for a first-year vault (50,000 records is about 100 ms of HMAC); worth measuring before it is fine for a fourth-year one;
  - **`sync::restore_into` computes its seed allowlist from `note_paths` at the moment it runs**, which is exactly right for a vault seconds old and would be exactly wrong if the route were ever reached for a vault that is not. It is called from one place; keep it that way.

  `CLAUDE.md` gains, in the engine-command list after `judge`:

  ```
  - `sync --vault <v> [--direction pull|push|both] [--via <via>] [--run-id <id>]` — the account's
    encrypted copy: new journal records and changed note text up, another device's writes down and
    applied through `write`. **Always exits 0**: no account, no sync key, no entitlement and no
    network are normal outcomes. The key is Credential Manager's `knowlu/<profile_id>/sync-key`,
    made on the device and never sent; the server stores blobs it cannot open. A machine whose key
    is not the account's is a named skip and a Good-to-know item, never a failed slot.
  ```

## Exit gate

1. **`POST /sync-push` and `GET /sync-pull` are deployed on staging**, both behind C1's `requireActiveEntitlement`, both answering their designed refusals (401 with no bearer, 402 with no subscription, 400 on a malformed row, 405 on the wrong method, 413 past the ceiling). **Every endpoint path is the hyphenated function name** — Supabase routes `/functions/v1/<function-name>` — and the loopback contract test asserts the same spelling the deploy uses.
2. **No row in either table could hold a note, and no row is a guessing oracle.** Every column that is not ciphertext is an opaque 16-hex device token, a 64-hex MAC, a 16-character base64 IV, one `keep` bit, a counter or a timestamp; `no column C3 creates could hold a note, a path or a title` passes, and `a_push_carries_ciphertext_and_no_path_no_title_and_no_token` proves it again at the wire. **All three opaque values are `HMAC-SHA256(K_index, …)` with `K_index = HKDF(sync key, "knowlu/index")`**, and `the_index_is_keyed_so_a_guessable_string_is_not_recoverable_from_its_row`, `the_three_index_values_cannot_collide_across_kinds` and `a_device_token_is_opaque_keyed_stable_and_not_the_hostname` prove that the bare digests are not what is stored.
3. **`knowlu-engine sync` always exits 0** on every one of: no `config/cloud.yaml`, no sync key, no session, no network, 402, 429, 5xx, timeout, an unparseable `cloud.yaml` — each as a distinct named line on stdout. A vault with no key reports **exactly** `sync (skipped: not turned on)`, because the three checks run `load` → `load_key` → `resolve` in that order, and the day still ranks.
4. **Rust and Deno open each other's envelopes** (`sync_envelope_test.ts` and `rust_opens_the_envelope_deno_sealed`), and a tampered ciphertext refuses rather than opening to something else.
5. **A fixture vault's notes and journal replay into an empty folder**: every note byte for byte at the same path, every journal record present as a record, and `a_restored_vault_ranks_the_same_day_as_the_one_it_came_from` green (it copies `state/{calendar,events,events-seen}.md` alongside `config/` and `profile/`, because those three are `rank` **inputs** a restore does not carry and `golden-today-full.md` proves both reach the rendered day). A restore refuses any note its caller did not name in the seed allowlist, **and the picker's route passes exactly the scaffold's seeds**, so it runs rather than refusing itself.
6. **A restore is `/sync-pull` from zero**, paged to the end. There is no third endpoint and no second code path that writes a note file except the two in `sync.rs` that say so in their own doc comments.
6a. **Nothing malformed reaches `state/journal/**`.** Every pulled record is checked before it is appended — `op` in `journal::OPS`, `via` in `journal::VIAS`, a parseable `ts`, an `actor`, a `device`, an `id` matching `ids::ID_RE` when present, and a `path` that passes the same three-way guard the pulled notes get — and a `move`'s destination is checked the same way before `write::move_note` sees it. `a_pulled_record_that_is_not_a_record_never_reaches_the_ledger` and `a_pulled_delete_or_move_may_not_escape_the_vault_either` pass.
7. **A field both devices moved is a `kind: amend` card**, filed through `write::propose_amendment` so the fifteen-a-day cap applies; a second pull of the same field set does not mint a second card; the note keeps this device's value until the card is answered; and a conflict on a field no card could ever apply takes reconcile's rule, keeps its supersede record and says so in one named line.
8. **A foreign record is journalled verbatim** — its own `ts`, `device`, `seq` and `actor` — and a record this device already has is not applied twice, whichever device pushed it.
9. **The switch is off by default, has its own screen, and the screen says the five sentences** `the_cloud_copy_has_a_switch_a_screen_and_the_five_sentences` pins. `sync_is_off_until_a_key_exists` drives `sync::is_on` against a real `ConsoleState` rather than asserting a credential it just deleted is absent. The recovery code can be shown again; the screen cannot be left until it is ticked as saved; **turning the switch off forgets the key, and the screen says what that means for the copy already in the account** — which the next `turn_on` discards and re-uploads, because `build_push` compares the cursor's `key_fingerprint` and sends `reset: true` (`a_new_key_re_uploads_everything_and_says_so_once`).
10. **The picker offers both restores** — *Restore from Knowlu's copy…* (the account's encrypted copy) and *Restore from a backup folder…* (the local mirror, which gives `onboarding::restore_vault` the caller it has never had) — the cloud one stages the code before the wizard starts, `create_vault_in` removes the folder it made if the key move or the pull **fails**, an **empty** copy keeps the vault and says so on the finish panel, and the wizard still has **nine** panels.
11. **Git is gone.** `engine/src/history.rs` does not exist, `lib.rs` does not declare it, no file under `app/src/` names `history::`, `refresh_history`, `refresh_head`, `run_sync` or `git_sha` — **including `app/src/main.rs`'s startup thread, which H8c deletes** — no file under `engine/src/` names `git_with` or `SyncLock`, `runs::git_sha` survives and is asserted to, no manifest names `git2`/`gix`, and the page renders none of `is_repo`, `has_remote`, `auto_sync`, `pending push` or `engine_newer`. `engine/src/backup.rs` is byte-identical to `main`.
12. **The two capability URLs are out of the vault** for any vault that has an account: `ics_url: ''` and `- name: personal` / `ics_url: 'cloud:personal'`, with `ingest` exiting **0** and naming the reason when the service cannot be reached, and a vault with no account unchanged. `site/privacy.html` describes the switch it once said did not exist.
11a. **No device of an account is ever left silently broken by another's re-key.** `public.sync_generation` holds the fingerprint of the generation the copy is sealed under; **every** `POST /sync-push` claims it by **compare-and-set** against the value it just read — an `ignore-duplicates` insert plus a re-read for an account's first push, a `key_fingerprint=eq.<the value read>` patch with `return=representation` for every later one — and both endpoints answer **409** — storing nothing, emptying nothing, reading nothing — to a device that does not hold it. The claim precedes `resetCopy` and both `save*` calls, so a device that lost a race destroys nothing and stores nothing; `on a first-push race exactly one device wins and the other stores nothing` and `a re-key landing under a normal push refuses the pusher instead of orphaning its rows` are the two shapes. The device turns that into `sync (skipped: this machine's key is not the account's)` with **exit 0, no `totals.errors` entry and no retry backoff**, plus one `info/` item (`STALE_KEY_INFO`) opened once and closed by the first sync that succeeds. *Settings → Cloud copy → use a code from another machine* calls `set_sync_key`, which **adopts** the account's generation — key written, cursor reset, so the next push is not a `reset` and does not destroy the copy it is joining. `a_stale_key_is_a_named_skip_and_a_notice_and_never_an_error`, `a_re_keyed_slot_pulls_nothing_and_is_not_a_failure`, `adopting_a_key_writes_it_and_resets_the_cursor_so_the_next_push_is_not_a_reset`, and the four 409/claim cases across the two handler suites all pass.
12a. **Two devices cannot strand a row between them.** `/sync-pull` reads only rows that settled at least `READ_LAG_SECONDS` ago, so a cursor cannot step past a push that has taken its `seq` and not yet committed; `the_clock_the_read_lag_uses_is_the_handlers_and_it_reaches_the_reader` pins the wiring and Task 5's smoke step observes both pulls.
13. `cargo test --workspace` is green at **0 warnings**; `oracle.rs` and `surface_oracle.rs` pass **unchanged**; `dependency_boundary.rs` names the two crypto crates and refuses a third; `deno check`, `deno lint` and `deno test` over `cloud/supabase/` are green **with C2's full flag set** (`--allow-read --allow-net=127.0.0.1 --allow-env=…`); no test opens a socket that is not `127.0.0.1`; every hand-off **H1a, H1b, H2, H3, H4, H5, H6a, H6b, H7a, H7b, H7c, H8a, H8b, H8c, H8d, H9, H10, H11, H12** is listed in the final report with exact code and the task it landed beside, and **no commit on `c3-sync` touches a file outside C3's ownership** (`git diff --name-only main...c3-sync` proves it).

## What is NOT in this plan

- **Server-originated writes down `/sync-pull`.** Spec §5.5's "Down" bullet lists judgment fields, Gmail-derived notes, event verdicts and rule proposals; **C2 already delivers all four by pull, inside the slot step that asks for them**, journalled through `write` under `agent:knowlu.<kind>` with judge-once intact. C3 does not re-route them, and the structural reason decides it: after this plan the down channel is encrypted with a key the server does not have, so a server-originated row could not be sealed and would have to travel in clear. Those writes go **up** through this module like any other record, so a second device receives them through the same encrypted channel — which is the property the sentence was written for. Fidelity ledger, row "§5.5 Down".
- **Supabase Storage.** Two Postgres tables instead, argued in the fidelity ledger. If a vault ever grows past what a `text` column wants to hold, Storage is the move and the envelope does not change.
- **A cloud-only picker, if Quinn rules P6 that way.** The plan builds **both** restore links — the account's encrypted copy and the local `Backups` mirror — on the recommendation in P6. If Quinn wants only the cloud one, Task 11 step 5 is deleted and this becomes: *the local-mirror restore is deferred; `onboarding::restore_vault` and `restore_vault_in` stay in the crate, registered in the wizard window's handler list, with no caller — which is the state `HANDOFF.md` §4 recorded on 2026-09-14 and the state C3 would then be leaving it in.*
- **Real-time sync.** One pull and one push per slot, plus the console's *sync now* button. A push the moment a note changes would mean a network call on every keystroke-committed edit and a second scheduler; twice a day plus a button is what §5.5's "the cloud copy is the durable one" asks for, and the local snapshot mirror is what covers the minutes in between.
- **A second device, actually run.** Every test here is one machine playing both parts, and that is honest but it is not the same thing. Recorded in Task 14's *what production still needs* as the first thing to watch.
- **Conflict resolution beyond `reconcile`.** No three-way text merge, no body reconciliation: a note's **body** is never merged — the file that exists locally keeps its body, and a body written on another device arrives only for a note this device has never seen. Frontmatter is what the system ranks on and frontmatter is what reconcile settles.
- **Deleting the cloud copy from the switch.** Turning sync off stops the copying; *Delete my data* removes what is there, and it already does (hand-off H5). A second delete path for the same rows would be a second thing to keep correct.
- **Selective sync.** All notes or none. A per-folder switch is a settings screen and a second set of rules for what a restore means, for a product where the whole vault is a few megabytes.
- **`runs::git_sha`.** It fills the `sha` field of a run record, that field is a contract with existing vaults, and on a vault that is not a repository — which is every vault the app creates — it already answers `None`. Its only app-side caller goes; the function stays. C4 or later may retire the field, with a migration note.
- **Removing the local llama.cpp runtime — C4.** `engine/src/runtime.rs` and `app/src/inference.rs` are left exactly as they are; nothing in this plan extends them.
- **The class-(c) opt-in and its export — C4.** The cloud copy is not telemetry and is not training data: it is the student's own encrypted backup, and nothing in it is readable by us or by a model. Named here so nobody reads the two features into each other.
- **The settings panel's Google connect/disconnect row, the "source went quiet" surface, the console's visual redesign, mobile, a second campus, production go-live.** Task 14 records production's list.

### Where this plan narrows the spec, and the recommendation

Four places, all fidelity-ledger rows.

**One. §5.5's "Down" bullet.** The largest narrowing, argued above and in the ledger. **Recommendation: as written.** The alternative is a plaintext channel beside an encrypted one, which cannot be explained on the switch's own screen.

**Two. §5.5's Storage.** Postgres instead, for one access path, RLS, a cursor that is an identity column and a purge list C1 already maintains. **Recommendation: as written**, and revisit only if a vault's corpus stops fitting comfortably in `text`.

**Three. §5.5's "client-side encryption … for note bodies" becomes everything, not only bodies.** A journal `set` record carries `old` and `new` for a field that may be `title`; encrypting bodies alone would leak note text by the other door. **Recommendation: as written.** The cost is real and named: the server cannot search, count or repair anything inside a row, and a lost recovery code is a lost cloud copy — which is why the local `Backups\` mirror stays and why the screen says so in its own words.

**Four. §11 R4's "account-derived key" is implemented as a device-generated key with a recovery code (P1(a)), not as a key derived from the password.** The spec's phrase does not say what happens at a password reset, and Supabase Auth offers one by email. **Recommendation: (a), and it is precondition P1.** (b) would mean either disabling password reset or accepting that a reset silently destroys the cloud copy; (c) would make the promise on the screen untrue. If Quinn picks (b) or (c), one function body changes — `sync::load_key` — and `app/src/sync.rs`'s `turn_on`; nothing else in this plan moves.

## Deferred minors

Every minor from `docs/reports/2026-09-14-c3-sync-plan-review.md` that fix round 1 did **not** fix, with the reason — plus four the plan carried from the start. Everything not listed here was fixed in the plan text; the resolution line for each is in that report's *Fix round 1* section.

| # | Minor | Why it is deferred |
|---|---|---|
| Review M9 | `note_paths` is non-recursive, so a note at `tasks/sub/x.md` is never pushed, while `apply` and `restore` both `create_dir_all(parent)` for a pulled path | **Half-fixed, half-deferred.** The write side is now explicit — `is_note_path` accepts any depth under a note folder, and the `create_dir_all` is what makes a pulled `courses/` path work on a vault that has no `courses/` yet. What is deferred is making the read side recursive: `ids::scan_notes`, `backup::BACKUP_FOLDERS` and every existing engine pass treat the six folders as flat, and a nested note is a shape this product has never produced. Making `note_paths` recursive alone would push a file nothing else in the engine can see. Recorded here rather than papered over. |
| Review M11 | `apply` MACs the whole local journal for its `known` set and `build_push` reads the ledger again in the same run | Fine for a first-year vault — 50,000 records is about 100 ms of HMAC, twice a day. The fix is a cached MAC set in the cursor, which is a second thing to keep true; **recorded in Task 14's production list** with the real numbers so it is measured before it is optimised. |
| Review M13 | The recovery code carries no checksum, so a mistyped code and a corrupt copy produce the same message | Crockford base32 has a standard check symbol and one extra character would separate "you typed it wrong" from "the data will not open". Deferred because the message it improves is already actionable (*the copy could not be opened with this key* — try the code again), and because the check symbol changes the code's length, which is on a screen Quinn reads under P2. **First thing to add if a real restore is ever reported as failing.** |
| Review M20 | The `no_console.rs` floor change to `>= 2` | Not a defect: the review confirmed the arithmetic. Listed so the next reader does not re-derive it. |
| M-a | `/sync-pull` returns rows this device pushed, and the device filters them by content MAC | **Now precondition P7**, with the recommendation to accept it: the MAC filter has to exist anyway for the re-install case, so a server-side `device=neq.` would be a second mechanism for a saving of one page per slot. Task 14 records it as the first lever if the pull ever costs anything. |
| M-b | `sync_usage` is a trigger-maintained counter, so a hand-run bulk delete leaves it wrong | The alternative is `sum(length(ciphertext))` on the hot path, which is O(rows) per push. The repair is one `update` and the next push rebuilds; it is written down in Task 14 rather than engineered around. (`resetCopy` goes through the same triggers, so the one delete this stream performs keeps the counter honest.) |
| M-c | A note's **body** is never merged | Frontmatter is what the system ranks on and what `reconcile` was written for; a three-way text merge of prose is a different product. A body written on another device still arrives whole for a note this device has never seen, which is the case that matters for a second device and for a restore. |
| M-d | The commit trailer hard-codes `Co-Authored-By: Claude Fable 5.1` | **Ruled not a defect** (R-C2-6): the trailers name the model and session that *execute* the plan, and the literal is the writing session's. Global Constraints say so in one clause, which is the whole fix the ruling asks for. |
